## 2026-07-16 — issue #122 — map xs:dateTimeStamp by aliasing dateTime's value model

- Shipped: `backend.Mapping` gains a `dateTimeStamp` case returning dateTime's
  mapping VERBATIM (`Parse: parseDateTime, Canonical: canonicalDateTime`).
  dateTimeStamp (§3.4.28) is a restriction of dateTime whose lexical/canonical
  mapping IS dateTime's, restricted to the tz-present value subset (§3.4.28.1) —
  no independent representation exists. The tz-required constraint is enforced
  generically by the explicitTimezone facet pipeline (#108,
  cvc-explicitTimezone-valid §4.3.14), reading dateTimeVal's value.TimezoneAware;
  it is NOT re-checked in Parse (would double-encode, PRINCIPLES 4). The fixed
  facet defaults (whiteSpace=collapse, explicitTimezone=required) already lived
  in the generated typespec (builtin/gen_typespec.go:760), so no generator work.
- Tests (builtin/strict/datetimestamp_test.go): Mapping(dateTimeStamp) ok with
  non-nil Parse/Canonical and a tz-bearing round-trip; and the REAL seeded
  builtin dateTimeStamp type through value.ValidateLexical — tz-bearing accepted,
  tz-absent rejected as cvc-explicitTimezone-valid (proves the generated fixed
  facet reaches enforcement for the actual seeded type, not just derive()-built).
- Conformance: widened the lexical-cohort regex (conformance/datatypes.go) to
  list dateTimeStamp for forward parity. FINDING: the checkout has ZERO
  msData/datatypes/dateTimeStampNNN.xml cases (only ibmMeta/dateTimeStamp.testSet
  metadata), so the alternative is inert today — no lane movement. Documented a
  fail-open GAP at the regex + package doc: unlike every other cohort member,
  dateTimeStamp's Parse-only path (execLexicalCase → parseDateTime) does NOT
  enforce the required timezone (that lives only in the facet cohort's
  value.ValidateLexical), so a future tz-absent dateTimeStamp case would be
  false-ACCEPTED and must instead move to the facet cohort. Flagged for arbiter.
- Spec: §3.4.28, §3.4.28.1, cvc-explicitTimezone-valid (§4.3.14).
- Ratchet: unchanged (zero dateTimeStamp cases in the suite; regex widening is inert).
- Gate: build/test/vet/golangci-lint/conformance all green.
- Verdicts (chronicler addendum, recorded at land time — mason wrote the entry
  above BEFORE the arbiter ruled): Arbiter round 1 on `wip/issue-122` **REJECT**
  (comment 4997317749), two doc-only findings, no logic defect: (1) the fail-open
  gap in conformance/datatypes.go lacked a greppable `GAP(datatypes)` token so it
  could not be inventoried alongside the other fail-open sites; (2) a
  datetimestamp_test.go docstring mis-attributed which enforcement path (facet
  cohort vs Parse-only) rejects the tz-absent case. Mason repair round (commit
  `26a5dfe`) added the `// GAP(datatypes)` marker and corrected the docstring;
  no code behaviour changed. Arbiter round 2 **ACCEPT** (comment 4997332999).
  Full gate (build/test/vet/golangci-lint/conformance) green in BOTH rounds;
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` confirmed ratchet unchanged in both — zero
  msData/datatypes/dateTimeStampNNN.xml cases in the current W3C checkout, so the
  lexical-cohort regex widening is inert today (no lane moved, no expectations
  file touched).
- Next (deferred, for cartographer post-land harvest): (1) file a tracking issue
  for the documented fail-open `GAP(datatypes)` in conformance/datatypes.go —
  dateTimeStamp's Parse-only lexical-cohort path (execLexicalCase → parseDateTime)
  does NOT enforce explicitTimezone=required, so a future tz-absent case would be
  false-ACCEPTED and must move to the facet cohort instead; harmless while zero
  cases exist, but worth tracking analogous to how #130/#133/#134 tracked the
  precisionDecimal-family gaps. (2) NARROW-PRECEDENT caution (warden's explicit
  note): do NOT reflexively alias the next derived types
  (yearMonthDuration §3.4.26 / dayTimeDuration §3.4.27) onto duration the way
  dateTimeStamp aliased dateTime — their canonical forms genuinely differ from
  duration's (dateTimeStamp's canonical mapping IS dateTime's, restricted to a

## 2026-07-17 — #124 widen datatypes Facets cohort to anyURI/hexBinary/base64Binary (mason)

- Widened the `datatypes` lane's Facets cohort (`conformance/datatypes.go`) to the
  three strict-mapped primitives `anyURI`, `hexBinary`, `base64Binary` by adding
  them to `facetsBaseTypes` (the `|`-alternation feeding both the directory and the
  filename-prefix of `facetsCase`). Zero backend change: all three are primitives
  strict already maps (#82 anyURI, #83 hexBinary/base64Binary), so `strictGoverns`
  resolves at the first step and validation runs through the existing generic
  pipeline (`value.ValidateLexical`).
- Spec (grounding on the issue, GROUNDING comment 4997679586): all three admit
  length/minLength/maxLength/pattern/enumeration (cos-applicable-facets §4.1.5,
  identical to string; unordered so NO bound facets), all already in `facetKinds`.
  The length UNIT split (§4.3.1.3 clauses 1.1/1.2) — characters for anyURI, decoded
  OCTETS for the binary types — is already realized by `value.Lengthed` Len()
  dispatch: `anyURIVal.Len()` counts runes, `hexBinaryVal`/`base64BinaryVal.Len()`
  count decoded `[]byte`. Verified by reading `builtin/strict/anyuri.go` and
  `builtin/strict/binary.go`; no fix needed there. Rules exercised unchanged:
  cvc-datatype-valid §4.1.4, cvc-pattern-valid §4.3.4.4, cvc-enumeration-valid
  §4.3.5.4, cvc-length/minLength/maxLength-valid §4.3.1.3–4.3.3.3.
- One shape correction beyond the raw widening: the anyURI Facets dir carries
  out-of-cohort `anyURI_a*`/`anyURI_b*` cases that also match the filename regex.
  The `a*` cases already declined (value in an xsi:schemaLocation-qualified <bar>,
  not noNamespaceSchemaLocation). The `b*` cases carry zero <foo> (b001, values in
  repeated <bar>) or several <foo> (b006, a list-style multi-value instance), which
  the previous single `Foo fooElem` reader mis-read as an empty / last-wins value —
  coincidentally PASSING b001/b005/b006 for the wrong reason. Changed
  `facetsInstance.Foo` to `Foos []fooElem` and made `readFacetsCase` require
  EXACTLY ONE <foo>, so every out-of-cohort anyURI shape is honestly declined
  (Fail, a recorded gap) rather than baking a wrong-reason pass into the ratchet.
  Confirmed via a throwaway DIAG-gated diagnostic (since deleted) that the exactly-
  one guard declines all 8 anyURI a*/b* instances and leaves every canonical
  <test><foo> case agreeing with the suite; non-ratchet `TestConformance` shows no
  regression from the guard.
- Observed locally (non-authoritative, no `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` — arbiter-only): of the
  49 claimed Facets cases across the three dirs, 40 canonical-shape cases agree with
  the suite in both polarities (22 valid-expected, 18 invalid-expected), 8 anyURI
  out-of-cohort shapes decline (honest gaps), and 1 anyURI case (a schema-only
  entry) is unclaimed. Expected ratchet movement: datatypes lane up by the ~40
  agreeing New cases recorded `pass`, the ~8 anyURI gaps recorded `fail`; nothing
  flips downward; no other lane touched. Arbiter to confirm exact numbers.
- Tests (`conformance/datatypes_test.go`): added `TestDatatypesFacetsBinaryAndURI`
  (both polarities over real anyURI/hexBinary/base64Binary cases, with
  hexBinary_maxLength001 "abcdef"=3 octets and base64Binary_length002 "MS0yLTM="=5
  octets chosen so a regression to lexical-character counting would flip them, plus
  a wrong-expectation-must-Fail that is octet-unit-load-bearing) and
  `TestDatatypesFacetsShapeGuard` (readFacetsCase accepts the single-<foo> shape,
  declines b001 zero-<foo> and b006 multi-<foo>). Extended
  `TestDatatypesSelectorClaimsOnlyCohort` with one claim per new dir.
- Doc comments updated: facet-cohort header (#57 line adds #124), the claimed-base
  narrative, `facetsBaseTypes` doc (new types + length-unit paragraph), the
  `newDatatypesExec` cohort enumeration, `facetsInstance`'s shape note, and the

- Gate green: build, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `golangci-lint run`

- Verdicts (chronicler addendum, recorded at land time — mason wrote the entry

## 2026-07-17 — issue #130 — QName/NOTATION length-facet clause-1.3 exemption

- Shipped (mason): `value.lengthFacet.CheckValue` now short-circuits to accept
  ANY value when the type's `{primitive type definition}` is QName or NOTATION,
  per clause 1.3 of cvc-length-valid (§4.3.1.3) / cvc-minLength-valid (§4.3.2.3)
  / cvc-maxLength-valid (§4.3.3.3) — an unconditional "any {value} is
  facet-valid" no-op, independent of the bound and of `Lengthed.Len()`. Added
  unexported `lengthExemptPrimitive(st)` keying off the atomic `{variety}`'s
  `Primitive.Name()` (not the value's Go type, not a blanket is-atomic test — the
  list case, clause 2, has no such exemption), the two builtin-name vars
  `qnameName`/`notationName`, and an `exempt bool` field on `lengthFacet` set at
  construction; `newLengthFacet` now takes `st *xsd.SimpleType` and the one
  `compile()` call site threads it through (mirrors newBoundFacet/newEnumFacet).
- Prior bug was a latent WRONG-REJECT (fail-closed polarity): a QName/NOTATION
  value under a length facet would have gated on `qnameVal.Len()`'s documented
  non-gating local-part rune count. Currently unreachable by the conformance
  suite (no selector routes a length facet at a QName/NOTATION value yet), so
  pure prerequisite cleanup — ratchet UNCHANGED, expectations untouched.
- Tests: `TestLengthFacetsQNameNotationExempt` (builtin/strict, package strict)
  drives real QName and NOTATION values end-to-end through ValidateLexical under
  length=3/minLength=6/maxLength=2 bounds a 5-rune local part violates — all
  accepted; plus narrow `value.TestLengthExemptPrimitive` covering primitive,
  QName restriction, string (not exempt), and nil-variety anySimpleType (no
  panic). Mutation-checked: removing the short-circuit fails both suites.
- Surface: none (predicate, vars, and struct field all unexported). Full gate
  (build/test/vet/golangci-lint 0 issues) + non-ratchet conformance green.
- Friction: (1) a mid-task `git checkout value/facets.go` (run to undo a mutation
  probe) silently reverted the unstaged implementation; caught by re-reading and
  re-applied. Lesson: never `git checkout` a file holding unstaged work — restore
  the mutation with a targeted Edit instead. (2) mason authored the four bullets
  above INTO `docs/LOG/2026-07.md` in the implementation commit (`0911a67`) —
  writing the log is the chronicler's Land-step duty, not mason's. The arbiter
  flagged it as a non-blocking process finding for the chronicler to reconcile
  (see below); this entry keeps mason's accurate content and adds the missing
  chronicler sections rather than rewriting. Recorded factually; no session was
  blocked, but the role boundary was crossed.
- Ratchet: unchanged — `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` left zero diff under
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/`, working tree clean, no stray root files
  (arbiter-verified; copied from verdict, not recomputed). No selector routes a
  length facet at a QName/NOTATION value yet, so clause 1.3 is unreachable by the
  suite today — pure prerequisite cleanup, no lane moved.
- Verdicts (chronicler addendum, recorded at Land — mason wrote the four bullets
  above BEFORE the arbiter ruled): Arbiter round 1 on `wip/issue-130` **ACCEPT**
  (comment 4999660811), single round, no repair needed. Independently verified
  against `docs/specs/md/xmlschema11-2.md`: §4.3.1.3/§4.3.2.3/§4.3.3.3 clause 1.3
  read directly — under clause 1 (`{variety}` atomic) the 1.1/1.2/1.3 sub-clauses
  are a mutually exclusive case split on `{primitive type definition}`, and 1.3
  ("if QName or NOTATION, then any {value} is facet-valid") is an unconditional
  no-op; clause 2 (list) carries no exemption, so the fix's keying off the atomic
  variety's resolved `Primitive.Name()` correctly covers a QName restriction and
  correctly excludes list-of-QName. Confirmed the short-circuit sits BEFORE the
  `Lengthed` assertion/panic, no schema-construction-time rejection was added, and
  general-case measurement (chars/octets/list items) is untouched. Full gate
  independently re-run green: `go build ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`,
  `golangci-lint run` (0 issues), non-ratchet `TestConformance`, and
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` (no expectations file changed, tree clean). STYLE re-checked
  clean (S1/S2/S4; T5 — nothing new exported, `lengthExemptPrimitive`,
  `qnameName`, `notationName`, the `exempt` field, and the `newLengthFacet`
  signature change all internal; value→xsd dependency direction only). Arbiter
  mutation-checked the tests itself: removing `if lf.exempt { return nil }` fails
  all six end-to-end `TestLengthFacetsQNameNotationExempt` cases, and breaking the
  predicate fails `TestLengthExemptPrimitive` — noting that mason's "removing the
  short-circuit fails both suites" is imprecise (the `value` unit test guards the
  predicate, not the short-circuit) but harmless, each test legitimately fails on
  its own target's mutation. Two process findings, neither a code defect: (a)
  [non-blocking] the mason-authored LOG entry, flagged for chronicler reconcile at
  Land — resolved by this addendum; (b) [resolved] the mid-task `git checkout`
  incident left NO trace in the committed diff — the full implementation is present

- Next: none carried forward — prerequisite cleanup, clean single-round accept, no

  `SOMITEM_DATATYPE_*` attribute directly as a facet-less builtin primitive.
  The lane's existing `readLexicalCase` only decoded the comp_foo/simpleTest
  shape, so these cases never reached the lexical check. Added a fallback path in
  `conformance/datatypes.go` (`execItemCase`/`readItemCase`/`decodeItemAttrTypes`)
  that parses the sibling `datatypes.xsd` (not hand-typed) into an attr→primitive

  (con-gMonthDay-dayValue / con-gMonthDay-day §3.3.12.1-2),
  `+2001-07-11T12:23:45` (dateTimeLexicalRep §3.3.7.2), `+2001-07-11`
  (dateLexicalRep §3.3.9.2), all under cvc-datatype-valid §4.1.4 — and that

## 2026-07-17 — issue #131 — QName/NOTATION lexical cohort claimed (mason, pre-arbiter)

- Shipped (branch `wip/issue-131`, not yet arbiter-judged at time of writing):
  a namespace-context adapter in the `conformance` package so the datatypes
  lexical cohort can decide the context-dependent QName/NOTATION primitive family.
  Surface: none (test-only, package `conformance`). No new `value` package surface
  needed — `conformance` implements `value.Context` itself with an internal
  `nsContext` type, exactly as the issue anticipated.
- What was built: `execLexicalCase` now routes QName/NOTATION (isContextDependent)
  to `execContextualCase`, which reads each comp_foo/simpleTest literal WITH the
  in-scope XML namespace bindings via `readQNameContexts` — a streaming
  `xml.Decoder` pass that tracks `xmlns:*`/`xmlns` declarations down the ancestor
  chain and snapshots them per leaf (a raw literal's prefix is character content,
  not an XML name the decoder resolves, so the accumulation is done by hand). The
  snapshot is wrapped in `nsContext`, which also binds the reserved prefixes
  `xml`/`xmlns` by definition (Namespaces in XML §3) and resolves the empty prefix
  to the default namespace (or no namespace when undeclared). `parseOK` gained a
  `ctx` param; the context-free primitives keep passing `nil`.
- Spec: cvc-datatype-valid §4.1.4 (every rejection), QName lexical mapping §3.3.18,
  NOTATION §3.3.19 (mapping "as given for QName"). PRINCIPLES 19 (unbound prefix ⇒
  real rejection, never a fabricated namespace).
- Cohort: QName is the whole exercised cohort today — 11 cases
  (`QName001..011.xml`), all decided correctly in both polarities (4 valid:
  002/003/006/009; 7 invalid: 001/004/005/007/008/010/011). Key subtlety: QName009
  `xmlns:xsi` is suite-VALID because the reserved `xmlns` prefix is bound by
  definition — the adapter binds it, so the case passes for the right reason, not
  by luck. NOTATION has ZERO plain lexical cases in the checkout (all its cases are
  facet-cohort under `Facets/NOTATION`), so it is listed in the regex for
  spec/mechanism parity and unexercised today.
- Expected ratchet: datatypes lane +11 pass (11 New QName cases), nothing flips
  downward. The QName cases also remain `fail` in the instance lane (stubFail,
  unchanged) — the two lanes both list them, matching the established boolean/
  decimal pattern, so no vanish. Arbiter to confirm via `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`.
- Gate: `go build ./... && go test ./... && go vet ./...` green; `golangci-lint run`
  0 issues; read-only `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance` reports no
  regressions. New tests: `TestDatatypesLexicalQNameCohort` (11 cases + wrong-
  expectation Fail + adapter read-back) and `TestNSContextLookup` (prefix
  resolution unit test); `TestDatatypesSelectorClaimsOnlyCohort` extended.
- Facets over QName/NOTATION remain deferred (need the length clause-1.3 exemption
  and tuple enumeration) — recorded in the "Still deferred" doc comment.

## 2026-07-17 — issue #131 — repair: exclude xmlns from resolvable QName prefixes (mason)
- Arbiter rejection (one finding): the pre-arbiter commit `4c31e50` had QName009
  (`xmlns:xsi`) inverted — claimed suite-VALID because the adapter bound `xmlns`.
  Ground truth: `QName009_2092` in `DataTypes_w3c.xml` carries `validity="invalid"`
  (WG 2010-02-05 telcon, bugzilla 4053: no binding exists for `xmlns` as a prefix).
  The `.v` in the file name is an MS artifact; `resolveExpected` reads the catalog's
  real invalid value.
- Fix: dropped the `case "xmlns"` branch and the `xmlnsPrefixNS` constant from
  `nsContext.LookupNamespace`; only `xml` remains an implicitly-bound reserved
  prefix (Namespaces in XML §3). `xmlns` now returns `ok=false`, so strict's Parse
  rejects `xmlns:xsi` via cvc-datatype-valid §4.1.4 — matching the suite.
- Tests flipped to the corrected expectation: `TestDatatypesLexicalQNameCohort`
  QName009 → invalid; `TestNSContextLookup` `xmlns` → `("", false)`. Three doc
  comments in `datatypes.go` reworded to state the correct rule + cite bugzilla 4053.
- Gate: build/test/vet green; `golangci-lint run` 0 issues; read-only
  `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance` no regressions. All 11 QName cases
  now decide correctly in both polarities (valid: 002/003/006; invalid: 001/004/005/
  007/008/009/010/011). Corrects the prior entry's mistaken "QName009 suite-VALID"
  claim. Ratchet unchanged from this commit alone; arbiter to confirm datatypes +11.
- Post-land pass (cartographer, PR #150 squash `ca25c6c`): unblocked #125 (the
  QName/NOTATION Facets cohort) `blocked` → `ready` — its `## Depends on` named
  #131 and #114, both now CLOSED (#131 itself closed on #130, also CLOSED). General
  scan confirmed no other `blocked` issue depends on #131 (#135/#133 mention it only
  in prose). Harvest: the "Still deferred" doc comment's Facets-over-QName/NOTATION
  follow-up is exactly #125 (now ready); NOTATION zero-lexical-cases and

## 2026-07-18 — issue #125 — datatypes Facets cohort admits QName (11 fail→pass)

- Shipped: `conformance/datatypes.go` now admits `xsd:QName` into the Facets
  cohort — added `QName` (not `NOTATION`) to `facetsBaseTypes` and threaded a
  real `value.Context` (built from the instance root's xmlns bindings via the
  existing `nsContext`/`childBindings` machinery from #131) into
  `execFacetsCase`'s `ValidateLexical` call, replacing the hardcoded `nil`
  (required even for the unprefixed QName literals — `resolveQNameLexical`
  rejects a nil context unconditionally). Commits `7ecd4e1` (mason) + `e6d667c`
  (ratchet-recorded expectations).
- Grounding (oracle, GROUNDING comment): corrected this issue's own "Spec" text
  — length/minLength/maxLength ARE applicable to QName/NOTATION per §4.1.5
  `cos-applicable-facets`, but vacuously pass per §4.3.1.3/§4.3.2.3/§4.3.3.3
  clause 1.3 (deprecated-but-legal), already correctly realized by #130's
  `lengthFacet` exemption and untouched here. So this was a harness-widening
  change, not a library correctness fix.
- Ratchet (arbiter ACCEPT, HEAD `7ecd4e1`, figures copied not recomputed):
  **datatypes lane 955→966 pass, 20→24 fail** (975→990 total). The +15 lines
  the ratchet wrote to `datatypes.txt` are exactly **11 pass** (length×3,
  maxLength×3, minLength×4, pattern×1) plus **4 honest fail-gaps**
  (enumeration001-004). Nothing regressed or vanished; no other lane's file
  touched. Arbiter independently confirmed the passes are genuine (reverted the
  length exemption, watched `foofo` flip to reject) and the enumeration declines
  are real gaps, not mis-decisions.
- Two deliberate, documented scope carve-outs (declined, not silently skipped):
  (a) QName `enumeration` cases are explicitly declined in `buildOwnFacets` —
  `value.newEnumFacet` hardcodes a nil context when parsing enum member
  lexicals, which cannot resolve a schema-declared prefix like `foo:fo`; fixing
  it needs new `xsd.Facet`/`EffectiveFacet` surface + a warden pre-flight,
  beyond this "Surface: none" issue. (b) `NOTATION` was not added to
  `facetsBaseTypes` at all — its Facets fixtures use an incompatible two-step
  restriction through a locally-named simpleType with `<xsd:notation>`
  declarations and the tested value carried in an attribute, which the cohort
  executor does not model.
- Gate: build/test/vet/lint green; read-only conformance no regressions;
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` exit 0. No new exported surface.
- Next: file the two follow-ups the arbiter flagged for the post-land harvest —
  (a) QName/NOTATION enumeration-facet schema-declaring-context threading (a
  `value`/`xsd` design item mirroring #131's lexical-cohort fix, one level up
  for the facet cohort, warden-gated); (b) the NOTATION Facets-cohort's
  incompatible instance/schema shape (two-step restriction + notation
  declarations + attribute-carried value, a larger harness-widening task).

## 2026-07-18 — issue #133 — enforce precisionDecimal maxScale/minScale at instance validation

- Shipped (impl ccf607e + warden doc-nit fix f49092f): closes GAP(facet)
  maxScale/minScale not enforced. Implemented the two instance-time rules
  cvc-maxScale-valid (xsd-precisionDecimal.md §4.2.3) and cvc-minScale-valid
  (§4.3.3) — the schema-construction SCCs (valid-restriction narrowing,
  minScale≤maxScale, {fixed} inheritance) stay out of scope.
- xsd/simpletype.go: extended the FacetKind enum from 14 to 16 members with
  FacetMaxScale/FacetMinScale (iota continues after FacetExplicitTimezone, the
  existing 14 unchanged); added String() cases ("maxScale"/"minScale"); rewrote
  the FacetKind package doc to state the real invariant (closed set the generic
  Facet/EffectiveFacets/pipeline machinery treats uniformly, precisionDecimal
  extension facets a documented cross-spec inclusion) rather than the old "NOT
  members" framing; HasFixed unchanged (both new kinds carry required {fixed} via
  the default branch, now noted in its doc).
- value/facets.go: new scaleFacet (one struct, both kinds — mirrors digitsFacet)
  implementing ValueFacet; new facetInt helper (xs:integer, may be negative,
  unlike facetCount's nonNegativeInteger); compile() routes both kinds. Vacuous
  pass on absent ·scale· (specials NaN/±INF, value.Scaled ok=false) is clause 2
  of both rules; non-Scaled candidate panics (boundFacet convention,
  cos-applicable-facets §4.1.5).
- Doc-sync: builtin/seed.go facetKind + ownFacets comments (they no longer claim
  the enum "excludes by design"), builtin/doc.go and builtin/strict/doc.go
  "Current coverage" prose (maxScale/minScale now enforced), and retired the
  GAP(facet) block in builtin/strict/precisiondecimal.go.
- Tests: value/facets_test.go TestScaleFacetCheckValue covers both polarities of
  both facets, negative bounds (proving the integer domain), and the special
  vacuous-pass; plus non-Scaled panic and bad-{value} rejection tests.
- Spec: cvc-maxScale-valid, cvc-minScale-valid.
- Verdicts: Oracle GROUNDING (§4.2.3/§4.3.3) → Warden design pre-flight APPROVE
  (extending xsd.FacetKind 14→16 members sanctioned) → mason implementation →
  Warden API-diff review ACCEPT (one doc nit, fixed in f49092f) → Arbiter
  ACCEPT. Arbiter re-verified spec fidelity against xsd-precisionDecimal.md
  §4.2.3/§4.3.3 and mutation-tested the `violates` logic. This session ran
  mason inline rather than as a separate step.

  regression and no expectation file moved. No conformance consumer exercises
  these facets yet — the datatypes cohort-widening is a separate follow-up;

## 2026-07-18 — #134 — datatypes lane: removed redundant fallbackPrimitives shim

- Removed the `fallbackPrimitives` type + its `Mapping` method and the
  `value.Override(fallbackPrimitives{}, strict.New())` composition in
  `conformance/datatypes.go`; `builtin.Seed` is now fed `strict.New()` directly.
  Post-#115 strict maps all 20 builtin primitives (19 spec-mandatory of
  Datatypes §3.3 + precisionDecimal), so the no-op identity fallback was fully
  redundant. It was also the wrong polarity as a floor: an accept-everything
  identity `Parse` would MASK a silently-dropped strict mapping by accepting
  every lexical, whereas `builtin.Seed`'s own precondition scan fails loud with
  a typed `*builtin.MissingPrimitivesError`. Net: a latent fail-open mask
  converted to the fail-loud behavior Seed already provides.
- Collapsed the now-identical `backend`/`strictBackend` pair to a single strict
  backend; `execFacetsCase` drops its second backend parameter. Refreshed the
  `newDatatypesExec`, `execFacetsCase`, and `strictGoverns` doc comments to drop
  stale fallback/Override prose. Updated both `value.Override(...)` sites in
  `datatypes_test.go` (TestDatatypesBackendSeeds still guards the precondition,

## 2026-07-18 — #135 — conformance: claim the Saxon precisionDecimal instance cohort

- Widened the `datatypes` lane to claim and decide the Saxon precisionDecimal
  instance cases under `saxonData/PDecimal/pdecimalNNN.{vK,nK}.xml`. These were
  UNDISCOVERED, not merely unclaimed: the W3C suite moved the precisionDecimal
  sets out of `suite.xml` (withdrawn from XSD 1.1, retained as a WG Note) into
  `extra-suite.xml`, which discovery never read. So `runner.go` now discovers
  `suite.xml` AND its sibling `extra-suite.xml`, de-duplicating the one shared
  set (`common/introspection.testSet`, listed in both) by resolved path
  (`casesFromIndex`/`seenSets`) so it is not a spurious duplicate-ID error.
- New `execPDecimalCase`/`readPDecimalCase` decode the cohort's distinct shape (a
  `<doc>` root with repeated `<e value="…"/>` children, all validated against ONE
  type; schema out-of-band, derived from the case-prefix filename → sibling
  `pdecimalNNN.xsd`). The tested leaf is synthesized (precisionDecimal primitive +
  schema facets) and run through the existing `value.ValidateLexical` pipeline —
  no new value code. Added `FacetMaxScale`/`FacetMinScale` to the lane's
  `facetKinds` (precisionDecimal-only, harmless to the msData cohort).
- Spec-correct facet behavior falls out of the existing pipeline: totalDigits
  vacuously passes zero+specials (value.TotalDigits→1, xsd-precisionDecimal.md
  §4.1, distinct from decimal); NaN fails EVERY bound facet over the partial order
  (boundFacet incomparable⇒reject, §3.1/§4.3.7–4.3.10); maxScale/minScale skip the
  specials' absent ·scale· (#133); enumeration is value-space equal-or-identical on
  ·numericalValue· (10 == 1.0E1; NaN matches a NaN member by identity, §4.3.5.4).
- Scope: only the directly-mapped and single-step restriction cases (pdecimal001–
  008,010) are decided. The two-step chain (016), list (019) and union (020) shapes
  are DECLINED by `decodePDecimalSchema` (honest gaps, Fail). One suite quirk kept
  spec-correct rather than bent: pdecimal006.n2 ("NaN" vs a NaN-bearing enumeration)
  is spec-VALID (identity match) but suite-declared invalid, so it records a Fail
  against the suite — flagged in `execPDecimalCase`, per the #135 GROUNDING. The
  arbiter independently confirmed this via `GOXSD_CASE` (goxsd8 computes VALID) and
  UPHELD it as spec-correct — cvc-enumeration-valid §4.3.5.4 "equal or identical"
  plus xsd-precisionDecimal.md §3.1's single NaN value identical to itself — an
  honest goxsd8-vs-suite disagreement, not a false-accept and not a mason error.
- Deferred: the IBM `ibmData/D3_3_4` precisionDecimal shape (several named types per
  schema) is NOT claimed — a distinct, larger executor; its instance cases route to
  the inert `instance` lane as recorded gaps meanwhile. Follow-up issue candidate.
- Spec: cvc-datatype-valid §4.1.4, cvc-pattern-valid §4.3.4.4, cvc-enumeration-valid
  §4.3.5.4, cvc-totalDigits-valid (xsd-precisionDecimal.md §4.1), cvc-min/maxInclusive/
  Exclusive-valid §4.3.7–4.3.10, cvc-maxScale-valid §4.2.3, cvc-minScale-valid §4.3.3.
- Ratchet (arbiter-recorded via `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`; ACCEPT on commit `74a84b9`,
  parent `b476b21` = origin/main): datatypes lane +50 new cases — 40 pass
  (pdecimal001–008,010 both polarities) + 10 honest gaps (016/019/020 declines and
  the 006.n2 suite quirk); instance lane +69, schema lane +54, all newly-discovered
  precisionDecimal schema / D3_3_4 cases entering as New gaps. ZERO deletions and no
  downward flip in any lane; xpath/json/ber untouched (0 cases). Per-lane numstat
  50/0, 69/0, 54/0.
- Gate: arbiter re-verified — `go build ./... && go test ./... && go vet ./...`
  green, `golangci-lint run` 0 issues, `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance` clean
  with the per-lane numstat above. Full gate green; verdict ACCEPT on #135.

## 2026-07-18 — issue #140 — dateTimeStamp timezone reject via explicitTimezone facet

- conformance/datatypes.go: the lexical cohort now decides a type whose
  EffectiveFacets fix a non-optional explicitTimezone (xs:dateTimeStamp, §3.4.28;
  or any date/time restriction fixing it, §4.3.14.4) through the facet cohort's
  value.ValidateLexical path instead of Parse-only parseOK. New helpers
  `fixesTimezone` (reads the fact from EffectiveFacets — no dateTimeStamp
  type-name special case, STYLE D1/D3) and `decideLexicalByFacets`. Retires the
  `// GAP(datatypes)` fail-open marker on datatypesCase.
- Spec: cvc-explicitTimezone-valid §4.3.14.3 is VALUE-based, checked at
  cvc-datatype-valid §4.1.4 clause 3 AFTER lexical mapping — so parseOK
  false-accepted a tz-absent literal; the facet pipeline enforces it.
- Verified with a SYNTHETIC test (TestDatatypesLexicalDateTimeStampTimezone):
  the corpus has ZERO msData/datatypes/dateTimeStampNNN.xml cases (verified), so
  the test writes its own facet-free xs:dateTimeStamp restriction fixtures in a
  TempDir and drives the real executor — tz-bearing accepted, tz-absent rejected,
  both polarities; the tz-absent-claimed-valid assertion is the anti-regression
  (would spuriously Pass under the old path). Also pins fixesTimezone(dateTimeStamp)
  =true / (dateTime)=false and the cvc-explicitTimezone-valid rejection rule.
- Ratchet: unchanged (no corpus cases exist today; fix closes the latent
  false-accept so a future tz-absent dateTimeStamp case cannot regress it).
- Gate green: build, test, vet, golangci-lint (0 issues), conformance read-only.

## 2026-07-18 — issue #141 — yearMonthDuration/dayTimeDuration mapped

- Shipped: builtin/strict now maps xs:yearMonthDuration (§3.4.26) and
  xs:dayTimeDuration (§3.4.27) into backend.Mapping, so both validate through
  value.ValidateLexical. New files yearmonthduration.go / daytimeduration.go +
  two strict.go case arms; neither aliases parseDuration/canonicalDuration.
- Design (oracle GROUNDING + warden PRE-FLIGHT on #141): each type gets its OWN
  anchored lexical regex gating the narrower space (parseDuration's wide §3.3.6.2
  grammar would false-accept a cross-half literal — value.Mapping.Parse contract).
  Parse reuses duration's durationFields extraction and addMonths/addSeconds/
  addSecondFraction math verbatim; the non-applicable half is a hard `new(big.Int)`
  / `new(big.Rat)` stated at the construction site (definitionally absent, not
  coincidentally zero — STYLE D3), not laundered through helpers on empty groups.
- Canonical: dayTimeDuration reuses durationVal.Canonical (·months· always 0 ⇒
  default day-time branch, whose "T0S" zero case is inside [^YM]*(T.*)?).
  yearMonthDuration CANNOT — its ·months·=0 value has NO canonical representation
  (§3.4.26.1 Note: duration's "PT0S" is outside [^DT]*), surfaced as a PLAIN
  fmt.Errorf wrapping an unexported errNoYearMonthCanonical sentinel — NOT an
  xsderr verdict, because the zero value is a legally VALID value, not a violation
  (per warden refinement #2, xsderr.Error contract).
- Spec: cvc-datatype-valid §4.1.4 (Parse rejections cite §3.4.26.1/§3.4.27.1);
  cvc-pattern-valid §4.3.4.4 (seeded fixed pattern [^DT]* / [^YM]*(T.*)?);
  §3.4.26.1/§3.4.27.1 lexical + canonical mapping; §2.3.1 dt-canonical-mapping
  "(where possible)" partial-domain license. No gen_typespec.go work — the fixed
  facet tables were already generated correctly.
- Tests: parse/canonical round-trip tables per type; cross-half + grammar-trap
  rejection tables (cvc-datatype-valid); yearMonthDuration zero-value no-canonical

## 2026-07-18 — issue #149 — LOG entry (not lane-file comment) is the dismissal record

- Shipped (e1dd769): conformance/testdata/expectations/README.md gains a 9-line
  paragraph establishing that the durable dismissal record for an honestly-declined
  recorded `fail` case is the docs/LOG entry (and/or a GitHub tracking-issue
  comment) — NOT a `#` comment in the lane file. Doc-only; no Go/test/expectations
  changes.
- Why: WriteExpectations (conformance/expectations.go:190) regenerates each lane
  file purely from its in-memory status map on every ratchet run, with no
  comment-emission channel — so any hand-added `#` comment in a lane file is
  silently discarded on the next write. Some past triage issues (e.g. #146) asked
  for the incompatible file-comment pattern; #149 was filed to resolve that standing
  contradiction by naming the one place a dismissal actually survives.

  implementation before ACCEPT.

## 2026-07-18 — issue #152 — thread schema-declaring namespace context into QName/NOTATION enumeration facets

- Problem: `value.newEnumFacet` parsed every enumeration `{value}` member with a
  hardcoded `nil` context, so a prefixed QName/NOTATION member ("foo:fo") could not
  resolve its prefix. The four W3C `Facets/QName/QName_enumeration001-004` cases
  were declined in `buildOwnFacets` (an honest #125 gap) rather than decided.
- Shape (warden-ruled, implemented exactly): namespace context lives PER MEMBER,
  not per Facet. New exported surface in `xsd`: `EnumerationMember` (Lexical /
  NamespaceBindings / DefaultNamespace, immutable, `NewEnumerationMember` with the
  `*string` absence discipline — no baseURI per warden), `NewEnumerationFacet`
  (mirrors `NewAssertionsFacet`), and `Facet.EnumerationMembers()` (mirrors
  `Facet.Assertions()`). `NewFacet` now PANICS on `FacetEnumeration` (as it does for
  `FacetAssertions`); `Facet.Values()` DERIVES the lexical strings from the members
  (STYLE D3, no double storage). `EffectiveFacet` unchanged (replace-kind overlay
  carries members through, warden-confirmed).
- `value`: unexported per-member `memberContext` adapter (§3.3.18 reserved-prefix
  rules matched to `conformance.nsContext` exactly — `xml` bound, `xmlns` never,
  empty prefix → default). `newEnumFacet` iterates members, builds the adapter, and
  remaps a failed member `Parse` to `src-enumeration-value` (§4.3.5.3, the
  `src-pattern-value` sibling — already in the xsderr catalog).
- `conformance`: `decodeRestriction` now snapshots each `<enumeration>`'s in-scope
  bindings down the schema document's ancestor chain (schema-side analogue of #131's
  instance-side `readQNameContexts`); `buildOwnFacets` retires the #125 QName-enum
  decline and threads the snapshot into `xsd.NewEnumerationMember`.
- Cohort: QName_enumeration001/003 (empty instance value → invalid) and 002/004
  ("foo:fo" resolving to the same expanded QName as the schema member → valid) all
  decide correctly in both polarities (driven end-to-end + flipped-expectation in
  `TestDatatypesQNameFacets`). Spec: cvc-enumeration-valid §4.3.5.4,
  src-enumeration-value §4.3.5.3, dt-QName §3.3.18, st-restrict-facets §3.16.6.4.
- Ratchet: strict `datatypes` lane +4 (QName_enumeration001-004 fail→pass);
  arbiter records via GOXSD_RATCHET (mason does not run it). Gate green: go
  build/test/vet, golangci-lint 0 issues.
- Next: arbiter review of the diff (incl. separate warden diff review of the new
  exported surface); land on ACCEPT.
- Post-land pass (2026-07-18, PR #186 squash `b59e833`): unblock scan found no
  issue with a hard `Depends on #152`; #153 (NOTATION Facets shape) carries only a
  soft co-requisite cross-reference and stays `ready` (no relabel) — commented there
  that #152's landed surface now backs its enumeration slice. Harvest: the warden
  diff review's three non-blocking notes (xml-namespace URI triplicated across the
  pure-leaf/no-import-conformance boundary, `memberContext`/`nsContext` duplication,
  `NewEnumerationFacet(nil)` representable-but-unreachable) were all explicit
  dispositions ("no action required to land" / "accepted as consistent"), not
  deferred follow-ups — nothing filed. `src-*-value` remap precedent checked: the
  spec defines an SRC-value rule only for `pattern` and `enumeration` (both now
  remapped); `newBoundFacet`'s bare `cvc-datatype-valid` propagation is correct as
  there is no precise `src-*` target for bound facet values. No new issues.

## 2026-07-19 — issue #158 — value: compile() fail-loud on unhandled FacetKind

- Shipped (`24810b3`): added a `default:` case to `compile()`'s FacetKind
  switch in `value/facets.go` that panics with
  `"value: compile: unhandled FacetKind %s"` instead of silently dropping the
  facet. Closes the silent-drop bug CLASS that #133 fixed only for its specific
  maxScale/minScale instance — #133 added FacetMaxScale/FacetMinScale checkers
  but left the switch's no-default gap intact, so any FUTURE FacetKind added
  without a matching `compile()` arm would again be dropped with no signal.
  #158 converts that latent no-op into a caught failure at the next enum
  extension. Scope fully closed by this one change (no residual gap, unlike
  #133).
- Convention: panic (not `*xsderr.Error`) is deliberate and matches this file's
  kind-dispatch sites (`boundFacet.violates`/`boundRule`/`digitsRule`/
  `lengthRule`/`scaleRule`) and `ValidateLexical`'s panic doc — a missing
  checker is a schema-construction / programmer error the caller must already
  have rejected, not an instance validity verdict.
- Test: new `TestCompileUnhandledFacetKindPanics` in `value/facets_test.go`
  forges an out-of-range `FacetKind(99)` as an own facet and asserts the panic
  fires; mutation-verified (removing the `default:` makes the test fail).
- Trade-off (accepted, documented in code comment + commit message): adding
  `default:` disables golangci-lint's `exhaustive` compile-time
  FacetKind-coverage check for this switch (`.golangci.yml` sets
  `default-signifies-exhaustive: true`). A future FacetKind addition is now
  caught at test/runtime via the panic instead of at lint time — still strictly
  better than a silent no-op drop.
- Surface: none — no new exported symbols.
- Spec: n/a (defensive kind-dispatch hardening; no spec rule newly
  implemented).
- Ratchet: unchanged — defensive-only change, no behavior difference for the 16
  currently-handled FacetKinds. Arbiter confirmed via `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` with no
  expectation-file diffs.
- Verdict: arbiter ACCEPT. Gate green (go build/test/vet + golangci-lint +
  conformance).

  `docs/LOG` entry — that is the chronicler's job at land … leave the file

## 2026-07-19 — #162 — conformance: claim the IBM D3_3_4 precisionDecimal cohort (multi-type-per-schema executor)

- Widened the `datatypes` lane to decide the IBM `ibmData/{valid,instance_invalid}/
  D3_3_4/*` precisionDecimal cohort — a SECOND, distinct shape from #135's
  single-leaf Saxon PDecimal cohort. Where #135 validated many `<e value="…"/>`
  children against ONE type, D3_3_4 schemas declare SEVERAL named precisionDecimal
  simpleTypes and route each instance element to its own matching type. New
  `execD34Case`/`readD34Case` + decoders (`decodeD34Instance`/`decodeD34Schema`/
  `indexD34Types`/`d34RootElemTypes`/`d34SchemaLocation`) and selector `d34Case`
  in `conformance/datatypes.go`; new test `TestDatatypesD34Cohort` in
  `conformance/datatypes_test.go`. Test-only, package `conformance` — no library
  surface change, no new `xsderr` rule ID.
- Schema resolution differs deliberately from #135: the schema is read from the
  instance's own `xsi:schemaLocation` (namespace/location pair), NOT a
  case-prefix filename derivation — the Saxon prefix-stripping trick does NOT
  hold here (e.g. `d3_3_4ii01a.xml` shares `d3_3_4ii01.xsd`, a different prefix).
- Decidable shape: index every named simpleType that is a DIRECT single-step
  `<restriction base="(prefix:)?precisionDecimal">`, and require the schema's
  `<element name="root">` to carry an INLINE `<complexType><sequence>` whose every
  child binds a `type=` attribute resolving into that index. The `type=` attribute
  is resolved per child — never guessed from the element name (v14's
  `elMinMaxScale` binds `decMinMaxScale`, not a name-matched type). Decided:
  `d3_3_4v14`, `v23`, `v24` (valid) and `d3_3_4ii01[,a-f]`, `ii02`
  (instance_invalid). `ii01`'s Arabic-Indic-digit child `٠١٣` is a genuine INVALID
  verdict, not a decline.
- Honest declines (ok=false, recorded gap — never a partial decision): v15 (root
  is a type-reference, no inline complexType), v16 (`<list>` variety), v17
  (`<union>` variety), v18 (children use `ref=` not `type=`), v19–v22 (3-step
  restriction chains — same multi-step decline class as the Saxon cohort's
  `pdecimal016`).
- No new value/builtin/xsd code: reuses `strict.New()`'s precisionDecimal mapping
  (#115), `buildOwnFacets`, `xsd.NewSimpleType`, `value.ValidateLexical`,
  `strictGoverns`, `primitiveOfType`, `facetChild`, `synthNS` unchanged. Every
  rule ID is already implemented and merely re-exercised by the new
  reader/executor pair.
- Spec (all in place and reused unchanged): cvc-datatype-valid §4.1.4;
  cvc-pattern-valid §4.3.4.4; cvc-enumeration-valid §4.3.5.4 (value-space
  equal-or-identical; 10 == 1.0E1, NaN matches a NaN member by identity);
  cvc-min/maxInclusive/Exclusive-valid §4.3.7–4.3.10 (NaN incomparable ⇒ fails
  every bound, xsd-precisionDecimal.md §3.1); cvc-totalDigits-valid
  (xsd-precisionDecimal.md §4.1.1 — zero + specials vacuous, NOT decimal's
  §4.3.11.3); cvc-maxScale-valid §4.2.3 / cvc-minScale-valid §4.3.3 (#133 — zero
  is NOT scale-exempt unlike totalDigits, since ·scale· is absent only for the
  three specials).
- Not reachable: `NaN.xml`/`NaN.xsd` in `ibmData/valid/D3_3_4/` are referenced by
  no testGroup in `ibmMeta/precisionDecimal.testSet`, so `parseSuite` never emits
  a case for them — orphaned fixtures, absent from the discovered set. The 20
  `d3_3_4si09`–`si28` schema_invalid files are schemaTest-only (schema-construction
  invalidity), structurally excluded by `selectsDatatypes` (gates on
  `kind == kindInstance`), same as the Saxon cohort's schema-kind cases.
- Ratchet (arbiter-recorded via `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`; ACCEPT, commit `27983fc`,
  implementation `77e06db`, parent origin/main): ONLY `datatypes.txt` changed.
  datatypes lane +11 passes (`d3_3_4ii01[,a-f]`, `ii02`, `v14`, `v23`, `v24`) and
  +8 recorded-gap fails (`v15`–`v22`, honestly declined, New-at-fail). All 6 lanes
  reconciled: 0 regressed, 0 vanished. `instance.txt`/`schema.txt` untouched — the
  declined instance cases stay `fail` in the overlapping `instance` lane exactly as
  the existing datatypes-lane instance cases already do (lateral move, established
  pattern, never a downward flip of a previously-passing case). Figures copied from
  the arbiter's verdict comment, not recomputed.
- Gate: arbiter re-verified — `go build ./... && go vet ./... && go test ./...`
  green, `golangci-lint run` 0 issues, `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance`
  green (no regression), `TestDatatypesD34Cohort` PASS (asserts both polarities on
  decidable cases + declines on v15–v22). Verdict ACCEPT on #162.
- Friction / Next: mason again wrote an unauthorized `docs/LOG` entry inside its
  implementation commit (`77e06db`), reverted here in `5104bc2` so this single
  authoritative entry could be written at land. This is a REPEAT of the exact
  failure mode the 2026-07-19 second /retro flagged as a watch-item ("watch whether
  the mason.md boundary clause ends the mason-authored-LOG recurrence") — it
  recurred on the very next issue, so the mason.md boundary clause alone is

## 2026-07-19 — #166 — value: document per-value partial-domain Canonical error case (doc-only)

- Extended the doc comments on `value.Mapping.Canonical` (struct-level prose +
  field comment, `value/backend.go`) and `value.Canonical` (`value/value.go`,
  commit `c424173`) to document a previously-undocumented contract case: a
  NON-nil `Canonical` func may legitimately return a PLAIN (non-`*xsderr.Error`)
  error for one specific VALID value that has no canonical lexical form of its
  own — the "per-value partial-domain" case — distinct from the existing
  whole-type `Canonical == nil` case (e.g. QName). No signature, field, or
  behavior change; the two cases are kept textually distinct in the prose.
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #166): dt-canonical-mapping §2.3.1's
  "(where possible)" domain hedge + the concrete in-codebase precedent
  `builtin/strict/yearmonthduration.go`'s `canonicalYearMonthDuration`, which
  returns a plain `fmt.Errorf` for the yearMonthDuration value where ·months·=0 ∧
  ·seconds·=0 — duration's canonical "PT0S" falls outside yearMonthDuration's
  `[^DT]*` lexical space (§3.4.26.1 Note). Also cites xmlschema11-1.md
  Appendix G.1.8 item 5.
- Spec: xmlschema11-2.md §2.3.1 (dt-canonical-mapping + Note), §3.4.26.1
  (yearMonthDuration Note); xmlschema11-1.md Appendix G.1.8 item 5. Doc-only,

## 2026-07-19 — #167 — parser: raw XSD schema-document tree (parse phase)

- Shipped (commit `075d297`) the first slice of a new `parser` package: reading
  an XSD schema document into an internal element TREE (parent/child linkage) on
  top of the existing `parser/xmltree` streaming token reader. New files
  `parser/tree.go` (`Node` sealed sum, `*Element`, `*Text`) and
  `parser/document.go` (`Document`, `ReadDocument`, base-URI composition), with an
  external `parser_test` suite in `parser/document_test.go`. One export in the
  existing leaf package: `parser/xmltree`'s `xmlNamespaceURI` → `XMLNamespaceURI`
  (`name.go`, `scope.go`). No `xsd` component production in this slice — pure raw
  parse-phase infrastructure.
- Exported surface (minimal, each mapped to a named near-term consumer): `parser.Node`
  (sealed by unexported `node()`), `parser.Element` (Name/Attributes/LookupPrefix/
  Loc/Parent/Children/BaseURI — all delegating to an unexported
  `src *xmltree.StartElement`, never re-derived), `parser.Text` (Data/Loc),
  `parser.Document` (URI/Root/IsSchema), `parser.ReadDocument(uri string, r io.Reader)
  (*Document, error)`, and `xmltree.XMLNamespaceURI`. No embedding, no exported
  struct fields, no re-exported `Offset()`.
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #167): schema-document recognition at this raw
  layer is a purely NOMINAL check — root expanded name == `{http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema}schema`
  (Glossary "schema document"; §3.17.2). Full sd-valid/S4S conformance (§2.4) and
  §4.2.2 conditional-inclusion preprocessing are heavier LATER phases and must not
  happen here; `{target namespace}` interpretation (from the `targetNamespace`
  attribute per §3.17.2) is component-production, not namespace-scope tracking, so
  it does NOT belong at this layer. §3.3.18 (xmlschema11-2) makes per-node in-scope
  namespace bindings + parent linkage the load-bearing facts src-resolve (§3.17.6.2)
  needs. Crucially: `xml:base` / base-URI composition is genuinely NOT defined in
  local Structures (zero "xml:base" hits) — only named by reference (§3.13.2,
  §4.3.2), deferred by xml-infoset.md to [[XML Base]] without reproducing the
  algorithm — so it is implemented as EXTERNAL RFC 3986 knowledge
  (`net/url.URL.ResolveReference`) with a deliberate no-`src-*`/`cos-*`-citation
  rationale rather than a fabricated cite.
- Unusually thorough review chain — three independent passes:
  - **Warden PRE-FLIGHT: REVISE** (before any code) — 15 concrete shape corrections.
    Load-bearing ones: the tree must live in `parser`, NOT `parser/xmltree` (whose
    doc.go promises bounded memory / STYLE P4 — a whole-document tree would violate
    it); reuse xmltree's Name/Attribute/Loc/scope machinery via an unexported
    delegate field (no embedding, no parallel `parser.QName`); collapse the node
    sealed sum (a tree needs no EndElement); retain character data as first-class
    `*Text` children with NO lossy concatenating `Text()` convenience (D3); and the
    hardest MUST-FIX — `IsSchema()` must be a pure DERIVED predicate, never a stored
    bool and never a constructor error, since no spec rule exists for "not a schema
    document" at this layer and §3.17.2 says `<schema>` need not be the document
    element (a root-only reject would overreach).
  - **Warden DIFF REVIEW: APPROVE** (post-implementation, `075d297`) — independently
    re-verified all 15 pre-flight points against the actual landed diff (not the
    mason's report); two soft non-blocking notes only.
  - **Arbiter VERDICT: ACCEPT** — a third fully independent pass, not a rubber-stamp:
    personally re-derived the RFC 3986 base-URI arms by hand (inherit / relative-
    resolve `sub/` → `.../base/sub/` / absolute-replace, each mutation-killable),
    re-confirmed `IsSchema()` never caches and never errors, and ran its OWN fresh
    `docs/LOG` boundary check — `git diff --name-only` = exactly the 5 parser files,
    nothing under `docs/LOG/` (process issue #195's failure mode did not recur here).
- Spec: xmlschema11-1 §2.4, §3.13.2, §3.17.2, §3.17.6.2, §4.2.2, §4.3.2, Glossary
  "schema document"; xmlschema11-2 §3.3.18; xml-infoset "Base URIs" intro, §2.2
  properties 7/8. No new rule ID implemented (raw tree carries facts; validation is
  later). The xml:base composition rule is deliberately external, uncited.
- Gate: `go build/test/vet ./...` green (incl. `parser` and `parser/xmltree` after
  the rename), `golangci-lint run` 0 issues, `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance`
  clean read-only (no movement). Ratchet unchanged — no conformance lane is wired to
  this slice yet, so `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` was correctly NOT run (nothing may move); the
  read-only run confirms no regression.
- Process: per the orchestrator, mason was explicitly instructed NOT to write a LOG
  entry this round (open process issue #195: mason recurring on unauthorized docs/LOG
  commits) — the chronicler kept that responsibility. This single authoritative entry
  is being written at land, and mason's `075d297` touched no file under `docs/LOG/`
  (independently confirmed by both the warden diff review and the arbiter). A clean
  data point for #195: the boundary held this issue.
- Next: no `xsd` component production yet — later parse-phase slices wire the loader/
  Resolver (phase 2, actual location resolution/fetching), §4.2.2 conditional-inclusion
  preprocessing, and src-resolve (§3.17.6.2) walking parent links to a `<schema>`
  ancestor. A future slice may add the currently-defensive-only test for a malformed
  `xml:base` lexical that trips `composeBaseURI`'s `url.Parse` error path (the arbiter
  flagged it as an uncovered but correctly-wrapped branch, not a finding).
- Post-land pass (cartographer): no issue unblocked — the only open issues naming #167
  are #174 (still `blocked` on #172/#173) and #176 (does not depend on #167); the five
  leaf follow-ups #72/#70/#63/#51/#46 point at the producer/finalize sub-slices, not
  #167. Progress-commented epic #79 (1/17 sub-slices; seam decisions recorded for the
  next M4 slice). No new issue filed: both warden soft notes were marked no-change, and
  the arbiter's untested-defensive-`url.Parse`-branch note is already captured in the
  "Next" bullet above as a future-touch-point (a producer slice will exercise `xml:base`
  naturally) — too minor for its own tracked issue. Flagged #167 to #195 as a confounded
  data point (boundary held via orchestrator instruction, not the prose guard on its own).

## 2026-07-20 — #169 — xsd: Attribute Declaration + Attribute Group Definition + Attribute Use component shapes

- Shipped (commit `80357cc`) three new leaf component shapes in the `xsd`
  package: `AttributeDeclaration` (§3.2.1, `xsd/attributedeclaration.go`),
  `AttributeGroupDefinition` (§3.6.1, `xsd/attributegroupdefinition.go`), and
  `AttributeUse` (§3.5.1, `xsd/attributeuse.go`), with tests in the matching
  `_test.go` files. Shape-only slice mirroring the #168 `ElementDeclaration`
  precedent: cheap structural checks at construction, all cross-component
  property-correctness needing resolved types / back-references deferred to the
  finalize phase (#173). Correctly reused the shared `xsd.ScopeVariety` closed
  set per the #168 post-land directive — no second scope-variety enum introduced.
- Renamed the pre-existing `xsd.AttributeUse` XML-token enum (the §3.2.2 `use=`
  lexical attribute) to `xsd.AttributeUseToken`, freeing the bare `AttributeUse`
  name for the new §3.5 component (`xsd/closedsets.go`, `xsd/closedsets_test.go`).
  This is a warden-directed design decision, IN-SCOPE (necessary to introduce the
  component cleanly under the package's bare-name convention — no `...Component`
  suffix wart), not scope creep; the #168 LOG entry already anticipated this
  collision.
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #169): §3.2.1 (`ad`, `sc_a`, `vc_a`),
  §3.2.2.2 (`dcl.att.local`), §3.5.1 (`au`, `vc_au`), §3.6.1 (`agd`), §3.6.6
  (`ag-props-correct`). Load-bearing spec nuances captured: `sc_a.{parent}`
  (CTD | AGD) must NOT be conflated with element declaration's `sc_e.{parent}`
  (CTD | MGD) — distinct two-member sums despite identical `{variety}` wording;
  a locally-declared attribute's own `{value constraint}` is always absent (only
  the sibling Use carries default/fixed — the structural reason #70 exists);
  `{attribute uses}` is spec-worded a *set* (`ag-props-correct` clause 2 forbids
  duplicate expanded names), represented here as a document-order slice.
- Warden PRE-FLIGHT: REVISE on the first sketch, one blocking finding — the
  `AttributeUse.{attribute declaration}` slot cannot be a bare QName ref. That
  orphans the local sibling declaration (`dcl.att.local`, §3.2.2.2), whose SOLE
  owner is that slot (it lives in no symbol table), and QName is not even a
  unique key for local declarations. Directed a two-member sealed sum instead:
  `AttributeDeclarationOrRef` = `LocalAttributeDeclaration` (by value, the
  `dcl.att.local` case) | `AttributeDeclarationRef` (deferred QName, the
  `ref.att.local` §3.2.2.3 case), mirroring `simpletype.go`'s `Variety`
  precedent. `ElementDeclaration` never hit this (particles reference terms
  structurally, not via a use→decl slot) — a new precedent, not a copied one.
- Warden DIFF REVIEW of `80357cc`: APPROVE — every blocking pre-flight finding
  resolved (sealed sum exactly as specified, flag-based optional slots for
  `{value constraint}`/`{attribute wildcard}`, D3 respected — `AttributeUse`
  stores only `required bool`, not the co-derivable `use=` token, rename complete
  with zero stale references). One non-blocking nit: the sealed-sum marker method
  is named `attributeDeclarationRef()` after the *Ref* variant rather than
  `attributeDeclarationOrRef()` after the interface (the `Variety`/`variety()`
  precedent) — unexported, invisible to consumers, seals correctly, explicitly
  not a repair item.
- Constructor validation enforced now (structural, resolution-free):
  `NewAttributeDeclaration` — `a-props-correct` clause 1 structural part (reject
  unknown `ScopeVariety`, reject a present `ValueConstraint` of unknown kind);
  `NewAttributeGroupDefinition` — `ag-props-correct` clause 2 (no two
  `{attribute uses}` sharing an expanded name), computable WITHOUT resolution
  because both sum variants expose an unresolved expanded name directly, scanned
  in document order against a `map[QName]struct{}` seen-set (membership only,
  never ranged for output/error order — S3/D2); `NewAttributeUse` —
  `au-props-correct` clause 1 (reject the nil interface).
- Deliberately unmodeled at this leaf (each with a named later owner):
  `a-props-correct` clause 2 (Simple Default Valid) — needs the resolved
  `{type definition}`, #173; `au-props-correct` clauses 2/3 — vacuous now, no
  `{value constraint}` slot on `AttributeUse` yet, that slot is #70; the
  §3.6.2.2 referenced-`<attributeGroup>` `{attribute uses}` union — cross-component,
  #173; `{scope}.{parent}` on `AttributeDeclaration` — entirely unmodeled (only
  `ScopeVariety` carried), because the Complex Type Definition half of the
  `sc_a.{parent}` sum does not exist yet (#170/#171), mirroring
  `ElementDeclaration`'s same documented gap.
- Spec: xmlschema11-1 §3.2.1, §3.2.2.2, §3.5.1, §3.6.1; `a-props-correct` clause 1
  (structural part), `ag-props-correct` clause 2, `au-props-correct` clause 1
  implemented (clauses deferred as listed above).
- Arbiter VERDICT: ACCEPT — full gate green (build/test/vet, `golangci-lint run`
  0 issues, conformance clean; new xsd tests 13 PASS with `-count=1`), no findings.
  Ran the ratchet (`GOXSD_RATCHET=1`) and confirmed unchanged — `ok`, exit 0,
  working tree clean afterward (`git status --short conformance/testdata/expectations/`
  empty). No lane moved either direction. As predicted for a leaf shape with no
  parser producer wired in.
- Ratchet: unchanged (arbiter ran the ratchet and confirmed no expectations lane
  moved). The `schema` lane moves only when a future producer (#176 and friends)
  wires these shapes in.
- Positive pattern (worth a retro glance if it recurs): this is the SECOND
  consecutive issue where a shared closed set (`ScopeVariety`) was correctly
  reused because a prior issue's code/thread comment flagged the reuse in advance
  ("expected to reuse this same type" / the #168 post-land directive comment on
  #169). Forward-pointing breadcrumbs — in-code comments AND issue-thread
  post-land notes — are doing real cross-session coordination work here. Two data
  points, not yet a confirmed trend; flagging for the next /retro to confirm or
  dismiss.
- Next: #170 (particle / model-group) remains the other unstarted first-wave (#79)
  shape. The two shape halves of the `sc_a.{parent}` / `sc_e.{parent}` sums still
  await Complex Type Definition (#170/#171) and Model Group Definition before
  `{scope}.{parent}` becomes constructible. #173 (finalize/resolve) is the named
  consumer for the deferred `a-props-correct` clause 2, the §3.6.2.2 union, and
  all `{type definition}`/`{attribute declaration}` QName resolution; #70 owns the
  `AttributeUse.{value constraint}` + `key-evc` override layer; #176 (complex-type
  producer) is the first slice that will actually populate these shapes and move a
  lane.


## 2026-07-20 — #170 — xsd: Particle + Model Group + Model Group Definition content-model shapes

- Shipped (commit `f4a12e1`) the content-model substrate in the `xsd`
  package: `Particle` (§3.9.1, `xsd/particle.go`), `ModelGroup` (§3.8.1,
  `xsd/modelgroup.go`), `ModelGroupDefinition` (§3.7.1,
  `xsd/modelgroupdefinition.go`), and the sealed `Term` set plus its
  pre-resolution slot sum `TermOrRef` (`xsd/term.go`), with matching
  `_test.go` files; surgical `term()` marker additions retrofitted onto the
  pre-existing `xsd/elementdeclaration.go` (#168) and `xsd/wildcard.go` (#31).
  10 files, +620, all additive. Composes existing `Occurs` (#29) and
  `Compositor` (`xsd/closedsets.go`) directly — no re-wrapping, no re-exposed
  min/max. Last of the M4 first wave (#79): #167 parse phase, #168 element
  decl, #169 attribute decl/group/use, #170 this one — **all four now landed.**
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #170): §3.9.1 Particle tableau, §3.8.1
  Model Group tableau, §3.7.1 Model Group Definition tableau, the closed
  3-kind `Term` set (Element Declaration | Wildcard | Model Group, spec line
  522 — exhaustive), and the deferred-resolution structure of `<group ref>` /
  `<element ref>` via `src-resolve` (§3.17.6.2) / `ref.elt.global` (§3.3.2.4) /
  `declare-namedModelGroup` (§3.7.2). Load-bearing spec facts captured: a
  resolved group reference's `{term}` literally IS the referenced MGD's
  `{model group}` (no wrapper component at the resolved level, so no permanent
  4th `Term` kind); `max occurs = 0` is an XML-mapping normalization
  ("maps to no component at all", producer #176's concern), NOT a
  Particle-constructor invariant; `mg-props-correct` clause 2 no-cycle-check is
  finalize-phase (#173/#176), never a constructor concern (PRINCIPLES 9).
- Warden PRE-FLIGHT: REVISE (planned surface, before code), one blocking
  finding — do NOT fold the deferred `<group ref>` case in as a 4th `Term`
  variant; keep `Term` = exactly the 3 resolved §3.9 kinds and model the
  deferred ref as a **separate slot sum** on `Particle.{term}`, mirroring
  `xsd/attributeuse.go`'s `AttributeDeclarationOrRef` precedent (a wrapping
  slot sum, not a baked-in extra variant). A 4th `Term` variant would leak a
  construction-phase concern into the resolved type and force every exhaustive
  Term switch to carry a dead case. Also corrected the grounding: `NewParticle`
  must ACCEPT `Occurs{0,0}` (the vacuous-particle allowance from #29), reject
  only a nil `{term}`.
- Grounding follow-up (gating the pre-flight finding): oracle confirmed
  `<element ref>` (§3.3.2.4 `ref.elt.global`) is a genuine forward-reference
  needing the SAME deferred treatment as `<group ref>`, so the slot sum's
  deferred axis needs both `ElementDeclarationRef` and `ModelGroupRef` (no
  `WildcardRef` — wildcards are never QName-referenced). Locked design:
  `TermOrRef` = `ResolvedTerm{Term}` | `ElementDeclarationRef{Name QName}` |
  `ModelGroupRef{Name QName}`, distinct from the 3-variant resolved `Term` sum.
- Warden DIFF REVIEW of `f4a12e1`: APPROVE — every pre-flight requirement met
  (`Term`/`TermOrRef` genuinely sealed via unexported markers; `TermOrRef` is
  the mandated separate slot sum; `NewParticle` accepts `Occurs{0,0}` and
  rejects nil term; `NewModelGroup` validates compositor with no cycle-check;
  `NewModelGroupDefinition`'s zero-`ModelGroup` rejection ruled a legitimate
  Required-singleton boundary check, not a D3 violation — AGD has no Required
  singleton so the shapes aren't comparable). One NON-BLOCKING hardening nit:
  `NewParticle` guards only the outer `TermOrRef` nil, not a
  `ResolvedTerm{Term: nil}` (a non-nil interface wrapping a nil inner `Term`,
  which would nil-dispatch on `term()` later). Not fixed now.
- Constructor validation enforced now (structural, resolution-free):
  `NewParticle` — `p-props-correct` (reject nil `{term}`; occurrence-range
  invariants delegated to `Occurs`, D3); `NewModelGroup` — reject invalid
  `{compositor}` incl. the zero value, mirroring `NewWildcard`'s
  `ProcessContents` guard; `NewModelGroupDefinition` — reject the zero
  `{model group}` (Required singleton). `{particles}`/`{annotations}` copied
  in and out, document order preserved losslessly (D2, PRINCIPLES 14), empty→nil.
- Deliberately unmodeled at this leaf (each with a named later owner):
  `mg-props-correct` clause 2 no-circular-groups and named-group-reference
  termination — finalize (#173/#176); all `<group ref>`/`<element ref>` QName
  resolution (the `TermOrRef` deferred variants swap to live `Term` values at
  finalize) — #173; the `max occurs = 0` "no Particle" normalization — producer
  #176. UPA (`cos-nonambig`), EDC (`cos-element-consistent`), `cos-all-limited`,
  and particle restriction (§3.9.6 → `derivation-ok-restriction` §3.4.6.3) all
  land in #180/#181 — the shape deliberately preserves particle identity,
  occurs, and document order so those remain decidable without reshaping.
- Spec: xmlschema11-1 §3.9.1, §3.8.1, §3.7.1; `p-props-correct`,
  `mg-props-correct` (structural part), `mgd-props-correct` implemented
  (cross-component clauses deferred as listed above).
- Arbiter VERDICT: ACCEPT — full gate green (build/test/vet, `golangci-lint run`
  clean, conformance ok). Ran the ratchet (`GOXSD_RATCHET=1`) and confirmed
  **unchanged** — zero changes to any expectations file (`git status` on
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/` empty afterward), verified not merely
  asserted. As expected for a leaf shape with no parser producer wired in; the
  `schema` lane moves only when #176 populates these shapes. Independent STYLE
  review (S1/S2/S3/D3/T1/T5) clean, exported surface fully justified and
  documented, tests verified able to fail by construction (incl.
  `AcceptsVacuousOccurs`, which guards the warden-mandated `Occurs{0,0}`
  acceptance in the other direction).
- Ratchet: unchanged (arbiter ran the ratchet and confirmed no expectations
  lane moved).
- Friction: arbiter noted the orchestrating session's LOCAL `main` ref was
  stale (`6882614`) during judgment, so it correctly judged `origin/main..HEAD`
  (`4fe23a7..f4a12e1`) instead. A process note, not a code issue — but the
  second consecutive session where a stale-ref/base-selection gotcha showed up
  in an arbiter comment; flag for the next /retro to confirm whether a
  "fetch/reset local main before judging" step belongs in WORKFLOW.md or the
  arbiter prompt (one more data point makes it a pattern).
- Next / REQUIRED FOLLOW-UP (harvest into a tracking issue on #173/#176, do
  not let it leak): the warden/arbiter `ResolvedTerm{Term: nil}` nil-dispatch
  guard. `ResolvedTerm` is the first sealed-sum variant in the codebase to wrap
  an INTERFACE (`Term`) rather than a value type — the `AttributeUse`
  precedent's `LocalAttributeDeclaration` wraps a value whose zero is benign,

  in this shape-only slice — a nil-wrapped `ResolvedTerm` can only arise from a
  caller bug and cannot reach a dispatch path until resolution exists) but
  recorded it as a REQUIRED follow-up: add a one-line guard in `NewParticle`
  (`xsd/particle.go:53`) rejecting `ResolvedTerm{Term: nil}`, OR enforce it at
  the finalize resolution chokepoint — whichever of #173 (finalize/resolve) or

- Next: M4 first wave (#167–#170) is complete. The post-land pass should now

## 2026-07-20 — #171 — xsd: Complex Type Definition + {content type} + derivation-method shapes

  `ComplexType` + `NewComplexType`; the `ContentType` sealed sum

  content collapsed into one `ElementContent{Mixed bool}` variant per the warden

  `DerivationRestriction`, `ContentEmpty`/`ContentSimple`/`ContentElementOnly`/
  `ContentMixed`, and `OpenContentInterleave`/`OpenContentSuffix` closed sets
  from `closedsets.go` — zero new enums introduced.
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #171): §3.4.1 Complex_Type_Definition_details.
  The grounding caught that the issue's original acceptance list under-specified
  the shape — it omitted `{final}`, `{context}`, `{annotations}`, `{name}`, and
  `{target namespace}` from the full 13-property §3.4.1 tableau. `{base type
  definition}` modeled pre-resolution-only (`baseTypeDefinitionName QName` +
  accessor, no resolved pointer field), mirroring
  `ElementDeclaration.typeDefinitionName`; resolution deferred to #173.

  would force breaking churn later; INCLUDE `{annotations}`/`{name}`/`{target

  deferral `ElementDeclaration` (#168) already made for `{scope}.{parent}` (a
  back-pointer that creates phase confusion before the containing component

  rather than two sum variants.

  repair round was needed: the pre-flight caught the scope gaps before any code

  front-loading the surface review — a clean single-pass landing.

  (reject unknown derivation method / content-type / open-content-mode values);
  `NewOpenContent` — reject an unknown `OpenContentMode`. Everything needing
  resolved cross-component state is deferred.

## 2026-07-21 — #172 — xsd: Schema component + schema-set symbol tables + phase seam + Query views

- Shipped (commit `84b96c5`) the top-level Schema/schema-set container in the
  `xsd` package: three additive files, `xsd/schema.go` (+297), `xsd/schema_test.go`
  (+268), `xsd/typedefinition.go` (+38). This is the first M4 issue past the leaf
  shapes — the schema-set container + phase seam that the producer spine (#174/#176)
  and finalize (#173) build on. New pieces: (1) a `TypeDefinition` sealed sum
  (`typeDefinition()` marker + `Name()`) unifying `ComplexType` and `*SimpleType`
  into the one `{type definitions}` bucket §3.17.6.2 clause 1.1 mandates; (2) a
  mutable `SchemaBuilder` accumulator — document-order slices only, one `Add*` per
  Schema-tableau property, §4.2.4 lazy/just-in-time construction; (3) an immutable
  `Schema` compiled set with 7 internal by-expanded-QName indexes; (4) a `Finalize`
  phase seam; (5) three minimal capability Query views
  (`ElementResolver`/`AttributeResolver`/`TypeResolver`) with noun-form
  `Element`/`Attribute`/`Type` lookups. First use of Go generics in the repo
  (`indexByName[T]`/`cloneSlice[T]`), collapsing 7 near-identical index builders.
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #172): §3.17.1 (the 8-property Schema tableau),
  `src-resolve` (§3.17.6.2), `sch-props-correct` (§3.17.6.1), `cvc-resolve-instance`
  (§3.17.6.3), §4.2.1/§4.2.3/§4.2.4. The grounding CAUGHT AND CORRECTED A MISLABELED
  CITATION IN THE ISSUE BODY ITSELF: the issue cited "§4.2.2 (`{schema documents}`
  assembly into one schema set)", but §4.2.2 is titled "Conditional inclusion"
  (vc:minVersion/maxVersion), unrelated to document assembly — the correct cites are
  §4.2.1 + §4.2.3, and `{schema documents}` is a PSVI-only, non-normative
  bookkeeping property (§3.17.5.1), NOT a component property to model as a field.
  A concrete instance of oracle grounding catching issue-authoring drift before it
  propagated into code/comments — the citation error would otherwise have been
  copied into the finalize/producer issues downstream.
- Warden PRE-FLIGHT (required by the issue body — largest new exported surface in
  the epic): mason posted a design sketch with 6 open questions; warden returned
  CHANGES-REQUIRED with one blocker plus 4 documentation requirements. The blocker
  (Q2): `Finalize` was originally proposed to SILENTLY last-writer-wins on a
  duplicate expanded name with a `// GAP: sch-props-correct clause 2 in #173`
  comment. Warden rejected this on two grounds — (a) a silent drop makes an illegal
  input masquerade as a legal finalized `*Schema`, the exact state this issue exists
  to forbid; the map-keyed symbol table structurally cannot hold two components
  under one key, so the collision must be a real `error` from `Finalize` (the same
  "cheap, locally-computable, cross-reference-free clause enforced where it's cheap"
  pattern `NewAttributeGroupDefinition`/`NewTypeTable` already use — NOT pulling in
  #173's cross-reference work); and (b) `GAP` is reserved for XPath fail-open sites
  (STYLE 9), so using it in schema construction would be a marker-convention
  violation. The 4 doc requirements: maps-are-derived-indexes godoc; Resolver godocs
  must each name their downstream consumer (STYLE 8); TypeDefinition must document
  the ComplexType-by-value / `*SimpleType`-by-pointer switch asymmetry; `Add*`
  scoped to top-level named components with a nil-guard panic (programmer error, not
  xsderr — matches `NewFacet`).
- Warden POST-IMPLEMENTATION DIFF REVIEW of `84b96c5`: APPROVE — confirmed all 8
  required fixes genuinely present in the code with file:line citations (dup

## 2026-07-21 — #173 — xsd: finalize/resolve phase — src-resolve QName resolution + named-circularity rejection

- Shipped (commit `69aebf0`) the finalize/resolve pass over the schema-set
  builder (#172): one new file `xsd/resolve.go` (+524) plus `xsd/resolve_test.go`
  (+316), and body-only edits to `complextype.go`/`elementdeclaration.go`/
  `identityconstraint.go`/`modelgroup.go`/`particle.go`/`schema.go`/`term.go`
  and `schema_test.go` (no exported-surface change). Three things landed: (1)
  `src-resolve` (§3.17.6.2) existence resolution over EVERY in-scope QName
  reference kind — element/attribute `{type definition}` (cl.1.1), `<attribute
  ref>` (1.2), `<element ref>` + `{substitution group affiliations}` (1.3),
  complex-type `{base type definition}` (1.1), `<group ref>` (1.5), keyref
  `{referenced key}` (1.7), plus the type-table alternatives/default the design
  hadn't enumerated; a dangling OR wrong-kind reference is the same kind-specific
  index miss, charged the one `src-resolve` rule differing only in message. (2)
  Dependency-order-free circularity rejection as Phase B — complex-type base
  chains (`ct-props-correct` cl.3, xs:anyType the one permitted self-base per
  §3.4.7), `<group ref>` graph (`mg-props-correct` cl.2 no-circular-groups),
  substitution-group affiliation graph (`e-props-correct` cl.5) — each a
  finalize-scoped DFS/chain-walk whose cycle guard is discarded when `resolve`
  returns (no seen-set threaded into any later traversal, PRINCIPLES 5). (3) The
  folded `ResolvedTerm{Term: nil}` constructor guard at `NewParticle` charging
  `p-props-correct` clause 1.
- Key design decision (mason DESIGN PRE-FLIGHT, warden-affirmed): resolution is
  a VALIDATION-ONLY pass — it walks the compiled component tree, looks each QName
  up through the existing indexes/Query views, rejects unresolvable/circular
  targets, and returns the SAME `*Schema` with no component rebuilt, no
  resolved-pointer slot stored, no ref QName rewritten. The rejected alternative
  (rebuild the immutable tree bottom-up with substituted pointer slots) was
  turned down on D3: a stored resolved `*TypeDefinition` beside a retained QName
  is state derivable from `(QName + index)` by an O(1) lookup that already exists
  — the exact two-encodings redundancy D3 forbids, with no measured hot path.
  It also honors D4 more cleanly (dependency ordering IS the one-time circularity
  detection D4 carves out) and adds ZERO exported surface — a consumer that later
  wants the live component follows the ref by read-time lookup
  (`schema.Type(ref)`/`Element`/`Attribute`). Consequence: finalize never
  PRODUCES a `ResolvedTerm`, so the nil-inner guard's only entry point is the
  producer path through `NewParticle` — the constructor is its correct home, not
  a fallback.
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #173, read from `xmlschema11-1.md`): the
  4-clause `src-resolve` conjunction + per-reference-kind mapping table; the
  forbidden-vs-permitted circularity taxonomy — crucially that ATTRIBUTE-group
  circular reference is explicitly PERMITTED (§3.6.2.1, a fixed-point union not a
  cycle check) and `<attributeGroup ref>` creates NO persistent ref component
  (folded inline at producer mapping time), both correctly out of #173's scope;
  that substitution-group circularity is actively FORBIDDEN (`e-props-correct`
  cl.5), not merely acyclic-by-construction; that dependency ordering is a goxsd8
  choice (§4.1 Layer 1 permits lazy or bulk), not a spec mandate; and that
  `Finalize` hard-failing on an unresolved required reference is a deliberate
  conformance-processor policy (§5.3 itself permits degrade-to-lax) — documented
  as a choice, not an oversight. Grounding also corrected a stray citation
  (§4.2.4 is `<redefine>`, laziness is §4.1) fixed in schema.go while touching it.
- Warden PRE-FLIGHT: `revise` — core validation-only decision AFFIRMED (D3
  no-stored-pointer, T1 guard-at-constructor citing p-props-correct cl.1, T5
  no capability-interface for the two index-direct readers, the clause-4 +
  `<attributeGroup ref>` deferrals all principled), with 7 findings, all
  addressed in the landed diff and each confirmed by the arbiter in code (not
  merely claimed): (1) stale "ref is REPLACED by the live component at finalize"
  doc comments across term/particle/elementdeclaration/complextype/modelgroup/
  identityconstraint rewritten to "finalize VALIDATES the ref resolves and
  RETAINS it, consumers follow by read-time lookup" — a wrong promise retracted,
  not a constraint honored; (2) D2 document-order iteration driven from the
  `s.types`/`s.elements`/`s.modelGroups` slices, indexes used only for point
  lookups (no `range …Index`); (3) D4 cycle guards proven finalize-scoped and
  discarded, never leaked into Walk/Match; (4) the simple-type base-cycle
  deferral justified by the concrete invariant (`{base}`/`{item}`/`{member}` are
  live setter-less pointers, so no QName-based simple-type base ref is
  representable to misuse) rather than "out of scope"; (5) no new
  `Schema.ModelGroup`/`IdentityConstraint` accessors (T5), the follow-cost
  asymmetry documented in the `resolve()` FOLLOW-COST block; (6) the #172
  follow-up framed as a PARTIAL discharge; (7) all six sealed-sum switches made
  exhaustive with a `default: panic` asserting the invariant.
- Arbiter VERDICT: ACCEPT (@`69aebf0` vs origin/main). Full gate run
  independently this session — `go build`/`test`/`vet` green, `golangci-lint run`
  0 issues, conformance read-only check ok, and `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run wrote NO
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/` file (git tree clean after), so movement
  is genuinely unchanged, matching the prediction. Spec fidelity spot-checked
  clause-by-clause against the grounding (every rule ID in every emitted message
  correct, incl. keyref→keyref charged `c-props-correct` cl.1 not `src-resolve`);
  tests confirmed mutation-killable via `assertRule`/`xsderr.RuleOf` pinning the
  EXACT rule ID (keyref-to-keyref, anyType-self-base accept vs other-self-base

- PARTIAL #172-follow-up discharge: this issue wires the present-tense callers
  the three Resolver views (`Type`/`Element`/`Attribute`) lacked, and reads
  `modelGroupIndex` + `idcIndex` for the first time (via `resolveModelGroupName`/
  `resolveKeyref`). It does NOT read `attributeGroupIndex` or `notationIndex` —

- Spec: `src-resolve` §3.17.6.2 (cl.1.1/1.2/1.3/1.5/1.7), `ct-props-correct`
  §3.4.6.1 cl.3 + §3.4.7 any-type-itself, `mg-props-correct` §3.8.6.1 cl.2,
  `e-props-correct` §3.3.6.1 cl.5, `c-props-correct` §3.11.6.1 cl.1 (keyref

- Ratchet: unchanged (arbiter verified — `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` wrote no expectation

- Next / Friction: the arbiter flagged three mason design DEVIATIONS as
