## 2026-07-21 — #174 — parser: producer spine — top-level simpleType/element/attribute declarations → xsd components

- Shipped (commit `85539fa`) the first end-to-end parser producer: new file
  `parser/produce.go` exposing `parser.Produce(doc *Document, backend value.Backend)
  (*xsd.Schema, error)` (+ `parser/produce_test.go`, 21 tests). It maps a schema
  document's top-level `<simpleType>`/`<element>`/`<attribute>` declarations from
  the raw parse tree (#167) into `xsd` components in DOCUMENT ORDER, feeds them to
  `xsd.SchemaBuilder` (#172), then calls `Finalize` (#173). Complex types/groups
  are deferred to #176 onward — this is the narrow top-level-only slice. Design
  process on the #174 thread: oracle GROUNDING → mason design-only sketch →
  warden PRE-FLIGHT review (verdict: revise, 4 findings) → mason implementation of
  the revised design → arbiter ACCEPT. Full gate green independently
  (build/test/vet/lint/conformance), verified by both mason and the arbiter.
- Key design decisions (for future-session context):
  - `xsd.SimpleType`'s `{base type definition}` is a live `*SimpleType` pointer
    required AT CONSTRUCTION (unlike `ElementDeclaration`/`AttributeDeclaration`'s
    deferred-QName `{type definition}` that finalize resolves later).
    `xsd/resolve.go` Phase A/B does not resolve or cycle-check simple-type bases
    at all — so this producer does its OWN topological/memoized build (mirroring
    `builtin/seed.go`'s `build(name)` pattern) AND owns the circular-base
    rejection (`st-props-correct` §3.16.6.1 cl.2) that no other code path in the
    repo provides.
  - Warden's pre-flight caught 4 issues in the original sketch before any code was
    written: (1) a two-map (`built`+`building`) cycle guard could disagree —
    collapsed to one 3-state map (absent / present-nil / present-non-nil)
    mirroring `xsd/resolve.go`'s existing color-map idiom; (2) builtin base
    resolution needed explicit pointer-identity pre-seeding from `builtin.Seed`;
    (3) unsupported forms (list/union simpleType, enumeration/assertion facets,
    inline anonymous element/attribute types) must be EXPLICIT rejections, not
    silent zero-value fallbacks that would false-accept; (4)
    restriction-of-atomic variety derivation must propagate the base's own
    `Atomic.Primitive` pointer, not synthesize a fresh one.
  - Element/attribute producers support the `type=` form ONLY this slice (no
    inline anonymous type child) — forced by the component shape (no anonymous-type
    pointer slot exists) and deliberately avoids the already-filed #210
    anonymous-type-uniqueness hazard in `Finalize`.
  - `Produce` returns only the FIRST error (no multi-error collection) — a
    deliberate, documented deviation from `parser/doc.go`'s eventual full-`Parse`
    promise to collect errors in document order; scoped narrowly to this slice.
- Known limitation (documented in test `TestProduceElementNoTypeDefaultsAnyType`):
  a bare `<xs:element name="e"/>` correctly defers to `xs:anyType` per spec, but
  `xs:anyType` is a Complex Type Definition this slice never produces
  (`builtin.Seed` only yields simple types), so finalize rejects with
  `src-resolve`. Inherent to combining the anyType-default rule with the
  no-complex-types scope; resolves once #176 lands.
- Spec: `src-element` (cl.1, cl.3), `src-attribute` (cl.1, cl.4), `src-simple-type`
  (cl.2 + list/union/enumeration-not-yet-supported), `st-props-correct` §3.16.6.1
  cl.2 (circular simpleType base), `src-resolve` (unbound QName prefix,
  unresolvable `base=`).
- Ratchet: unchanged (arbiter confirmed via `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` — zero
  expectation-file changes; no lane driver is wired to this producer yet, that's
  #175). Figures carried from the arbiter, not recomputed.
- Surprises: none material. The warden pre-flight front-loaded all 4 findings, so

## 2026-07-22 — #46 — xsd: cross-type variety/base shape + st-props-correct cl.2/3/5 + cos-st-restricts

- Shipped (commit `3c47c35`) construction-time validation of simple-type
  derivation in a new file `xsd/derivation.go` (329 lines) + table-driven
  `xsd/derivation_test.go` (219 lines, both polarities) + a 21-line wiring change
  in `xsd/simpletype.go`. Implemented: `st-props-correct` §3.16.6.1 cl.3 (base
  `{final}` blocking-set check, a shared predicate that also discharges
  cos-st-restricts 1.2/2.2.2.2/3.2.2.2), cl.5 (facet processor-support check),
  cos-st-restricts §3.16.6.2 cases 1/2/3 structural sub-clauses (atomic
  base-variety compatibility; list item-shape + constructed-vs-restricted
  branching on `B==anySimpleType`; union member-shape + same branching), and
  `cos-st-derived-ok` §3.16.6.3 as the `derivedOKSimple` relation. Zero new
  exported identifiers. Full gate green independently (build/vet/test/lint),
  verified by both mason and the arbiter.
- Design process on the #46 thread: oracle GROUNDING → mason design-only sketch →
  warden PRE-FLIGHT review (verdict: APPROVED WITH CHANGES, 5 required) → mason
  implementation of the revised design → arbiter ACCEPT (no repair round).
- Key decisions (for future-session context):
  - Grounding corrected the issue's guessed section number (§3.16.6.3 → the actual
    §3.16.6.2 for cos-st-restricts) and found the issue's guessed helper rule names
    `cos-list-of-atomic`/`cos-no-circular-unions` don't exist in this local XSD 1.1
    spec text — their substance is folded into cos-st-restricts cl.2.1/3.3.
  - Mason's design pre-flight OVERTURNED the issue's own framing: the checks were
    assumed to need a finalize-phase pass (they "need the resolved graph"), but
    `SimpleType`'s `{base}`/`{item}`/`{member}` are already live `*SimpleType`
    pointers at construction (unlike `ComplexType`, which defers via QName), so the
    checks belong in the `NewSimpleType`/`NewPrimitiveType` constructors — stronger
    than a finalize pass (illegal states unrepresentable, T1/T7).
  - Warden's 5 required changes, all verified applied by the arbiter: (a) scope
    cos-st-restricts to structural/derivation clauses only, deferring the
    facet-value sub-clauses (1.3, 2.2.1.2, 2.2.2.4/.5, 3.2.1.2, 3.2.2.4/.5) that
    need package `value` — `xsd` must stay a pure leaf; (b) reword the no-cycle-check
    justification from "structurally unrepresentable" to "unconstructible via
    constructors" (PRINCIPLES 5), because it surfaced a genuine pre-existing [T1]
    gap; (c) new helpers in their own file, not inline in `simpletype.go`; (d)
    precise per-clause rule citations; (e) the atomic-variety walk must terminate on
    base identity, never by dereferencing the self-referencing `Atomic.Primitive`
    field.
  - Deferred, documented in-code (not silently dropped): the facet-value sub-clauses
    (out of pure-leaf `xsd`'s reach); st-props-correct cl.2 and cos-st-restricts
    cl.3.3 (both correctly framed as "unconstructible via constructors" no-ops per
    warden's wording, with #215 tracking the field-tightening that makes that claim
    airtight rather than convention-dependent).
  - Collateral: the new cl.5 check rejects a forged out-of-range `FacetKind(99)` at
    construction time, earlier than a pre-existing test's panic-path assertion inside
    `value.compile()`, so mason renamed/retargeted
    `TestCompileUnhandledFacetKindPanics` → `TestUnsupportedFacetKindRejected` to
    assert the earlier rejection point. `compile()`'s internal fail-loud default
    stays as defense-in-depth.
- Spec: `st-props-correct` §3.16.6.1 cl.3, cl.5; `cos-st-restricts` §3.16.6.2 cases
  1/2/3 (structural sub-clauses); `cos-st-derived-ok` §3.16.6.3.
- Ratchet: unchanged (arbiter confirmed via `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` — zero
  expectation-file changes; this is a leaf-only construction-time hardening and no
  conformance lane driver exercises this path yet, consistent with the issue's own
  stated expectation). Figures carried from the arbiter, not recomputed. Real
  evidence against false-rejects: `./builtin/...` construction still passes cleanly
  through the stricter constructors.
- Survey note: a stale leftover `wip/issue-145` branch (issue already
  closed/merged, not resumable) was found and skipped during survey; #46 was the
  oldest `ready` issue with all deps closed (#174 producer spine, #173
  finalize/resolve, #33 facet+variety model).
- Surprises: the warden pre-flight surfaced a genuine pre-existing [T1] gap —
  `Atomic.Primitive`/`List.Item`/`Union.Members` are exported fields guarded only
  by a "read-only by convention" comment, so a mutation-induced cycle actually IS

- Friction: none material this cycle; warden pre-flight again front-loaded all

- Next / deferred: (a) the facet-value sub-clauses of cos-st-restricts (1.3,

## 2026-07-22 — #157 — xsd: precisionDecimal maxScale/minScale schema-construction SCCs

- Shipped (commits `4ab2f50` then `eb77edc`) four new schema-CONSTRUCTION-time
  checks in `xsd/derivation.go` — a `checkScaleFacets` dispatch plus
  `checkScaleValueRestriction`, `checkScaleFixed`, and `checkScaleConsistency`
  helpers — enforcing `maxScale-valid-restriction` (§4.2.4),
  `minScale-valid-restriction` (§4.3.4), the minScale≤maxScale consistency SCC,
  and `{fixed}` inheritance (`f-ms-fixed`/`f-mns-fixed`). New test
  `TestScaleFacetSCCs` in `xsd/derivation_test.go` (16 sub-cases) covers both
  value-restriction directions, the vacuous cases, the {fixed}-independent-of-
  value-restriction distinction, and a multi-level (A←B←C) transitive chain
  proving `EffectiveFacets()` handles inheritance with no manual ancestor walk.
  Added the five new rule-ID literals to `tools/rulecat/main.go`'s
  `irregularRules` and regenerated `xsderr/catalog.go` via `go generate ./...`
  (never hand-edited).
- Key decisions (for future-session context):
  - These are schema-CONSTRUCTION-time checks, distinct from the instance-time
    `cvc-maxScale-valid`/`cvc-minScale-valid` that landed earlier in #133 —
    same facets, different phase (are the facet VALUES a legal restriction of the
    base's, vs. does a given instance value satisfy them).
  - The minScale≤maxScale consistency SCC has a genuinely odd spec anchor: its
    real rule-ID in the local spec text is `minScale-totalDigits` — a copy-paste
    bug in the spec itself — so messages cite it by its true title "minScale less
    than or equal to maxScale" to stay honest about the anchor while remaining
    findable. Grounded on the #157 thread (oracle GROUNDING,
    https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/157#issuecomment-5042533076).
  - #157 stayed a pure-`xsd`-package leaf (no `value` dependency): maxScale/
    minScale {value} are plain `xs:integer`, so their comparison needs no
    value-space machinery — concretely demonstrating the pure-leaf-feasible vs.
    needs-`value`-package precedent that may inform #217 (the general Part 2
    cos-st-restricts facet-value SCC family, its sibling in value-space territory).
- Spec: `maxScale-valid-restriction` §4.2.4; `minScale-valid-restriction` §4.3.4;
  the minScale≤maxScale consistency SCC (spec anchor `minScale-totalDigits`, cited
  by true title); `f-ms-fixed`/`f-mns-fixed` {fixed} inheritance.
- Ratchet: unchanged (arbiter confirmed via `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` — zero
  expectation-file changes; schema-construction leaf, no conformance lane driver
  exercises this path yet, consistent with the issue's own stated acceptance
  criteria). Figures carried from the arbiter, not recomputed.
- Arbiter verdict: reject-then-accept — ONE repair round total (round 1 of the
  2-round cap). REJECT on `4ab2f50`
  (https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/157#issuecomment-5042639670), ACCEPT on
  `eb77edc` after repair
  (https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/157#issuecomment-5042676306).
- Surprises: the rejection was a real correctness bug, not a false-positive —
  the first commit's `scaleValue` helper PANICKED on a malformed/non-integer
  scale-facet lexical value (e.g. `"abc"`), reachable through the PUBLIC
  `NewSimpleType`/`NewFacet` API (the arbiter reproduced it directly). Repaired
  by changing `scaleValue` to return `(int, error)` and charging a real
  `*xsderr.Error`, mirroring the identical parsing pattern already used by
  `value/facets.go`'s `facetInt` for the same maxScale/minScale {value} at
  instance-validation time; added a regression test proving the malformed literal
  now errors instead of panicking.
- Friction: none material beyond the one repair round; the panic path had a
  ready-made sibling pattern (`facetInt`) to mirror, so the fix was mechanical.
- Next / deferred (for the cartographer post-land pass to harvest — NOT filed
  this session): (a) `parser/produce.go`'s `facetKindOf` doesn't map the
  `maxScale`/`minScale` XML elements yet, so the producer silently DROPS them if
  encountered in a real schema document — a pre-existing gap, not caused or fixed
  here, but it leaves these new construction-time checks unreachable from the real
  parser pipeline until closed; candidate follow-up. (b) #217 (general Part 2
  cos-st-restricts facet-value SCC family) is #157's explicit sibling in value-
  space territory; the pure-leaf vs. needs-`value`-package precedent is now
  concretely demonstrated and worth folding into #217's design framing.

## 2026-07-22 — #98 — value: effectiveWhiteSpace gains a real union "not applicable" answer

- Shipped (commit `a5aceab`) the widening of `value.effectiveWhiteSpace(st)`
  from panic-on-absence to a total comma-ok `(ws whiteSpace, applicable bool)`
  answer, so a union-variety `*xsd.SimpleType` routes through
  `value.ValidateLexical`'s whiteSpace stage without panicking — whiteSpace is
  CATEGORICALLY not an applicable facet for `{variety}=union`
  (`cos-applicable-facets` §4.1.5). Three files under `value/`, +179/-35:
  `value/whitespace.go` (new signature + union-only relaxation branch +
  doc-block rewrite), `value/facets.go` (call-site comma-ok guard before
  `normalizeWhiteSpace`, `normalized`→`lexical` rename through the pattern
  stage and `Parse`, plus the `ValidateLexical` PRECONDITION + SCOPE(union)
  doc rewrite), `value/whitespace_test.go` (two-value signature updates + new
  union/list/end-to-end tests). No new exported identifiers — pure internal
  `value`-package hardening, matching the issue's "none expected" Surface.
- Exported surface: unchanged. `effectiveWhiteSpace`, `unionType`,
  `emptyBackend` all unexported; only framework-mandated `TestXxx` caps added.
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING on the #98 thread,
  https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/98#issuecomment-5047295822): union
  whiteSpace normalization is genuinely DEFERRED per active basic member
  (§4.3.6 `rf-whiteSpace` + `cvc-datatype-valid` §4.1.4 cl.2.3/cl.3) — different
  members can carry different whiteSpace facets, so there is NO single mode
  resolvable "once up front" for a union. This confirms `ValidateLexical`'s
  resolve-whiteSpace-once-up-front architecture is NOT the shape to validate a
  union end-to-end, and bounds #98's legitimate scope to the LEAF whiteSpace
  STAGE only — not a claim that the function now validates unions. Grounding
  also confirmed (Q2) every atomic primitive's `{facets}` always carries a
  whiteSpace entry (§3.16.7.4) and (Q3) list whiteSpace is immovably `collapse`
  (§4.3.6 / §4.3.6.1 `f-w-fixed`) materialized through the ordinary
  `EffectiveFacets()` scan — so list needs NO special-casing (confirmed, zero
  code change on the list path).
- Review chain — grounding → warden PRE-FLIGHT (accept-with-notes) → mason
  implementation → warden DIFF REVIEW (approve + own mutation test) → arbiter
  ACCEPT (own independent mutations). Three key design rulings, all honored
  with no deviations:
  - Warden pre-flight
    (https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/98#issuecomment-5047369990):
    widen to `(ws, applicable bool)` and REJECT a fourth enum sentinel
    (`notApplicableWS` would make an illegal state representable, T1). The
    relaxation must be STRICTLY union-only — an absent whiteSpace facet on
    atomic OR list must STILL panic (a genuine construction bug for those
    varieties, §3.16.7.4 / §4.3.6.1), gated via `st.Variety().(xsd.Union)` on
    the sealed `xsd.Variety` sum (the `lengthExemptPrimitive` `.(xsd.Atomic)`
    idiom). `normalizeWhiteSpace` stays pure/untouched; the skip lives at the
    `ValidateLexical` call site. Doc comment gets a rewritten PRECONDITION plus
    an explicit SCOPE(union) paragraph — the "primary safeguard" — stating this
    does NOT make the function a complete union validator. No hard union
    refusal anywhere.
  - Warden diff review
    (https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/98#issuecomment-5047496421):
    APPROVE, and personally RAN A MUTATION — reverted the `xsd.Union` gate to
    an unconditional `return 0, false` and confirmed both
    `TestEffectiveWhiteSpaceListNoFacetPanics` and
    `TestEffectiveWhiteSpaceNoFacetPanics` fail (`want panic, got none`),
    proving the existing suite genuinely catches the union-only regression, not
    just asserts it.
  - Arbiter ACCEPT on a third independent pass
    (https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/98#issuecomment-5047533931),
    running TWO distinct mutations: (A) retargeted the type-assert to
    `.(xsd.List)` — suite bit on BOTH sides (union test panics, list test
    stops panicking), so the variety is pinned exactly, not merely
    "non-atomic"; (B) made the call site ignore the `applicable` bool — the
    union end-to-end test bit, panicking in `normalizeWhiteSpace` on the zero
    ws, proving the comma-ok result is load-bearing. Plus an edge check:
    `xs:anySimpleType` returns a nil `Variety()`, `nil.(xsd.Union)` → false →
    falls through correctly to the construction-error panic.
- Spec: `rf-whiteSpace` §4.3.6 (whiteSpace does not apply directly to union;
  list is fixed collapse), `cos-applicable-facets` §4.1.5 (union's applicable
  facets are pattern/enumeration/assertions — whiteSpace conspicuously absent),
  `cvc-datatype-valid` §4.1.4 cl.2.3 + cl.3 note (per-active-basic-member
  deferral, out of scope here), §3.16.7.4 (atomic {facets} always carries a
  whiteSpace entry), §4.3.6.1 `f-w-fixed` (list materialized fixed collapse).
- Gate: `go build ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...` green;
  `golangci-lint run` 0 issues; `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance`
  read-only clean, no regression (all independently re-run by the arbiter).
- Ratchet: unchanged (arbiter verdict — leaf-only value-pipeline hardening; no
  conformance lane consumer is wired to this path yet, so no `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`
  run per the issue's own leaf-only scope). Figures carried from the arbiter,
  not recomputed.
- Dependency movement: #98 was unblocked by #46's landing earlier today
  (a non-atomic union/list `*xsd.SimpleType` is now constructible via the
  public `xsd` constructors and routable through `value.ValidateLexical`
  leaf-only), which broke the multi-week #98↔#75 circular tangle. Landing #98
  now flips #75 (`datatypes`-lane widening to list/union Facets cohorts) to
  `ready` — its other dependency (#46) is already closed.
- Surprises: none material. The warden pre-flight front-loaded every finding,
  so implementation landed with zero arbiter repair rounds — same payoff as the
  recent #171/#172/#173/#174 cycles.
- Friction: none material this cycle. Per open process issue #195 (mason

## 2026-07-22 — #75 — conformance: datatypes lane decides list-variety Facets cases

- Shipped (mason impl `1219a6f`, arbiter ratchet `013073d`) a generic
  list-variety Mapping in `package value` that lets the `datatypes` lane decide
  list-of-builtin + enumeration/length restriction cases end-to-end. Four
  files: new `value/list.go` (unexported `listMapping`/`listValue` implementing
  `dv_list` §4.1.4, `cvc-length-valid` §4.3.1.3 item-count length, and
  `cvc-enumeration-valid` §4.3.5.4 with itemwise §2.2.1/§2.2.2 identity/equality
  via each item's OWN capability — no reflect/DeepEqual), `value/facets.go`
  (a `governingMapping` change that checks `node.Variety().(xsd.List)` before
  the existing `Base()`-walk, so both the main parse and the enum/bound-facet
  widest-space resolution route list-variety types), `value/list_test.go`
  (table/targeted tests for resolution, Len, identity/equality across
  same/diff-length/diff-item/cross-type/capability-less, and end-to-end item
  error propagation), and `conformance/datatypes.go` (widened the
  `execLexicalCase` `!seeded` fallback into `execListCase`/`readListCohortCase`/
  `resolveListLeaf`/`buildListOwnFacets`).
- Exported surface: unchanged — `go doc ./value` shows no new `List*` surface,
  no package-level exports added in either file. Matches the issue's stated
  "Surface: none" (verified independently by the arbiter, T5).
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING on the #75 thread,
  https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/75#issuecomment-5049800466): the list
  Datatype-Valid algorithm is `dv_list` clause 2.2 of `cvc-datatype-valid`
  §4.1.4 (each space-delimited substring is Datatype Valid against the
  {item type definition}); length/minLength/maxLength count LIST ITEMS not
  characters (`dt-length` §4.3.1.3 / §4.1); enumeration identity+equality both
  require equal length + pairwise item comparison (§2.2.1/§2.2.2); pattern runs
  on the WHOLE un-split lexical BEFORE the variety-split (`dv_pattern` clause 1
  + `cvc-pattern-valid` §4.3.4.4 — already the shape of the existing
  `ValidateLexical` stage order, so no pipeline reordering); and §3.16.1
  `std-item_type_definition` guarantees {item type definition} is never itself a
  list, so `dv_list` bottoms out at one level (item is atomic or union-of-atomic
  — which is why the `governingMapping` list check on `node` alone is sound: no
  ancestor is list-variety if `node` isn't).
- Review chain — grounding → mason implementation → arbiter ACCEPT on first
  review, NO repair rounds (arbiter verdict
  https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/75#issuecomment-5050054802). No warden
  design pre-flight was triggered (Surface: none). The arbiter re-ran the full
  gate itself rather than trusting mason's self-report, re-derived the
  spec-soundness of the list-first `governingMapping` check, the
  Identical/Eq itemwise comparison, list error propagation, the
  `buildListOwnFacets` fixed `whiteSpace=collapse` seed (so `effectiveWhiteSpace`
  cannot hit its documented absent-facet panic — the #98 path this issue waited

## 2026-07-22 — #175 — conformance: activate the schema lane (first real movement)

- Shipped: the `schema` conformance lane goes live for the first time. It was
  a blanket `stubFail` placeholder over all ~15432 lines of
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt`; this issue swaps in a real
  `parser.Produce`-backed executor guarded by a shape-decidability gate.
  Commits on `wip/issue-175`: `84c5dbb` (mason initial `conformance/schema.go`
  + `schema_test.go` + one-line `defaultLanes()` exec swap in `runner.go`),
  `e24e9fc` (mason UTF-16 repair), `9624adc` (arbiter ratchet).
- Design — a shape-decidability gate, not a full schema validator. The lane
  CLAIMS a schemaTest case only when every top-level `<schema>` child is one
  of `annotation`/`simpleType`/`element`/`attribute`, with per-kind guards:
  `element` requires `type=` and no inline child (bare elements decline —
  they'd default to unseeded `xs:anyType`); `attribute` may be bare (defaults
  to seeded `xs:anySimpleType`) but forbids inline children; `simpleType`
  requires a restriction-only chain (recursing into anonymous inline bases)
  with no enumeration/assertion facets. Any other top-level child
  (complexType/group/attributeGroup/notation/include) declines the WHOLE case.
  That guard closes a false-accept gap: `parser.Produce` silently skips those
  top-level kinds, so without the gate a case could earn a "valid" verdict
  while hiding genuine invalidity in a skipped declaration.
- Grounded (oracle, sch-props-correct §3.17.6.1): the rule has exactly two
  clauses — clause 1 is a not-locally-decidable catch-all, clause 2 is the
  duplicate-name rejection, checked PER KIND and locally decidable. §3.17.2
  does not require a `<schema>` document root. The four src-*/src-resolve
  clause citations carried in from #174 (`parser/produce.go`) were all
  re-verified correct. The grounding also flagged the skipped-declaration
  false-accept risk that the shape gate was built to close.
- Review chain — one repair round (within the two-rejection cap). FIRST
  arbiter review REJECTED: 27 IBM fixtures (`ID_IDREF`,
  `constraintsOnAttribute`, `defaultAttributesApply`) are well-formed
  UTF-16-encoded XML that `parser.ReadDocument` currently misreads as invalid
  UTF-8. The original executor treated any ReadDocument error as a genuine
  schema-well-formedness failure, so these 27 "passed" for the WRONG reason —
  an encoding limitation, not a real violation. A ratchet landmine: they would
  flip pass→fail the moment UTF-16 decoding lands in the parser as an unrelated
  future change. Mason's repair (`e24e9fc`) changed the ReadDocument-error
  branch to unconditionally `Fail()`/decline rather than claim invalid,
  dropping all 27 from the claimed set. SECOND review APPROVED: repair touched
  only the flagged lines, all 27 landmine cases now decline (0 flipped across
  the three categories), full gate clean, ratcheted.
- Spec: sch-props-correct §3.17.6.1 (cl.2 per-kind duplicate-name, locally
  decidable; cl.1 catch-all declined), §3.17.2 (no `<schema>` root required),
  plus the #174-inherited src-*/src-resolve citations re-verified in
  `parser/produce.go`. No new library rule ID — a conformance-lane activation
  over the existing producer spine.
- Ratchet: `schema` lane Improved=2016, Regressed=0, Vanished=0, New=0,
  committed by the arbiter as `9624adc`. This is 27 below the pre-repair first
  submission (2043), exactly the UTF-16 landmine cases removed. Dominant claimed
  categories: NIST2004-01-14 restriction-only atomic simpleType schemas, then
  SType/MS-SimpleType/MS-Wildcards/MS-Annotations/ElemDecl/MS-Element/
  MS-Additional/ID_IDREF(remaining genuine)/constraintsOnAttribute(remaining
  genuine)/MS-DataTypes/MS-AttributeGroup/MS-Notations/MS-Attribute and smaller
  singleton sets. Figures copied from the arbiter verdict, not recomputed.
- Surprises: the 27 UTF-16 cases were an invisible-until-later trap — they
  looked like clean passes on today's parser and only the arbiter's
  "passed for the right reason?" scrutiny caught that a future UTF-16 decode
  landing would silently regress them. Declining on ReadDocument error is the
  conservative fix that keeps the lane honest.
- Friction: none material this cycle. Per open process issue #195 (mason
  recurring on unauthorized `docs/LOG` commits), this entry was again kept with
  the chronicler and written/committed on `wip/issue-175` before the land.
- Next: the shape gate is deliberately narrow — complexType/group/
  attributeGroup/notation/include top-level declarations remain UNCLAIMED (they
  decline the whole case), as do bare-element and enumeration/assertion
  simpleType cases. Widening any of these is a natural follow-up once the
  producer covers the corresponding component kinds; not filed by anyone this
  session. UTF-16 decoding in `parser.ReadDocument` is the other standing
  follow-up — when it lands, the 27 declined cases become genuinely decidable.

## 2026-07-23 — #176 — parser: complex-type + content-model producer

- Shipped: the schema producer now builds `xsd.ComplexType` with content
  models from the raw parse tree. Commits on `wip/issue-176`: `094570f`
  (mason implementation), `42a0ca5` (arbiter ratchet). New file
  `parser/produce_complex.go` plus changes to `parser/produce.go` construct
  `<complexType>` with inline `<sequence>`/`<choice>`/`<all>` model groups,
  particles with occurrence (min/maxOccurs), local element declarations,
  local attribute uses, and attribute wildcards (`<anyAttribute>`). Also
  seeds `xs:anyType` as a resolvable Complex Type Definition so bare
  `type=`-less elements/attributes finalize (rather than failing
  `src-resolve`) by defaulting to `xs:anyType`. The `schema` conformance
  lane selector (`conformance/schema.go`) was widened with new
  `complexTypeDecidable`/`contentDecidable`/`modelGroupDecidable` guards so
  the lane claims the complex-type / model-group / particle categories.
- Design — the producer widens strictly by decline-not-fail. Everything not
  yet produced is refused via non-`xsderr` "not yet produced" errors, each
  matched by a corresponding conformance-lane decidability guard so no false
  verdict can leak from a silently-skipped construct. Deliberately deferred
  this cycle: `<simpleContent>`, `<complexContent><extension>` (both need a
  resolved base — finalize-time work), `<group>`/`<attributeGroup>`
  references, inline anonymous `<simpleType>`/`<complexType>` on local
  elements/attributes, `<openContent>`, and the `<all>` occurrence-grammar
  restriction. Each is declined cleanly and the matching guard keeps the
  lane from claiming those cases.
- Review chain — arbiter ACCEPT, no repair rounds (verdict
  https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/176#issuecomment-5055307317). Full
  gate green (build/test/vet/lint/conformance), all independently re-run by
  the arbiter, not trusted from mason's self-report. No new exported surface
  in `parser`/`xsd`/`conformance`, so no warden design pre-flight was
  triggered (the issue's Surface note said none was required unless something
  new is exported — verified nothing was).
- Spec: complex-type / model-group / particle construction over the existing
  `parser.Produce` src-*/src-resolve spine; `xs:anyType` seeded as the
  ur-type Complex Type Definition so `type=`-less declarations resolve. No
  new library rule ID — a producer widening plus the matching conformance
  lane guards.
- Ratchet: `schema` lane strictly upward, committed by the arbiter as
  `42a0ca5`. 715 cases flip `fail`→`pass`, 0 regressed, 0 new, 0 vanished;
  only `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt` moved (datatypes and
  instance lanes untouched). Dominant claimed categories: MS-ModelGroups 224,
  MS-ComplexType 82, MS-Particles 79, MS-Element 75, ElemDecl 41,
  MS-Additional 33, Wild 30, wildcard 17, All 15, RestrictionOfComplexTypes
  12, CType 10, plus MGroup/Complex/allGroup/anyAttribute and smaller sets.
  Figures copied from the arbiter verdict, not recomputed.
- Surprises: none material. The decline-not-fail discipline (every deferred
  construct paired with a lane decidability guard) meant the large 715-case
  lane jump carried 0 regressions and 0 false accepts on first review.
- Friction: none material this cycle. Per open process issue #195 (mason
  recurring on unauthorized `docs/LOG` commits), this entry was again kept
  with the chronicler and written/committed on `wip/issue-176` before the
  land (same pattern as the recent #175/#98/#75 landings).
- Next: two items for the cartographer's post-land pass.
  (1) Follow-up-issue candidate: the arbiter's one non-blocking observation —
  `disallowedNames` silently drops an unresolvable `notQName` member
  (conservative fail-open, no verdict impact this cycle). Worth a tracking
  issue if not already filed; not filed by anyone this session.
  (2) The deferred scope (`<simpleContent>`, `<complexContent><extension>`,
  `<group>`/`<attributeGroup>` references, inline anonymous type children,
  `<openContent>`, `<all>` occurrence-grammar restriction) is what a future

## 2026-07-23 — #70 — xsd: Attribute Use component carries its own {value constraint}

- Shipped (commit `2ae0680`) the `{value constraint}` property on the Attribute
  Use component (§3.5.1 `vc_au`): `AttributeUse` gained `valueConstraint
  ValueConstraint` + `hasValueConstraint bool` fields (mirroring the established
  `AttributeDeclaration`/`ElementDeclaration` pattern), `NewAttributeUse` gained a
  `valueConstraint *ValueConstraint` param, a new `ValueConstraint() (ValueConstraint,
  bool)` accessor, and the `au-props-correct` clause-3 variety-agreement check wired
  into the constructor with an `xsderr`-wrapped, positioned error. All existing
  `NewAttributeUse` call sites (parser `produce_complex.go` + xsd test files) were
  threaded with `nil` — no live producer wires a real value constraint into an
  AttributeUse yet.
- Key scope decision (warden PRE-FLIGHT, before code): the originally-planned
  `EffectiveValueConstraint()` / §3.5.4 `key-evc` accessor was CUT and deferred to
  #173's downstream (#173 landed; the ref-resolution it provides is the enabler),
  because the `AttributeDeclarationRef` variant's declaration isn't resolved on this
  path and a `(ValueConstraint, bool)` return can't distinguish "unknown" from
  "absent" — answering now would be a silent wrong answer for that branch. Likewise
  `au-props-correct` clause 3's `{value}`-identity half was cut (xsd.ValueConstraint
  carries only `{lexical form}`, no `{value}`; identity needs a resolved type
  definition); only the variety-agreement half (a fixed declaration ⇒ the use's own
  VC, if present, must also be fixed) was kept, and only for the
  `LocalAttributeDeclaration` variant — the `AttributeDeclarationRef` variant is
  skipped, deferred to #173.
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #70): §3.5.1 `vc_au` property tableau, §3.5.4
  `key-evc` effective value constraint definition + `cvc-au`, §3.2.2.2
  `dcl.att.local`, and §3.5.6 `au-props-correct` clause 3 consistency rule.
- Warden diff review: approved, no findings. Doc comments were rewritten to state
  what's present vs. what's deferred to #173, so the leaf's promise matches its
  behaviour.
- Arbiter VERDICT: ACCEPT. Full gate green (build/test/vet/lint/conformance).
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run: unchanged — a leaf-shape addition with no producer wiring
  yet, so no conformance movement expected or observed.
- Spec: §3.5.1 `vc_au`, §3.5.6 `au-props-correct` cl.3 (variety-agreement half only).
  §3.5.4 `key-evc`, `au-props-correct` cl.2 (`cos-valid-simple-default`), cl.3
  `{value}`-identity half, and the whole check for the `AttributeDeclarationRef`
  variant satisfied-later / deferred, documented not checked.
- Ratchet: unchanged (arbiter-verified). Figures carried from the arbiter, not
  recomputed here.
- Surprises: none. The warden pre-flight narrowed the scope before any code, so
  implementation landed clean with no arbiter repair round.
- Next / Friction: follow-ups all route through #173's resolved-declaration path —
  `EffectiveValueConstraint()` / `key-evc` (§3.5.4), `au-props-correct` cl.2
  (`cos-valid-simple-default`), and cl.3's `{value}`-identity half + the

  default/fixed XML-representation work (presumably #174/#175 scope) — worth the
  post-land pass confirming an existing issue covers it, else file a follow-up.

## 2026-07-25 — #177 — parser: attribute-group + model-group definition producers + reference resolution

- Shipped: top-level `<attributeGroup>` and `<group>` definitions now become
  real `xsd` components, and `ref=` to either resolves inside complex types.
  Commits on `wip/issue-177`: `c918333` (mason implementation), `75c1f66`
  (mason repair round), `9b1b78f` (arbiter ratchet). Producer work covers the
  §3.6.2 / §3.7.2 XML representations plus the §3.6.6 `ag-props-correct`,
  §3.7.6 `mg-props-correct` / `mgd-props-correct` property checks; a dangling
  `ref=` is reported as §3.17.6.2 `src-resolve` rather than silently dropped.
  This picks up two of the constructs #176 deliberately declined
  (`<group>` / `<attributeGroup>` references), leaving that entry's other
  deferrals (`<simpleContent>`, `<complexContent><extension>`, inline
  anonymous type children, `<openContent>`, `<all>` occurrence grammar) still
  open.
- Resumed session: grounding, design, and most of the implementation happened
  in a prior session before this container restarted; this session picked the
  branch back up from its pushed tip and ran the judgment + repair + land
  cycle. The cross-session hand-off worked as WORKFLOW.md intends — nothing
  had to be reconstructed from memory, only from the branch and the issue
  thread.
- New exported surface (warden-approved twice — design pre-flight and again
  on the post-implementation diff): `xsd.IntersectNamespaceConstraint`, the
  §3.10.6.4 `cos-aw-intersect` binary primitive, and a
  `xsd.Wildcard.NamespaceConstraint()` getter. Both have a consumer in this
  diff (attribute-group wildcard folding needs the intersection), so they
  clear the STYLE 8 "export nothing without a consumer" bar.
- Design decision worth remembering — attribute-group reference *cycles are
  spec-legal* (§3.6.2.1), so `collectAttributeContent` traverses with a
  cycle-TOLERANT visited set: revisiting a group is a no-op, not an error.
  That is deliberately the opposite of the cycle-REJECTING pattern used
  elsewhere in the parser, where §3.7.6 `mg-props-correct` requires circular
  model groups to be rejected outright. The two look superficially alike and
  must not be unified; the asymmetry is the spec's, not an inconsistency.
- Review chain — arbiter round 1 REJECT. The conformance schema-lane
  allowlist had been widened far enough to admit schemas carrying
  `defaultAttributes` on `<schema>`, even though §3.4.2.4 default-attribute-
  group folding is not implemented: 7 cases passed for the WRONG REASON.
  Two minor findings alongside it — a stale `parser/doc.go` comment still
  claiming no traversal carries a `seen` set (falsified by the new
  cycle-tolerant visited set), and a duplicate case in
  `xsd/namespaceconstraint_intersect_test.go` whose two operands were not
  actually disjoint, so the empty-intersection boundary went untested.
- Repair round (`75c1f66`) fixed all three: `schemaShapeDecidable` now
  declines any schema whose root carries `defaultAttributes`, the `doc.go`
  comment was corrected to describe the visited-set traversal, and the test
  got a genuinely-disjoint operand pair. Arbiter round 2 ACCEPT (`9b1b78f`),
  verified independently — document resolution replicated across the W3C
  suite's `.testSet` files, the repaired test mutation-tested, and a sweep
  confirming no wrong-reason pass survived.
- Spec: §3.6.2 / §3.7.2 XML representations; §3.6.6 `ag-props-correct`;
  §3.7.6 `mg-props-correct`, `mgd-props-correct`; §3.17.6.2 `src-resolve`
  for dangling refs; §3.10.6.4 `cos-aw-intersect` via the new
  `IntersectNamespaceConstraint`. §3.4.2.4 default-attribute-group folding
  (`sch-props-correct` territory on `<schema defaultAttributes=…>`) is NOT
  implemented and is now explicitly declined by the lane guard.
- Ratchet: `schema` lane 2731/15432 → 2866/15432 (**+135**). `datatypes`
  lane unchanged at 1043/1074. No lane regressed. Figures copied from the
  arbiter's round-2 verdict, not recomputed here.
- Surprises: the round-1 rejection was a *lane* defect, not a library defect —
  the implementation was correct, but the conformance selector claimed more
  than the code could decide. Wrong-reason passes are the failure mode the
  decline-not-fail discipline exists to prevent, and it took an arbiter to
  catch it because the gate itself was green. Worth noting that a widened
  allowlist is exactly as dangerous as a downward expectations edit and is
  not caught by the same guardrail.
- Friction: none material beyond the container restart mid-issue, which cost
  a re-orientation pass but no work (the branch tip and issue thread carried
  everything). Per open process issue #195, this entry was again kept with
  the chronicler and written/committed on `wip/issue-177` before the land.
- Next: ONE unfiled follow-up, from the round-2 arbiter's advisory note (not
  blocking, not caused by this diff). `conformance/runner.go:164` declares
  `SchemaDoc docRef` as a NON-slice field, so a `schemaTest` with multiple
  `<ts:schemaDocument>` children silently keeps only the last one — the
  earlier documents are never loaded. This affects the override/redefine
  tests (12 of ~500 schemaTests) and is the same wrong-reason-pass risk

  conformance-runner correctness issue).

  ratchet). Twelve files, +1119/−155 against `7262b51`; the net-new producer
  code is `parser/produce_xpath.go` (+ its 412-line test file), with the
  dispatch sites in `parser/produce.go` / `parser/produce_complex.go` and the
  lane gate in `conformance/schema.go`.
- **The wildcard quarter of this issue was already done.** The issue's title
  and Acceptance section named four producers; the oracle grounding
  established that `produceWildcard` / `namespaceConstraint` / the
  disallowed-names handling shipped by #176/#177 already implement §3.10.2
  and `src-wildcard` (§3.10.3) for both `<any>` and `<anyAttribute>`. No
  wildcard code was touched. The `Wild`/`MS-Wildcards` ratchet movement the
  issue anticipated would only ever have arrived indirectly — from schemas
  becoming decidable for IDC/assertion/notation reasons — which is what the
  flip table below shows. The still-declined neighbour is `<openContent>` /
  `<defaultOpenContent>`, which is an {open content} *placement* gap, not a
  Wildcard mapping gap, and stays out of scope.
- **Reversal of a prior logged decision — stated here because the LOG is where
  a decision's reversal survives (#149's rule, applied in the opposite
  direction).** The 2026-07-07 entry for #5 (`parser/xmltree` package) records
  decision (5): *"Exported surface kept minimal (STYLE T5): no
  `InScopeNamespaces` enumerator — `LookupPrefix` covers QName-valued-content
  resolution."* **This issue REVERSES that call** and lands the enumerator, as
  `xmltree.StartElement.InScopePrefixes` (renamed from the `InScopeNamespaces`
  the earlier entry declined) plus the `xmltree.Namespace` value type and a
  `parser.Element` delegator. Append-only, so that entry stands as written;
  this entry carries the reversal. The reason it is a reversal and not a
  contradiction: the earlier call was made against *QName-valued-content
  resolution* (Datatypes §3.3.18), which is a point query — one prefix in, one
  URI out, exactly what `LookupPrefix` is. §3.13.2's `{namespace bindings}`
  property is a *set* — every in-scope prefixed binding of the `<selector>` /
  `<field>` / `<assert>` / `<assertion>` host element — and you cannot
  enumerate a set through a point query. No prior use case needed enumeration;
  this one structurally does. Both the warden pre-flight and both arbiter
  rounds confirmed the reversal is contained: the enumerator is built strictly
  *on top of* the existing `lookup` (candidates collected from the scope chain,
  then each resolved through `lookup` with `!ok` dropped), so it cannot drift
  from `LookupPrefix`'s answers, and it has exactly one real consumer
  (`buildXPathExpression`) in the same commit.
- Warden pre-flight on the PLANNED surface, before any code existed — verdict
  **revise**, five required modifications, all five landed in `683c772` and
  verified line-by-line by the arbiter: (1) exclude the empty/default prefix at
  the xmltree layer and name the method `InScopePrefixes`, since the default
  namespace is already `LookupPrefix("")`'s answer and returning it twice is
  one fact in two encodings (D3) *and* would let a caller build an illegal
  `xsd.NamespaceBinding{prefix: ""}` whose `{prefix}` is a Required xs:NCName
  (T1); (2) implement on top of `lookup`, not beside it; (3) handle the two
  reserved-prefix hazards — seed the candidate set with `xml` and dedupe so an
  explicit `xmlns:xml=` yields exactly one entry, and drop `xmlns` candidates
  so they never leak into an XPath static context; (4) unexported fields +
  `Prefix()`/`URI()` accessors on `Namespace`, matching the `Attribute`/`Name`
  peers (T4); (5) sort by prefix for determinism (D2), and fix
  `xsd/xpathexpression.go`'s doc comments, which falsely claimed document
  order — false the moment the producer's input is not already sorted.
- Scope widened beyond the brief, deliberately, in two places. **IDCs on local
  elements**: the brief offered top-level-only as an option, but §3.17.1
  sources `{identity-constraint definitions}` from every `<key>/<keyref>/
  <unique>` "anywhere within the [[children]]", so the producer wires both
  top-level and local `<element>`s and registers every IDC schema-wide —
  `sch-props-correct` clause 2 (§3.17.6.1) names that property in its
  same-expanded-name prohibition, so two identically-named IDCs under
  *different* elements genuinely collide. **The `ref=` form** of
  `<unique>/<key>/<keyref>` was not in the brief at all; mason declined it
  honestly rather than fabricating a rejection (it is legal per
  `src-identity-constraint` clause 1) and added `identityConstraintsDecidable`
  to the conformance gate in both reachable positions (`elementDecidable` and
  `modelGroupDecidable`) so the decline cannot become a wrong-reason pass. The
  test asserts the decline carries **no** xsderr rule — that is the property
  that keeps it a limitation rather than a fabricated verdict.
- Review chain — **arbiter round 1 REJECT**, one blocking finding, gate fully
  green (a defect no gate could catch). `parser/produce_xpath.go`'s
  `##defaultNamespace` branch in `xpathDefaultNamespace` treated "no default
  namespace in scope" as *present-with-empty-string* instead of ·absent· per
  §3.13.2 `{default namespace}` clause 1.2, and — the part that made it worth
  a round rather than a nit — the doc comment argued FOR the wrong reading
  (the empty string "is the no-namespace name, a legitimate present value,
  not an absence sentinel") instead of stating the rule. The sibling `##targetNamespace` branch gets the
  exactly-parallel clause-2.2 absence case right and has a test for it; clause
  1.2 had neither guard nor test, and the existing test's host declared
  `xmlns="urn:d"`, so it only ever exercised 1.1.
- Repair round (`10d0768`, exactly 2 files, +55/−4): the branch now keys off
  the URI *value* — `uri == "" ⟺ clause 1.2 applies` — because
  `LookupPrefix("")`'s `ok` is unconditionally true for the empty prefix and is
  therefore no signal at all (discarding it is correct, not a dropped error).
  A declared default namespace can never bind the empty namespace name, and
  `xmlns=""` is a Namespaces-in-XML-1.1 *undeclaration* that removes the
  in-scope entry rather than adding an empty one, so both clause-1.2 shapes
  collapse to the same observable. Doc comment rewritten to state the rule.
  New `TestProduceXPathDefaultNamespaceInScope` covers 3 cases including the
  `xmlns=""` sub-case; mason verified it by temporarily reverting the fix and
  confirming both absence cases fail on the old code.
- **Arbiter round 2 ACCEPT** (`4d4892e`), verified rather than taken on report:
  repair scope confirmed as claimed with nothing else drifted; §3.13.2 clause 1
  re-read directly and checked against the actual `scope.go` implementation
  (including that the `b.uri == "" && prefix != ""` guard deliberately does not
  fire for the empty prefix); the mutation test re-run independently; and all
  70 flipped conformance cases traced individually rather than inferred from
  folder names. Also confirmed in round 1: all five warden modifications, the
  local-element IDC collision reasoning, the T5 surface justification, and
  mason's correction of the grounding — no `GAP(xpath)` marker was needed for
  `{base URI}` because `parser/document.go` already does real `xml:base`
  resolution (`composeBaseURI`), which the grounding note had wrongly assumed
  absent. Adding the marker would have documented a gap that does not exist.
- Spec: §3.11.2 / §3.11.3 IDC XML mapping + `src-identity-constraint`;
  §3.13.2 XPath Expression property record (`{namespace bindings}`,
  `{default namespace}`, `{base URI}`) and `<assert>`; Datatypes §4.3.13 /
  §4.3.13.2 `xr-assertions` for `<assertion>`; §3.4.2.1 `dcl.ctd.common` for
  `{assertions}` assembly in document order; §3.14.2 / §3.14.6 Notation and
  `n-props-correct`; §3.17.1 + §3.17.6.1 `sch-props-correct` cl.2 for
  schema-wide IDC registration; §3.10.2 / §3.10.3 wildcards (confirmed already
  discharged, untouched).
- Ratchet: `schema` lane 2866/15432 → 2936/15432 (**+70**). Regressed 0, New 0,
  Vanished 0; total case count unchanged at 15432. `datatypes` unchanged at
  1043/1074, `instance` unchanged at 0/26426. Flip attribution from the
  arbiter's round-2 verdict: 37 `MS-Notations`/`Notation`, 27
  `assertion`/`Assert`, 1 `ID_IDREF/s3_3_4ii24` (two `<xs:assert>` in the
  schema), and 5 stragglers (`anyURI_a010`, `anyURI_a011`, `annotA018`,
  `errC004`, `addB004`) each individually confirmed to contain `<notation>`.
  The +70 belongs to `683c772`; the repair commit provably cannot move a lane
  (nothing outside tests consumes `XPathExpression.DefaultNamespace()` yet).
  Figures copied from the arbiter's round-2 verdict, not recomputed here.
- Surprises: (1) a quarter of the issue was already built — the grounding pass
  paid for itself by preventing a re-implementation of `produceWildcard`.
  (2) The grounding was *wrong* about `{base URI}`, and mason caught it by
  reading the code instead of trusting the note; a spec grounding's claims
  about *our* implementation state are the part that ages, not its claims
  about the spec. (3) The blocking finding was a doc comment defending a
  divergence — the code error was three lines, but the comment turned an
  oversight into an assertion, which is what earned the round.
- Friction: none material. The reversal bookkeeping (warden's pre-flight note
  and the arbiter's "for the record" note both pointing at this entry) worked
  exactly as intended — the requirement to state it here reached the
  chronicler through the issue thread with no orchestrator relay.
- Next: ONE unfiled follow-up. The **`ref=` form of `<unique>/<key>/<keyref>`**
  (referencing an existing named identity-constraint rather than defining one,
  legal per `src-identity-constraint` clause 1) was declined this round —
  mason's own scope note, backed by `identityConstraintsDecidable` in the lane
  gate so it cannot pass for the wrong reason. It is a natural next producer
  slice and is not filed by anyone yet; the cartographer's post-land pass
  should file it (`area/parser`, `kind/feature`). Also still open from #177's
  entry and unfiled: the `conformance/runner.go:164` single-`SchemaDoc`
  defect.
## 2026-07-25 — #179 — parser: multi-document assembly via `<xs:include>` + chameleon coercion

- Shipped: `parser.Parse(location string, opts ...Option) (*xsd.Schema, error)`
  — a schema is now assembled from a root document plus its transitive
  `<xs:include>` closure, with §F.1 chameleon coercion for included
  no-target-namespace documents. Single commit `846639d` on `wip/issue-179`
  (mason implementation; arbiter accepted round 1, no repair round). New
  `parser/parse.go` carries the four-phase assembly: discover (walk
  `<include>` children depth-first pre-order in document order, resolve
  against `BaseURI()`, dedup on the *resolved* location) → pre-scan (ONE
  assembly-wide symbol table over all documents) → produce (every document
  into one shared `xsd.SchemaBuilder` under the assembly's effective
  namespace) → finalize (once).
- New exported surface, exactly the pre-flight list and nothing more:
  `parser.Parse`, `parser.Option`, `parser.WithResolver`, `parser.WithBackend`,
  `parser.WithLogger`. `WithResolver(nil)`/`WithBackend(nil)` panic
  (programmer fault, matching `SchemaBuilder.AddType`); `WithLogger(nil)`
  installs `slog.DiscardHandler`; no resolver supplied → `loader.Dir(".")`.
  `parser.Produce` survives unchanged as the single-document entrypoint —
  now a thin wrapper sharing the per-document producer rather than
  duplicating it — because `conformance/schema.go:229` is still its caller.
- Design decision to remember — **no `xsd.SchemaSet`, and not as a
  compromise**. Warden's pre-flight was `revise`, and its central finding is
  that §3.17.2 already says a document with `<import>` children "corresponds
  to a schema with components with more than one {target namespace}": the
  §3.17.1 Schema component IS the multi-namespace container, and `xsd.Schema`
  already implements that tableau with no `targetNamespace` field anywhere.
  A `SchemaSet` would have been a second Go name for the same component
  (STYLE T4, PRINCIPLES 4). This also settles #182's shape in advance —
  `<xs:import>` needs *assembly-phase* per-document import scope, which is
  unexported state, not a new public container. Consequently
  `parser/doc.go`'s planned-contract block (plus the same stale mentions in
  `validate/doc.go:43` and `codegen/doc.go:35`) was corrected from
  `*xsd.SchemaSet` to `*xsd.Schema` in this commit, and `ParseMultiple` was
  retired-with-reason as consumer-less (T5/T6).
- Chameleon coercion is TWO tasks, and shipping one is shipping nothing:
  (a) mint the included document's components under the including namespace,
  (b) rewrite every *unqualified QName reference inside* that document —
  §4.2.3 clause 3.2.1 note (b), plus its closing paragraph extending the same
  conversion to references still unresolved at finalize. `chameleon()` is a
  derived predicate (`no targetNamespace attribute && tns != ""` — literally
  clause 2.3), never a stored field, and part (b) goes through the single
  `resolveQName` chokepoint via `unqualifiedRefNS()`.
- Mason found and fixed a latent dead branch that was blocking the whole
  feature: `xmltree`'s `scope.lookup("")` returns `("", true)` both when no
  default namespace is bound and when `xmlns=""` is explicit, so
  `resolveQName`'s empty-prefix `if !ok` guard was unreachable and coercion
  could never fire. Changed to `if uri == ""`; non-chameleon behaviour is
  bit-identical in both directions. The arbiter mutation-verified this:
  reverting to `if !ok` fails `TestParseChameleonCoercion` and
  `TestParseChameleonTransitive`, so the fix is load-bearing, not cosmetic.
- Spec-reading call, confirmed by the arbiter to be stronger than mason
  claimed: `src-include` clause 2 is compared against the *assembly's*
  effective namespace, not the raw `targetNamespace` of the document
  physically holding the `<include>`. This looked like the more permissive
  of two readings; it is not a second reading at all. §F.1 task 1 literally
  *adds* a `targetNamespace` attribute to the transformed document, and
  clause 3.2.1 requires D2′ (transformed), not D2, to correspond to a
  conforming schema — so "compare against the assembly namespace" and
  "compare per-hop against the transformed parent" are the same rule. A
  document with its own explicit differing `targetNamespace` is rejected at
  the earlier hop under both. `TestParseChameleonIncludesCoercedNamespace`
  pins it.
- The load-once index is keyed on resolved location alone and is documented
  as **document identity (§4.2.3), not a cycle guard** — include cycles are
  spec-legal, and PRINCIPLES 5 forbids cycle checks. It is what makes a
  repeated include, a diamond, and a cycle each contribute their components
  once without tripping `sch-props-correct` clause 2. Relatedly,
  `builtin.Seed`/`xs:anyType` now seed exactly once per assembly (via the new
  unexported `symbols` struct, so "once" is enforced by construction); the
  naive per-document sketch would have double-seeded and failed
  `sch-props-correct` on any two-document schema.
- Relative `schemaLocation` bases deliberately avoid `net/url`:
  `url.ResolveReference` roots a relative base at `/`, so
  `sub/deep/a.xsd` + `../c.xsd` becomes `/sub/c.xsd`. The code uses
  `path.Join(path.Dir(base), location)` instead. The arbiter tested the claim
  rather than accepting it and found the *justification* overstated — the
  real breakage is exact-key resolvers (`loader.Map`, `FS`) missing the key,
  not `loader.Dir` rejecting a root escape, which it would not. Advisory
  only; correct a comment next time the file is touched.
- Spec: `src-include` (§4.2.3, clauses 1, 2.1/2.2/2.3/2.4, 3.1.2, 3.2),
  `chameleon-xslt` (§F.1, both tasks), `sch-props-correct` (§3.17.6.1)
  clause 2, `src-resolve` (§3.17.6.2) clause 4.1.1. Missing `schemaLocation`
  is a plain `fmt.Errorf`, NOT `xsderr` — no SCC governs a missing required
  attribute (that is schema-for-schemas grammar), following the existing
  `<group>`-without-`ref` precedent at `produce_complex.go:263`.
  `<xs:import>`, `<xs:redefine>` and `<xs:override>` remain explicitly out of
  scope (#182 / #183).
- Ratchet: **unchanged, deliberately.** Figures are the arbiter's, not
  recomputed here. The arbiter ran `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` and it was a genuine
  no-op — zero changes to `conformance/testdata/expectations/`, so neither a
  regression nor an unharvested improvement. The reason is structural, not a
  quality signal: `conformance/schema.go` still runs
  `os.Open` → `ReadDocument` → `parser.Produce` on single documents and
  `schemaShapeDecidable` declines any top-level `<include>`, so no W3C
  include or chameleon case is decidable *regardless of how correct `Parse`
  is*. The issue's "moves the schema lane" acceptance criterion is therefore
  unmet, and was accepted as a legitimate scope split: widening the lane is a
  `conformance/` change (per-document `loader.Dir` rooting + selector
  rewrite), and bundling it here would put harness rewiring under a
  parser-scoped review. The arbiter conditioned the accept on the follow-up
  existing, checked (#238 is adjacent but distinct), and filed **#242**.
- Surprises: the feature was gated on a *dead branch* in code nobody
  suspected — `resolveQName` looked correct and its empty-prefix guard had
  simply never been reachable. Green tests could not have found it, because
  nothing yet needed the branch it guarded. Also worth flagging: the two
  readings of `src-include` clause 2 that mason asked the arbiter to
  adjudicate turned out to be provably the same rule once §F.1's "task 1
  adds a real attribute" step is taken literally — a reminder that the
  chameleon transform is defined as an actual document rewrite, not as a
  resolution-time fiction.
- Friction: none material. One warden `revise` round pre-flight, one arbiter
  round, no repair round.
- Next: #242 (arbiter-filed) widens the `schema`-lane conformance harness
  onto `Parse` so includes and chameleon cases become decidable and the lane
  can actually move; it also carries warden's outstanding note to migrate off
  `Produce` and unexport it once the lane no longer needs it. Two advisory
  notes carried on that issue rather than left loose: the imprecise
  `resolveSchemaLocation` doc comment, and the forward-looking one that §F.1's
  normative stylesheet also rewrites `substitutionGroup`,
  `xs:schema/@defaultAttributes`, `xs:keyref/@refer`, `xs:list/@itemType` and
  `memberTypes` — none producible today (grepped, no live gap), but
  `unqualifiedRefNS` must be revisited as each construct lands or chameleon
  documents will be silently under-rewritten. The prior entry's unfiled
  follow-up (`conformance/runner.go`'s non-slice `SchemaDoc` dropping all but
  the last `<ts:schemaDocument>`) is now filed as **#238**; ledger clean.

## 2026-07-25 — #51 — xsd: `##defined`/`##definedSibling` {disallowed names} resolved against the declaration graph (cvc-wildcard cl.2-3)

- Shipped in one commit, `34ead77` on `wip/issue-51` (19 files, +1261/−97
  against `cc0397b`; arbiter **ACCEPT on round 1**, no repair round). Three
  pieces: (a) *representation* — `xsd.DisallowedNameKeyword` (sealed
  `defined`/`sibling`, deduped and range-checked at construction) beside
  `disallowedNames []QName` on `NamespaceConstraint`, with a fifth positional
  parameter on `NewNamespaceConstraint`; (b) *resolution* — a new unexported
  `*Schema` layer in `xsd/wildcardadmit.go` (285 lines + a 365-line test file)
  answering `cvc-wildcard` cl.2.1/2.2 by top-level element/attribute lookup and
  cl.3 by a content-model walk covering *directly* / *indirectly* (nested
  groups, `<group ref>`, `<element ref>`) / *implicitly* (substitution groups)
  contained declarations; (c) *wiring* — the parser stops dropping the `##`
  tokens, `cos-aw-intersect` cl.3 now actually propagates `defined`, and
  `w-props-correct` cl.5 is enforced by one unexported helper.
- **The representation/resolution split is what dissolved the T8 problem.** The
  issue's Surface section demanded the keyword representation be justified
  against a real consumer, and cl.2-3 are *validation-time* rules with no
  instance validator in the repo yet. Mason's pre-flight found the
  representation half already had two live false-accepts today —
  `produce_complex.go` silently dropped the tokens, so `<any
  notQName="##defined"/>` produced a wildcard that *admitted* globally declared
  elements, and `IntersectNamespaceConstraint` (called for real by
  `combineAttributeWildcards`) lost `defined` on every fold. So: implement both
  halves in full, export only the representation half, keep
  `allowsElementWildcardName` / `allowsAttributeWildcardName` unexported until
  M5 supplies the caller. Nothing deferred, no GAP restated more narrowly — only
  the accessor question. Warden approved this as ruling 1 and reversed #34's
  make-it-unrepresentable ruling explicitly (ruling 4: "#34's ruling is
  correctly reversed"), the original objection — a keyword stored and ignored by
  a bool-returning `AllowsName` — having been the thing this commit removes.
- **GAP retirement is deliberately asymmetric, and that asymmetry is the
  warden's must-fix.** `NewNamespaceConstraint`'s marker is retired
  *substantively* (the keywords are now carried). `NamespaceConstraint.AllowsName`'s
  is retired *by restatement*: mason's §1 finding was that the marker had always
  been misstated, since `cvc-wildcard-name` cl.2 tests "C.{disallowed names}
  does not contain E" where E is an *expanded name*, and keywords are not
  expanded names — §3.10.4.2 was complete w.r.t. its own rule from the start.
  But `Wildcard.AllowsName` **KEEPS** a `GAP(xsd)`, warden rejecting its
  retirement outright: it is exported, and after #51 it becomes a *silently
  partial* cvc-wildcard — clauses 2-3 live but unreachable from outside package
  `xsd` — i.e. an untracked false-accept for any external caller. The
  nonstandard `// GAP:` (no parens, invisible to `grep -rn "GAP("`) in
  `produce_complex.go` was deleted; the adjacent unresolvable-literal-QName
  prefix drop is #232 and stayed untouched by design.
- Warden pre-flight verdict **revise**, 9 rulings + must-fixes, all honored and
  verified line-by-line by the arbiter. Beyond the two above: cl.5 collapsed
  from three call sites to one unexported helper called from `NewComplexType` +
  `NewAttributeGroupDefinition` only — **never** `NewOpenContent`, since
  open-content wildcards are element wildcards and legitimately carry `sibling`;
  the sibling walk reads only `ComplexType.ContentType()` and never `{base type
  definition}` (D4 — `xs:anyType` is its own base, so a base-chain walk
  reintroduces the self-loop); `String()` returns the §3.10.1 *component*
  keywords `defined`/`sibling`, with `##definedSibling` mapping to `sibling`
  ALONE and not `{defined, sibling}`; keyword slice deduped at construction (a
  set carrying "defined defined" is an illegal state); `cos-aw-intersect` cl.3
  implemented in the same commit rather than merely re-documented; no
  `WildcardKind` property (D3: state derivable from position); no
  `DisallowedNameKeywords()` accessor (all readers in-package).
- **`cos-aw-intersect` cl.3 is a UNION and must not be transposed.** Oracle
  grounding #2: intersection-of-wildcards keeps `defined` if it is a member of
  **either** operand's {disallowed names}; `cos-aw-union` (§3.10.6.3) uses
  **both**. Opposite quantifiers on adjacent rules — the exact shape of mistake
  that reads as a typo in review. `sibling` is not propagated and cannot appear
  on an attribute wildcard at all (cl.5), stated in the doc with no defensive
  branch.
- **Three self-reported deviations from the approved design, all scrutinized by
  the arbiter and all confirmed legitimate.** Recorded here in full because a
  deviation that survives review is the kind of thing a later session will
  otherwise re-litigate:
  1. *Upward substitution-group affiliation walk instead of the prescribed
     document-order scan of `s.elements`.* Confirmed **equivalent and stronger**.
     The scan was prescribed to satisfy D2 (never let map order reach output);
     the upward walk ranges no map at all — all three `range`s in
     `wildcardadmit.go` are over slices, and `s.Element(...)` /
     `s.modelGroupIndex[...]` are point lookups — so "D2 is satisfied more
     strongly than by the prescribed scan, because there is no scan to order."
     It is also a direct transcription of `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec` cl.2.2, and
     drops nothing (`elementIndex` is 1:1 with `s.elements` per §3.17.1;
     diamonds are traversed; termination licensed by
     `checkSubstitutionGroupsAcyclic`). Mutation-proved.
  2. *`cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec` cl.2.1 checked only on the head H, not on
     intermediate chain members.* Confirmed **spec-correct — the oracle's own
     grounding gloss was wrong.** Grounding #2 had parenthesized "(or any
     intermediate head in the chain)"; the arbiter re-read the rule and found
     cl.2.1 scoped to H alone, with "intermediate" occurring exactly once in the
     whole rule — inside cl.2.3, qualifying *type definitions in the type
     derivation*, not element declarations on the affiliation chain. Corroborated
     by `cvc-accept` cl.2.3.2 (`c-psg`). "Not a simplification — a correction."
     Mason preferring the grepped spec over the paraphrase is exactly what
     CLAUDE.md's ground-truth rule asks for; the standing lesson (same as #178's)
     is that a grounding's *paraphrase* ages faster than the clause it quotes.
  3. *`cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec` cl.2.3 left unimplemented.* Confirmed
     **fail-closed and honestly documented**. It needs a type-derivation walk
     over resolved type definitions, which anonymous types (zero `{type
     definition}` QName) cannot supply here. Omitting a *blocking* clause
     over-approximates membership ⇒ superset substitution group ⇒ more names
     disallowed ⇒ false-**reject**, which is the safe side of #51's "never a
     false-accept" criterion. The `PARTIAL (fail-closed, not fail-open)` block at
     `xsd/wildcardadmit.go:242-252` names the clause, the blocker, and the
     direction, so STYLE P3 — whose marker requirement is scoped to *fail-open*
     fallbacks — is not violated.
- Testing: 12 mutations applied and reverted, **all 12 caught**.
  `TestSiblingIsNotMemoizedPerWildcard` guards the one optimization the
  grounding forbids — one `Wildcard` reachable from two complex types, `foo`
  disallowed in one and admitted in the other, because *T* is per-complex-type
  and the sibling set is per-(complex type, occurrence), never a property of the
  Wildcard. Exported-surface delta is exactly the five approved items; all 11
  new `*Schema` methods are unexported.
- Spec: `cvc-wildcard` (§3.10.4.1 cl.1-3) — a rule SEPARATE from
  `cvc-wildcard-name` (§3.10.4.2), never to be conflated when citing via
  `xsderr`; `cvc-resolve-instance` (§3.17.6.3); `w-props-correct` (§3.10.6.1
  cl.1, cl.5); `cos-aw-intersect` (§3.10.6.4 cl.3); `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec`
  (§3.3.6.3 cl.2.1-2.2); `key-en-match` (plain expanded-name equality for
  ·match·, NOT the substitution-aware `key-e-d-match` — substitution awareness
  enters through *implicitly contains* expanding the candidate set, not through
  the match relation); `cvc-datatype-valid` for the `##` token and
  `xs:qnameListA` rejections.
- Ratchet: **unchanged**. The arbiter ran `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` — no lane moved, no
  regression, working tree clean afterward, no expectations file rewritten; the
  commit's `Ratchet: unchanged` is honest. Expected: no caller consumes the
  resolution half until M5. Gate green on `34ead77` (`golangci-lint run` → 0
  issues). Figures copied from the arbiter's verdict, not recomputed here.
- Surprises: (1) the oracle was wrong in a *parenthetical*, not in a citation —
  the quoted rule text two lines above it contradicted the gloss. Groundings
  earn their keep on what they quote; what they summarize is commentary. (2) The
  design's D2-motivated prescription (document-order scan) was satisfiable by
  *removing* the scan entirely, which is a reminder that a ruling's rationale,
  not its letter, is what a deviation has to preserve. (3) `w-props-correct`
  cl.5 already guarantees attribute wildcards never carry `sibling`, so the
  attribute-`sibling` path is unreachable by construction and was deliberately
  NOT defensively re-checked.
- Friction: **the `PARTIAL` marker convention does not exist.** `PARTIAL` is not
  a greppable debt token in this repo — `grep PARTIAL` returns ~19 unrelated
  "PARTIAL order" hits in `value/` and `builtin/` — so the cl.2.3 gap block is
  honest prose that no debt sweep will ever find. That is a process gap, not a
  mason error, and it is the reason the follow-up below must be filed rather
  than trusted to a future grep.
- Next: **TWO items, both flagged non-blocking by the arbiter and both needing a
  tracking issue filed in the post-land pass — recording them here so they
  survive, per the convention that got #195 filed off a LOG footnote.** (1) The
  `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec` **cl.2.3** gap at `xsd/wildcardadmit.go:242-252`
  has **no owning issue** — searched, and #52 owns the
  `cos-aw-union`/`cos-ns-subset` keyword clauses, nothing owns this
  (`area/xsd`, `kind/gap`). (2) The new `GAP(xsd)` at `xsd/wildcard.go:107`
  names **#51 as its retirement owner, but #51 closes with this commit** — it
  must be repointed at the M5 follow-up (an exported cvc-wildcard-complete entry
  point over the declaration graph). Both are bookkeeping, not correctness.
  Also carried forward: an observation, no action — `inSubstitutionGroupOf` /
  `affiliationChainReaches` carry no memo, so a wide multi-head affiliation DAG
  is worst-case exponential in paths; D4 forbids a cache without a measured hot
  path and no caller exists, so re-measure at the M5 wiring, not before.
