## 2026-07-25 — #63 — xsd: c-props-correct clause 2 — keyref {fields} cardinality must match the resolved {referenced key}

- Shipped (commit `29f4a3e`) the last open clause of `c-props-correct`
  (§3.11.6.1): `resolveKeyref` in `xsd/resolve.go` now rejects a keyref whose
  `{fields}` cardinality differs from that of its resolved `{referenced key}`.
  The production change is **4 lines** — a length comparison against the
  `target` local the existing `s.idcIndex` lookup already produced — charged
  `ruleICProps` / `c-props-correct` through `xsderr` with position. Plus 4 doc
  comments retired (`ruleICProps` const, `ReferencedKeyName`, `resolveKeyref`,
  `resolve()`'s Phase A bullet) and 3 new tests in `xsd/resolve_test.go`
  (cardinality mismatch in both directions, a matching pass case, and
  double-visit safety across the top-level and element-nested IDC reachability
  paths).
- Scope going in was already narrowed by a 2026-07-25 issue comment after
  #173/#178 landed: only (1) clause 2 and (2) settling whether the resolved
  component needs storing were left.
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #63): `c-props-correct` §3.11.6.1 quoted
  verbatim — it has **two clauses only** — plus the `{referenced key}`
  property-record entry (§3.11.1/§3.11.2). The decisive finding is that the
  property-record formalism is **representation-agnostic** (§2 key-null, §2.2
  "abstract data model"): the spec nowhere mandates storing a resolved pointer,
  so "resolve" here means "check", not "materialize".
- Warden PRE-FLIGHT (before code): approved a **zero-new-exported-surface**
  design — no field on `IdentityConstraint`, no `Schema.IdentityConstraint(name)`
  accessor (STYLE T5/8). Required 4 adjustments, all taken: read the unexported
  `.fields` rather than `Fields()` (whose defensive copy would be allocated only
  to be discarded in-package), order the clause-2 check **after** the existing
  clause-1 category check so a doubly-wrong target reports one deterministic
  first failure (STYLE D1), update **4** doc-comment sites rather than the 2
  originally planned, and give the new error a distinctly-shaped message.
- Arbiter VERDICT: ACCEPT, no repair round. Full gate green. The exported
  surface was checked by **diffing `go doc -all ./xsd`** — the only delta is
  doc-comment prose, confirming the design claim rather than trusting it. The
  3 new tests were also **copied onto unmodified main and confirmed failing**,
  so they are real regression coverage, not vacuous.
- Spec: `c-props-correct` clause 2 (Structures §3.11.6.1). Clause 1's LOCAL
  presence-iff-keyref half stays in `NewIdentityConstraint`; clause 1's
  resolvability + category half and clause 2's cardinality now both live in
  finalize. **`c-props-correct` is complete with this commit** — no clause of
  this rule remains deferred.
- Ratchet: **unchanged — measured, not assumed.** The arbiter initialized the
  submodule and ran `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` for real, both before (a baseline on main,
  which wrote nothing) and after: zero movement on every lane — datatypes
  1043/1074, schema 2936/15432, instance 0/26426, xpath/json/ber empty. Mason's
  "Ratchet: unchanged" commit line was therefore **verified correct rather than
  taken on faith**, and needed no correction. Figures copied from the arbiter,
  not recomputed here.
- Surprises: the reason the ratchet cannot move is structural and worth naming —
  the schema lane's ~919 IDC-shaped cases sit at **2/919 passing** because they
  fail *upstream* of finalize, so a finalize-phase tightening has nothing to
  reach yet. A correctness win with a provably flat lane is the expected shape
  here, not a disappointment; #242's harness rerouting is what unblocks these
  cases.
- Friction: none material. The warden pre-flight again did the work before any
  code was written (cf. #70), so implementation landed clean on the first pass —
  4 production lines from a session that spent its effort on grounding and design
  instead of repair rounds.
- Next: no follow-up issue owed by this change — the "store/expose the resolved
  component" question was **settled as NO** on spec grounds (representation
  independence), not deferred, so nothing needs tracking. #63 was the last open
  M4 leaf follow-up; the develop loop returns to the PLAN.md priority order,
  **#242 first**.

## 2026-07-25 — #242 — conformance: wire the schema lane onto parser.Parse; `<xs:include>` / chameleon cases are now decided instead of declined

- Shipped (commits `c647476` implementation, `b40b683` ratchet) the rerouting of
  the `schema` conformance lane off single-document
  `parser.ReadDocument`+`parser.Produce` and onto multi-document `parser.Parse`,
  widening `schemaShapeDecidable` to admit a top-level `<xs:include>`.
  `<import>`/`<redefine>`/`<override>` stay DECLINED (#182/#183 unlanded — the
  assembly does not follow them, so a verdict would be vacuous). New file
  `conformance/schema_closure.go` (232 lines), 7 new tests, and a substantive
  **rewrite** (not an append) of the ~200-line doc comment at the top of
  `conformance/schema.go` to describe the new closure-decidability model.
- **This was PLAN.md's explicitly named top-priority ready issue** — "the single
  highest-conformance-value issue in the queue… top of the frontier" — because
  the lane had been static at 2936/15432 since #178 for *structural* reasons
  (the harness declined every top-level `<include>`), and neither #179 nor #51
  moved it. **That framing is now discharged**: the lane moved, and PLAN.md's
  "top of the frontier" pointer at #242 is spent.
- Ratchet: **schema 2936 → 2951 pass (+15)**, zero regressions on the schema
  lane or any other lane (datatypes / instance / xpath / json / ber byte-identical
  before and after). Measured by the arbiter with a real `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run;
  figures copied from the arbiter, not recomputed here. The 15 flipped cases,
  named: MS-Schema (schB5, schB8), MS-Particles (particlesIc005–007),
  MS-SimpleType (stA003, stC007), MS-AttributeGroup (attgC004, attgC015),
  MS-Additional (addA001, addA002 — the include-the-same-schema-twice diamond),
  MS-DataTypes (anyURI_a006_1341), MS-ComplexType (ctA029), MS-Annotations
  (annotA014).
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #242): `src-include` §4.2.3 clauses 1,
  2.1–2.4, 3.1.2, 3.2 quoted verbatim. Four findings decided the design.
  (1) src-include imposes **no shape constraint** on an included document — only
  existence and targetNamespace agreement — so shape *decidability* is the
  harness's own orthogonal concern, not something the spec hands us.
  (2) Chameleon inclusion (§F.1) is **purely a namespace-level transform** (adds
  targetNamespace, rewrites unqualified QNames); it does not restructure
  elements, so running the existing per-document `schemaShapeDecidable` on the
  RAW document is correct for a chameleon-transformed document too.
  (3) Document-level dedup by resolved location and `<include>`-cycle legality
  are **spec-mandated** (§4.2.3), not engineering safety nets.
  (4) §4.3.2 clause 4 resolves `schemaLocation` against the **owner element's**
  base URI (xml:base-aware), not the document URI.
- Design decision, and the reason for a whole new file: `parser.Parse`'s
  `<include>`-closure document discovery is **unexported**, so the harness cannot
  ask "which documents did Parse actually read?". Without an answer, the exact
  false-accept `schemaShapeDecidable` was built to close — an undecidable shape
  silently skipped by the producer — reappears at every *non-root* document in a
  multi-document assembly. So the harness performs its **own** closure walk and
  gates every document it discovers before calling Parse. That walk carries a
  **verbatim duplicate** of the parser's unexported `resolveSchemaLocation`;
  exporting it instead would have added public surface with zero non-test
  consumer (STYLE 8).
- Bug found and fixed during implementation, worth naming because it was silent:
  the harness's own root-document read must use the **same uri string** that
  `parser.Parse` uses internally for its root read — `filepath.Base(c.doc)`, not
  the full test-document path — or `<include>` resolution inside the walk targets
  the wrong document while appearing to work.
- No warden pre-flight: the issue's Surface section says "none", and the change
  adds no exported surface.
- Arbiter VERDICT: ACCEPT. The base-URI-consistency fix was verified by
  **mutation, not by reading** — each half reverted in turn and the suite rerun
  (7 test failures with `c.doc` in place of `filepath.Base(c.doc)`; 2 assertion
  failures with `el.BaseURI()` zeroed). The duplicated `resolveSchemaLocation`
  was diffed against the original and confirmed **byte-identical**. Three design
  points judged: (a) the walk deliberately does **not** mirror Parse's
  targetNamespace-agreement check, so it recurses into a *superset* of what Parse
  reads — strictly conservative, since `assembly.include` RETURNS on a clause-2
  mismatch while the walk recurses past it, hence walk-set ⊇ Parse-set always;
  accepted as-is. (b) `loader.Dir`'s `..`-traversal defense is an engineering-only
  confinement that, at the resolver interface, is **indistinguishable** from a
  genuine src-include clause 2.4 "not an error, skip" — a latent false-accept
  path. (c) The walk asks the resolver under the root's targetNamespace
  (mirroring `parser.assembly.fetch`) rather than the no-namespace sentinel —
  confirmed consistent with Parse's own behavior.
- **Follow-up #257 was filed as an explicit PRECONDITION of the ACCEPT, not as a
  routine post-land harvest item** — the arbiter required it *during* landing
  before the verdict stood, and the orchestrating session filed it (`ready`)
  before the branch landed. It tracks design point (b): the latent false-accept
  is verified empty against the currently-pinned W3C submodule (114 include
  hints, **zero** containing `..`) but is not structurally guaranteed against a
  future submodule bump or the #182 import lane. Recording the provenance here so
  a later retro or post-land pass does not have to re-derive it as a missing
  follow-up.
- Two non-blocking arbiter findings, neither filed: a T4 note that the duplicated
  `resolveSchemaLocation` has **no mechanical sync guard** (a shared-package
  extraction is the eventual fix), and a doc-completeness nit — step 4's list of
  eliminated error types omits the missing-`schemaLocation` grammar fault, which
  the walk does nonetheless correctly decline.
- Spec: `src-include` (Structures §4.2.3, clauses 1 / 2.1–2.4 / 3.1.2 / 3.2),
  chameleon inclusion §F.1 / `c-chamvalidi`, §4.3.2 clause 4.
- Surprises: the highest-conformance-value change of the month touched **no
  producer code at all** — 15 W3C cases were pinned entirely by *how cases are
  fed to the library*, exactly as the 2026-07-25 backlog entry predicted. Also
  notable: the new tests include a deliberate **decoy** — a decidable
  `grandchild.xsd` at the resolver root beside an undecidable one at the real
  nested path — so a mis-resolving walk declines for the WRONG reason rather than
  "passing" by finding nothing.
- Friction: none material. Grounding answered the shape question before any code
  was written, and the ACCEPT came without a repair round.
- Next: the develop loop continues down the PLAN.md priority order — the
  remaining M4 sub-slices **#180, #181, #182, #183**; #182 (import lane) is now
  the natural successor since it both widens the same allowlist and is the
  scenario under which #257's latent path could become live. #257 is `ready` and
  tracked. PLAN.md's `schema`-lane paragraph now carries a stale figure (2936)
  and a spent "#242 is the highest-value issue" pointer — worth correcting at the
  next backlog.

## 2026-07-25 — #180 — xsd: Unique Particle Attribution (cos-nonambig) + Element Declarations Consistent (cos-element-consistent) enforced at finalize

- Shipped (commits `b7de442` implementation, `240ac19` ratchet) finalize-phase
  enforcement of Structures §3.8.6.4 and §3.8.6.3 over already-produced
  `xsd.ComplexType` content models, as a new **Phase C** in `Schema.resolve()`.
  Three new files — `xsd/substitutiongroup.go`, `xsd/particleattribution.go`,
  `xsd/elementconsistent.go` — plus edits to `xsd/resolve.go` and
  `xsd/wildcardadmit.go`, and 30 tests. One line summary: **UPA and EDC are
  decided by a Glushkov position-automaton reduction of the spec's
  non-normative Appendix J guidance.** No new exported surface.
- Ratchet: **schema 2951 → 2988 pass (+37)**, measured by the arbiter with a
  real `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run; figures copied from the arbiter, not recomputed
  here. **ALL 37 flips verified fail→pass with ZERO pass→fail on any lane —
  verified directly rather than inferred from the aggregate count.** That is
  precisely the outcome this issue's entire design-review arc was built to
  prevent, and it did not occur. The 37 flipped W3C cases, by category:
  MS-ModelGroups (30 — mgQ001/004/005/021, mgR001–022, mgS002–005),
  MS-Additional (3 — addB100/113/178), MS-Particles (2 — particlesZ033_c,
  particlesZ037), All (2 — all240, all243).
- **This was the hardest and highest-stakes change of the session**, and the
  process shape is worth preserving as a reference for future sessions: three
  grounding rounds, a design-sketch pre-flight *before* any code, a warden
  CHANGES-REQUESTED cycle, and an arbiter review that did its own independent
  adversarial testing instead of trusting the implementer's self-report. This is
  what "getting genuinely hard finalize-phase logic right" looks like here.
- Grounded (oracle GROUNDING, issue #180): `cos-nonambig` and
  `cos-element-consistent` quoted verbatim. The decisive finding is that the
  spec defines UPA **purely declaratively**, via a `·compete·`/`·path·`
  relation, with **no normative algorithm anywhere in the main body** — the only
  implementable algorithm is **Appendix J, explicitly marked non-normative**
  ("guidance, without claiming to be complete"). The 1.0→1.1 diff was confirmed
  from the spec's own changelog: element/wildcard competition is now *permitted*
  and is the **sole** substantive UPA relaxation; element/element and
  wildcard/wildcard competition remain forbidden. Also confirmed UPA's sole home
  is §3.8.6.4 on **Model Group**, not Particle. Saved to
  `.agent/grounding-issue-180.md`.
- Second grounding round (two narrow questions, after the warden pass):
  `{abstract}` plays **no role** in UPA/EDC — it is orthogonal to §3.8 and
  governs only runtime substitution legality; and `cos-nonambig` **applies even
  to an unreferenced named `<group>` definition** (medium-high confidence,
  composed inference), because §3.8.6's chapeau is unconditional and
  component-scoped, not usage-scoped.
- Design-sketch pre-flight (mason, sketch only, no code) earned its keep twice
  over. It found Appendix J's declarative two-bullet shortcut **inadequate on its
  own** — it misses the canonical violation `(a,b)|(a,c)` inside a
  choice-of-sequences — and proposed instead implementing Appendix J's FSA
  sketch as a **Glushkov position automaton**. It also flagged, without
  deciding, the one real hazard: `xsd/wildcardadmit.go`'s
  `inSubstitutionGroupOf` is a **documented over-approximation**, safe for its
  existing wildcard-admission consumer (which needs to fail *closed*), so
  reusing it here would **invert the safety direction into a false-REJECT risk
  for a valid schema**.
- Warden pre-flight: **CHANGES REQUESTED**, and it ruled on that hazard **from
  first principles rather than by analogy to an existing PRINCIPLES pattern**.
  The two failure directions are asymmetric: a false *accept* of a UPA violation
  is graceful (§3.8.4.2 still defines how a processor picks a ·validation-path·,
  so instance validation proceeds), while a false *reject* is a hard
  denial-of-service and a genuine ratchet regression. Mandated splitting the
  predicate into `mayBeInSubstitutionGroupOf` (renamed, over-approximating, kept
  for its original consumer) and a new `certainlyInSubstitutionGroupOf`
  (under-approximating, exact whenever a cheap schema-global precondition holds,
  `// GAP(subst)`-marked otherwise) so the UPA/EDC consumer gets the
  safe-direction answer. Also required three corrections to the Glushkov
  derivation — `<all>` needs `(P1|...|Pn)*` and not a literal interleave; finite
  occurrence ranges must be **unfolded, not loop-encoded**, or the checker
  false-rejects `minOccurs=maxOccurs=2` followed by a bare same-name element;
  positions need an **opaque particle-identity marker** so unfolded copies never
  flag against each other — plus a list of D1–D4/T1/T4/T6 findings.
- **Implementation surfaced errors in the design review itself, and mason
  reported them honestly and specifically rather than deviating silently.** This
  is the part of the session most worth preserving:
  - The review's `key-equiv-tt`/`key-equiv-ta` **clause assignment was
    backwards** (tt is positional over an ordered list; ta is a set comparison).
    Mason implemented the spec-correct reading.
  - The review's "short-circuit equivalent-to-itself before running clauses 1–5"
    instruction was **impossible as framed** (no `==` comparability) *and* was
    solving the wrong problem. The real false-reject risk was **upstream**, in
    `cos-element-consistent`'s own antecedent conflating "the same element
    declaration reached twice" with "two declarations." Mason's fix: an explicit
    **component-identity key** (expanded name for top-level; position-under-
    nearest-enclosing-named-root for inline), deduplicated *before* any clause
    runs. Mason called this **the single biggest false-reject class in the whole
    change** — and the design review never named it.
  - Particle-identity assignment had to **replay at every depth** (reset the ID
    allocator per unfolded copy so nested IDs match too), not just at the
    flatten-visit level the review implied — otherwise `(a,b){0,2}`
    false-rejects.
  - Naive unfolding of large `maxOccurs` needed a **bounded, verdict-preserving**
    variant (2 mandatory + 2 optional copies, with a proof sketch that further
    copies produce no new distinct competition-set shape). The review did not
    anticipate the hang risk.
  - The `<all>` → `(P1|...|Pn)*` transcription needed an explicit
    **emptiability carve-out** the review never mentioned: the star form is
    always emptiable, but an `<all>` group is emptiable only when every member
    is.
  - One item mason **explicitly left for arbiter judgment** instead of deciding
    alone: passing the zero `ComplexType{}` value into
    `s.allowsElementWildcardName` for the unreferenced-named-`<group>` case
    (which has no containing complex type), on the reasoning that the zero
    value's nil `ContentType()` correctly makes the sibling-exclusion machinery
    exclude nothing.
- Arbiter VERDICT: **ACCEPT**, no repair round — and it **independently verified
  all six self-reported corrections rather than trusting the report**. It
  re-read the spec text for the key-equiv-tt/ta swap and confirmed mason's
  reading right and the original review wrong. It wrote **four adversarial test
  shapes of its own for the false-ACCEPT direction** of the component-identity
  dedup — the more dangerous failure mode, two genuinely-different same-named
  declarations wrongly merged, letting a real type mismatch go unchecked — and
  confirmed all four correctly reject. It verified the unfold-bound claim
  **empirically**, rebuilding with the bound raised 2→5 and re-ratcheting the
  whole schema lane in an isolated worktree: **byte-identical expectations**,
  confirming the bound masks nothing. It confirmed the zero-`ComplexType`
  reading by tracing the actual code path.
- One non-blocking arbiter finding: the `<all>` emptiability carve-out is
  **correct code with no test currently exercising it**. The arbiter confirmed
  the hazard is real via a **4000-model differential** (2804 vs 2805 rejections
  with/without the carve-out) — the shipped code has it right, but a ~12-line
  regression test is owed.
- Spec: `cos-nonambig` (Structures §3.8.6.4), `cos-element-consistent`
  (§3.8.6.3), `key-equiv-tt`/`key-equiv-ta` (§3.8.6.3),
  `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec` (§3.3.6.3), `cos-aw-intersect` (§3.10.6.4),
  Appendix J (non-normative), Appendix G.1.3.
- Surprises: the spec hands you **no algorithm at all** for its own most famous
  content-model constraint — the entire implementable core of this issue lives
  in a non-normative appendix, so grounding produced a *licence to engineer*
  rather than a rule to transcribe. And the design review, despite being the
  thing that saved the change, was **itself wrong in six identifiable places**;
  the implementation is what found that out. A review's value here was not in
  being right, but in forcing the hazard into the open early enough that being
  wrong about it was cheap.
- Friction: none material, but the shape is worth noting — the cost of this
  issue sat almost entirely in *design*, not repair. Three grounding rounds and
  a CHANGES-REQUESTED cycle before the first line of code bought a
  first-pass ACCEPT on a 2033-line change.
- Next: **two items handed to the cartographer's post-land pass.** (a) Promote
  issue #180 comment `5080336967` — the `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec` clause 2.3
  follow-up writeup — to a **real filed issue**; it currently exists only as a
  comment and is therefore invisible to the `ready` backlog. (b) File and track
  the **missing `<all>`-emptiability regression test** the arbiter identified.
  Otherwise the develop loop continues down the PLAN.md priority order: the
  remaining M4 sub-slices **#181, #182, #183**.

## 2026-07-26 — #262 — xsd: `derivation-ok-restriction` (all clauses but 2.4.2) + the last `ct-props-correct` clauses enforced at finalize

- Shipped (commits `6082c2c` implementation, `63018ed` repair, `595d899`
  ratchet) finalize-phase enforcement of Structures §3.4.6.3
  `derivation-ok-restriction` clauses 1/2/3/4/5 for restriction-derived complex
  types, plus the remaining `ct-props-correct` (§3.4.6.1) clauses **2 and 4**,
  as a new **Phase D** (`checkComplexDerivations`) appended to
  `Schema.resolve()` and driven from `s.types` in document order like every
  other phase. Three new files — `xsd/effectivetotalrange.go` (the unexported
  `effectiveRange` type + one `combineRange` combinator covering
  `cos-seq-range`/`cos-choice-range`, and `particleEmptiable` for
  `cos-group-emptiable`), `xsd/defaultbinding.go` (a sealed-sum `defaultBinding`
  for `key-dft-binding`, plus `checkBindingSubsumes` for `loc-testSubP`), and
  `xsd/complexderivation.go` (the clause checks themselves, with
  `derivedOKComplex` taking the blocking-keyword set as a parameter).
  **Zero new exported identifiers.** Clause **2.4.2 is explicitly out of scope**,
  delegated to #263 behind a GAP seam.
- Ratchet: **schema 2988 → 2997 pass (+9)**, measured by the arbiter with a real
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run; figures copied from the arbiter, not recomputed here.
  All 9 flips were **individually verified against the rule actually charged**
  (`derivation-ok-restriction` clauses 2/3/4, including `loc-testSubP` 5.3) —
  not inferred from the aggregate — with **zero regressions on any lane** and a
  `go doc -all` diff whose only delta is one cosmetic middot.
- `effectiveRange`/`particleEmptiable` was **deliberately not unified** with the
  pre-existing `emptiable` notion in `xsd/particleattribution.go`: the two
  diverge on an empty `<choice>`, so merging them would smuggle one rule's
  domain into another (cf. the #180 `mayBe`/`certainlyInSubstitutionGroupOf`
  split). Same-shaped fact, genuinely different fact.
- Design pre-flight (mason sketch → warden review) returned
  **approve-with-changes with 6 MUST items, all taken before a line of code was
  written.** The two worth preserving: (a) **do not reuse `Occurs` for
  effective-total-range** — same shape, different domain, a smuggled-domain risk;
  and (b) **drop the proposed `tools/rulecat` change entirely** — the five "rule"
  IDs mason planned to add were spec *clause-anchors and definition-anchors*, not
  Schema Component Constraints, and every rule ID this change actually charges
  **already existed in `xsderr/catalog.go`**. The rest: fold the unbounded×0
  guard into a single combinator; model the default binding as a sealed sum, not
  a kind-tagged struct; no visited/seen maps (Phase B already proves the relevant
  graphs acyclic); drive Phase D from `s.types` in document order.
- Arbiter round 1: **REJECT — one real bug, and it erred in the
  false-REJECT direction.** `checkRestrictionAttributes` rejected a *valid*
  3-level chain `A(@x) ← B(inherits, does not redeclare) ← C(redeclares @x)`
  because it never walked the base chain for attribute uses that are **inherited
  rather than redeclared**. Naming the direction matters here: a false accept is
  the dangerous one (a bad schema goes unchallenged and the ratchet says
  nothing), but a false *reject* is the one the ratchet catches loudly — it
  denies service on a legal schema and shows up as a lane regression. Repaired
  (`63018ed`) with a new `foldedAttributeUse` mirroring the existing
  `locallyDeclaredAttributeType` base-chain-walk pattern.
- The repair was **deliberately scoped to attribute uses only, not the base's
  {attribute wildcard}** — folding wildcards too would make the check vacuous
  (every type chains up to `xs:anyType`'s lax `##any` wildcard) and is
  unreachable today anyway, since the producer never emits `<extension>`. The
  arbiter **verified that scope call independently** rather than accepting it:
  it read `seedAnyType`'s wildcard and confirmed the vacuity claim, and ruled
  that a plain doc comment — **not** a `// GAP` marker — is the right disclosure,
  because there is no unsupported-construct fallback at that read site.
- Arbiter round 2: **ACCEPT.** Full gate green, and the repair was
  **mutation-tested rather than read**: reverting `foldedAttributeUse` makes
  3 of the 5 new test rows fail with the original false-reject error, confirming
  the guard is load-bearing.
- Four GAPs disclosed (fail-open, never false-reject, STYLE 9), all filed as
  follow-up **#265**: (1) clause 5's assertions-prefix check — it holds *by
  construction* given how `{assertions}` is mapped, and both a literal-check and
  a folded-check implementation would be wrong, so it is intentionally **not
  charged**; (2) `loc-testSubP` clause 3's strict-wildcard branch, which needs
  `cos-ns-subset` (§3.10.6.2) — not yet implemented, and also owned by #52;
  (3) `key-dft-binding` case 3 (the assessment-episode governing declaration);
  (4) clause 3's required-attribute implication firing only on *explicit*
  relaxation, pending a §3.4.2.4 clause-3 inheritance fold.
- Spec: `derivation-ok-restriction` (Structures §3.4.6.3, clauses 1/2/3/4/5),
  `ct-props-correct` (§3.4.6.1, clauses 2 and 4) — **`ct-props-correct` is
  complete with this commit** — `cos-seq-range`/`cos-choice-range`,
  `cos-group-emptiable`, `key-dft-binding`, `loc-testSubP`.
- Surprises: the design review's most valuable output was a **deletion** — the
  `tools/rulecat` change it removed would have polluted a deliberately closed
  rule set with anchors that are not constraints. Cheaper to catch in a sketch
  than in a repair round. And the one bug that survived to the arbiter was in
  the *inheritance* path, not the clause logic: every clause was transcribed
  correctly, and what broke was the ordinary base-chain walk around them.
- Friction: none material. One rejection, one repair, accept — well inside the
  two-rejection cap, and the design round in front paid for itself again.
- Next: **two non-blocking follow-ups logged on #262 for the cartographer's
  post-land pass** (neither blocked this landing). (a) #265 needs a line added
  for **§3.4.2.5 clause 2.2** — the extension base-wildcard fold — currently
  untracked and false-reject-shaped. (b) #265's eventual fix must **replace**
  `foldedAttributeUse`, not sit beside it (STYLE T4 — no duplicate encoding of
  the same fact). #263 still owns clause 2.4.2.

## 2026-07-26 — /retro (weekly, third) — stale-base-ref pattern fixed at three entry points; debt-marker convention enforced; 5 ledger leaks found and filed as #270; repair rate up 3.4× on the M4 frontier

- Trigger: weekly `/retro` over 2026-07-19..-26 (window since the second
  retro, `7e81f78`). Evidence: docs/LOG (31 dated entries), `git log`
  (`7e81f78..ef77dda` = 38 commits — 11 `meta:` + 1 `docs:` bookkeeping,
  **26 issue landings**), the `needs-replan` list (**empty, 0 issues**,
  verified via the label query, not assumed), issue #195/#265's bodies, and
  the arbiter/warden verdict comments on the window's landings. Chronicler
  Part 1 only; the steward's Part 2 architecture audit is delegated
  separately by the orchestrating session.

- **Recurring friction #1 (FIXED here) — the base a session diffs, judges,
  or branches against is routinely a STALE ref.** Three independent data
  points, two in-window, and the pattern was already flagged on record and
  never picked up:
  - **#87 (07-13, ~L1922):** the arbiter first diffed against a stale local
    `main` and flagged a **phantom expectations change** — a false alarm on
    the one thing the project treats as constitutional. Re-diffed against
    `origin/main`, clean.
  - **#170 (07-20, ~L4783):** the arbiter again found the local `main` stale
    (`6882614`) at judgment and correctly judged `origin/main..HEAD` instead.
    Its own entry filed the ask verbatim: *"the second consecutive session
    where a stale-ref/base-selection gotcha showed up in an arbiter comment;
    flag for the next /retro to confirm whether a 'fetch/reset local main
    before judging' step belongs in WORKFLOW.md or the arbiter prompt (one
    more data point makes it a pattern)."* That flag postdates the 07-19
    retro, so it was live in this window, not an old leak — and nothing
    picked it up.
  - **This retro session (07-26):** reproduced live at session start. HEAD
    detached, and `origin/main` itself pointed at `2953e43` — **18 commits
    behind** the real tip `ef77dda` — stale from the container's shallow
    clone (`git reflog show origin/main` confirms:
    `fetch --no-progress --depth 50 origin main: storing head` @ `2953e43`).
    Every base this session could have picked without an explicit fetch was
    wrong. Confirmed via GitHub search that **no open issue tracked this**.
  - **Entry point = the arbiter's judging step (WORKFLOW step 5) and session
    start.** WORKFLOW step 5 said *"reviews the branch diff against main"* —
    the ambiguous phrasing that licenses `git diff main..HEAD` — and
    `arbiter.md`'s procedure step 1 said *"Read the ENTIRE `git diff` (staged
    + unstaged)"*, naming no base at all and explicitly contemplating an
    **uncommitted** tree.
  - **Smallest durable fix applied in this `meta: retro 2026-07-26` commit**
    — one canonical statement plus two operative mirrors, no new machinery:
    (a) a new invariant bullet in WORKFLOW.md's branch-scheme section,
    *"Freshly-fetched `origin/main` is the only base"*, stating the rule once
    where the doc already collects rules of exactly that kind, naming the
    failing shape (`git diff main..HEAD`) against the working one
    (`git diff origin/main...HEAD` after a fetch); (b) WORKFLOW step 5
    retargeted to *"the branch's committed diff against freshly-fetched
    `origin/main` (never a local `main`)"*; (c) `arbiter.md` procedure step 1
    replaced with the three-command preamble (`git fetch origin main`,
    `git status --porcelain` must be empty, `git diff origin/main...HEAD`).
  - The fix deliberately carries a **second direction** the evidence
    demanded: *judge the COMMITTED tree, not the working tree.* **#244** is
    why. `main` went **RED** in this window — `go build ./...` failed on
    `parser/produce_xpath.go:164: undefined: resolveQName` — because #179's
    merge-conflict fix was applied to the working tree and **gate-verified
    green there**, but the file had been staged before the fix and was never
    re-staged, so `git commit --no-edit` silently landed the broken version.
    Same family as the stale-base failures: *the artifact that was verified
    was not the artifact that landed.* One occurrence, so not a pattern on
    its own — but its mechanism is closed by the same clause, at no extra
    cost, and a red `main` violates the branch scheme's "always green".
  - Scope check: `arbiter.md`'s **Procedure** section is ordinary process
    text. The **Ratchet integrity** section and CLAUDE.md's "one rule" were
    **not touched** — constitutional guardrail respected.

- **Recurring friction #2 (CORRECTLY TRACKED, not re-filed — but escalated)
  — mason writing unauthorized `docs/LOG` entries.** The 07-19 retro's fix
  (a `mason.md` boundary clause) failed on the **very next issue**: #162
  (07-19) had mason write a LOG entry inside implementation commit `77e06db`,
  reverted at land in `5104bc2`. That recurrence is already filed as **#195**
  (open, `ready`/`kind/process`/`kind/tooling`), which correctly diagnoses
  the prose guard as insufficient and proposes a mechanical one. **Not a
  ledger leak — the tracking is clean, and I am not filing a duplicate.**
  What the window shows that #195's body does not: **the workaround held 100%
  of the time and cost 100% manual effort.** Zero recurrences after #162
  across **six** mason-touched landings (#167, #98, #75, #175, #176, #177),
  and every one of those six entries records that the orchestrator had to
  *explicitly instruct mason not to write the LOG entry that session* (LOG
  ~L4529, 5598, 5688, 5766, 5825, 6031). That is textbook **repeated manual
  toil** (PRINCIPLES 27) — six sessions of standing overhead to work around
  an open, ready, already-specified fix. **Recommendation for the
  cartographer, recorded here rather than as a new issue: promote #195 in the
  priority order.** The 07-26 backlog's band-1 sequence (#263, #210, #228,
  #230, #231) does not contain it, so a `ready` process fix with six sessions
  of demonstrated carrying cost is currently queued behind feature work while
  the toil keeps accruing.

- **Minor correction folded into the same commit — WORKFLOW.md's branch table
  asserted something false.** It read *"Exactly three kinds of branches
  exist; nothing else is ever pushed"*, but every maintenance trigger pushes
  a fourth: PR **#268** (`meta: backlog 2026-07-26`) landed from
  `claude/optimistic-bardeen-4cygla` and PR **#254** from
  `claude/optimistic-bardeen-18qxvw`, and the LOG records at least two
  earlier naming shapes (`meta/retro-2026-07-12`, L2910). Left alone, the
  namespace claims to be an exhaustive index while a whole class of branch is
  invisible to `git ls-remote --heads origin 'refs/heads/wip/*'` — the exact
  shape of the old #112 stranded-commits incident. Corrected in one clause
  that admits the short-lived maintenance branch and points at "Other
  triggers" (which already governs its same-session landing). Not dressed up
  as a friction pattern: one session's confusion (mine, choosing a branch
  name for this run), plus a normative doc stating a verifiable falsehood.

- **Recurring friction #3 (FIXED here) — the debt-marker convention is not
  enforced, and #51 deferred the fix to this retro by name.** `#51`'s entry
  (~L6449) records it as friction outright: *"the `PARTIAL` marker convention
  does not exist … `grep PARTIAL` returns ~19 unrelated 'PARTIAL order' hits
  in `value/` and `builtin/` — so the cl.2.3 gap block is honest prose that no
  debt sweep will ever find."* Same entry found a third site written
  `// GAP:` **without parens**, invisible to `grep -rn "GAP("`. Two more data
  points: #262's mason wrote `// GAP(derivation-ok-restriction 2.4.2)` —
  a rule ID where the house form takes an *area* — and #172's warden had to
  reject a `GAP` marker used at a non-XPath site as *"a marker-convention
  violation."* Corroborating from the other end: the 07-26 backlog's GAP sweep
  found #267 as the only one of 13 sites with no owning issue, and that sweep
  is necessary **precisely because** markers drift out of the net.
  - **Entry point = STYLE.** STYLE **P3** was scoped to *"Fail-open XPath
    gaps"* while the convention had long since gone repo-wide (`GAP(xsd)`,
    `GAP(datatypes)`), so nothing told a mason outside `xpath` which token to
    use — or that the parenthesis is what makes the sweep work.
  - **Fix applied in this commit:** P3 broadened from XPath to *any* deliberate
    incompleteness, with the sweep command named, the parenthesis called out as
    load-bearing, `PARTIAL`/`TODO`/`XXX` explicitly ruled out as tracking
    tokens, `<area>` distinguished from a rule ID (the #262 mistake), and the
    rule that a marker naming a closed issue must be repointed by the landing
    that closes it (the #51 `xsd/wildcard.go:107` mistake, where the marker
    named #51 as its own retirement owner).

- **Follow-up ledger audit — NOT clean: five confirmed leaks, now filed as
  #270.** This corrects my own first pass. A fast read of the LOG plus the
  headline issue bodies said "zero leaks"; an exhaustive read of the **verdict
  comments on all 26 landings** — which is what this duty actually requires,
  because the log under-reports them — found otherwise. Recording the
  correction rather than the first answer, since the first answer is exactly
  the failure mode a ledger audit exists to catch.
  - **Root cause, and the more important finding: #179 is the only landed
    issue in the window with NO cartographer post-land pass at all.** WORKFLOW
    step 7(b) is what harvests advisory verdict notes while context is fresh.
    It did not run for #179, and **both** of #179's advisories leaked for
    exactly that reason. Nothing in the loop makes a *skipped* post-land pass
    visible — it leaves no trace, and the leak surfaces a week later at a
    retro. Deliberately **not** fixed here: the candidate fixes (a required
    post-land comment, a checklist gate) are all heavier than one doc clause
    and belong to the cartographer's own loop. **Flagged as the leading
    candidate for the next retro's durable fix** if a second skipped pass
    appears.
  - The five leaked items, all living only as comments on **closed** issues:
    (1) `resolveSchemaLocation`'s doc comment states a wrong failure mode —
    **aggravating**, because open issue **#259** reproduces the same wrong
    prose in its body, so landing it would propagate the error;
    (2) §F.1 chameleon rewriting is under-specified for `substitutionGroup`,
    `@defaultAttributes`, `@refer`, `itemType`/`memberTypes` (the last two
    needing a list-aware `resolveQName`) — inert today, but #182/#183/#229
    will each silently under-rewrite with no failing test;
    (3) `conformance/schema.go:180-184`'s step-4 list reads exhaustive and
    omits the missing-`schemaLocation` fault; (4) `parser/doc.go`'s stale
    `*xsd.SchemaSet` prose, acknowledged by mason AND warden on #174 and filed
    by neither; (5) #75's `buildOwnFacets` non-repeatable-facet advisory,
    never confirmed or dismissed by any verdict — needs a decision, not
    necessarily a change.
  - Two more recorded on #270 rather than trusted to memory: #175's
    list/union/enum lane widening (left to "the next `/backlog` that carves
    the simpleType-variety producer" remembering it) and #173's
    `attributeGroupIndex`/`notationIndex` wiring, whose verdict says *"keep
    the follow-up open"* when no issue exists to keep open.
  - **Verified NOT leaks** (each has a real tracker, checked against issue
    bodies): #242's two verdict-mandated follow-ups → **#259** and **#257**;
    #157's `facetKindOf` false-accept → **#219**; #180's clause 2.3 → **#249**
    (and note #180's near-miss: mason, the arbiter, and the LOG's `Next:` all

    earlier — none of the three searched the backlog); the `<all>`
    emptiability test → **#261**; #172's `xsderr.Loc{}` → **#208**; #171's
    `{context}` → **#206**.
  - Items below still verified clean on their own terms:
  - **#262's two non-blocking follow-ups — BOTH TRACKED**, contrary to any
