- **Metrics (window 2026-07-19..-26 vs second retro 2026-07-13..-19):**
  - Sessions per landing commit: 31 entries / 26 landings ≈ **1.19** (was
    1.26, then 1.35) — continuing to tighten.
  - Repair rounds per accept: **5 / 26 ≈ 0.19** (was 0.056) — **up 3.4×**,
    the one metric that moved the wrong way. All five (#157, #175, #177,
    #178, #262) were single REJECT→ACCEPT, gate green, zero near the cap.
    Read as a **difficulty signal, not a discipline regression**: the window
    moved off the mature `datatypes` vertical onto the brand-new M4
    schema-construction/parser frontier (component shapes, finalize phases, a
    Glushkov automaton for UPA, multi-document assembly). The rate is
    essentially the *first* retro's 0.21 — i.e. what this project costs on
    unfamiliar ground. The counter-evidence that discipline held: the
    **design pre-flight** (warden reviewing a mason sketch before any code)
    demonstrably prevented repairs — #171/#172/#173/#174/#46/#51/#63/#180 all
    record "landed with no arbiter repair round … the pre-flight front-loaded
    the fixes", and #262's warden round produced a *deletion* (dropping a
    `tools/rulecat` change whose five "rule IDs" were spec clause-anchors,
    not Schema Component Constraints) that would otherwise have been a
    rejection finding.
  - Rejects per accept: **≈ 0.19** (was 0.056); **zero** issues hit the
    two-reject park cap; **zero** `needs-replan`; **zero** parked branches;
    branch namespace clean (`wip/*` and `parked/*` both empty at tip).
  - Ratchet slope — **the window's headline, and it is enormous.** The
    `schema` lane went from **0 → 2997 pass / 12435 fail (15432)**: dead
    baseline until #175 brought the lane live at 2731, then #176 **+135**
    (→2866), #177 **+70** (→2936), #242 **+15** (→2951), #180 **+37**
    (→2988), #262 **+9** (→2997). `datatypes` **1025 → 1043 pass**, fail
    30 → 31 against a total that grew 1055 → 1074 (19 new rows entering the
    denominator: +18 pass, +1 honest new-at-fail — not a regression).
    `instance` untouched at 0 / 26426 (M5 unstarted); `xpath`/`json`/`ber`
    empty by design. **Upward-only, zero regressions on any lane all window.**
    All figures copied from the arbiter's verdict comments and the 07-26
    backlog entry — **not recomputed here; the chronicler never runs the
    ratchet.**
  - Ready-queue depth: **35** (was 8; target band 8–10) — the fifth backlog
    running out of band, and the band itself is now the stale number, not the
    queue. Cause, for the record: the M4/M5 producer epics (#79/#250) opened a
    **wide DAG frontier** — many genuinely-parallel leaves unblocked at once —
    and the develop loop's own post-land harvest is *self-feeding* the queue
    (#232, #238, #242, #257, #263, #264, #265, #267 were all filed by landings
    in this window, not by a planning session). A deep queue produced by
    harvesting is a health signal, not a planning failure; the 07-25 backlog
    already reclassified it as structural and answered it with an explicit
    priority ordering in PLAN.md rather than by trimming. **Flagged for the
    cartographer: the 8–10 target in WORKFLOW.md's `/backlog` description no
    longer describes any reality and should either be re-based or re-expressed
    as "enough ready work at the frontier's width".** Not changed here — the
    band is the cartographer's instrument, and re-basing it on one retro's
    read would be the chronicler overreaching.

- **Observed but deliberately NOT fixed this run — three patterns that are
  real, recur, and are each too big for a retro's one-clause budget.**
  Recorded so the next retro inherits evidence rather than starting over:
  - **Defective issue bodies (enters at: ISSUE BODY; caught at grounding or
    warden, i.e. after a session has already started).** The window's most
    common friction by volume. Wrong spec anchors (#46 §3.16.6.3→§3.16.6.2,
    #172 §4.2.2→§4.2.1/§4.2.3, #170 §3.9.6→§3.4.6.3), invented rule IDs (#46's
    `cos-list-of-atomic`, `cos-no-circular-unions` — 1.0 terminology),
    acceptance criteria naming already-landed work (#178, #180), a wrong
    dependency (#51 names #178 as the "wildcard producer"; #178 touched no
    wildcard code), and hardcoded stale ratchet baselines (#75's "939/25" vs a
    live 730). Compounding mechanism worth naming: **bodies are treated as
    unsafe to edit** — #179 recorded a correction as a comment *"rather than
    edited into the issue body to avoid corrupting existing entity-escaped
    markup"*, so corrections accumulate in threads while the body stays wrong
    and downstream issues inherit it. The fix is the cartographer's (filing
    discipline, or a safe body-edit path), not a WORKFLOW clause.
  - **"Fix it next time this file is touched" as a disposal channel with no
    owner (enters at: VERDICT).** Seven instances (#242, #179 ×2, #177, #168,
    #174, #167). It has no forcing function, and #242 proved the failure: it
    was explicitly named as *"the most likely next toucher"* for #179's
    advisory, it landed, and the correction was not made. Three of the five
    leaks filed as #270 arrived through this channel. A durable fix probably
    means abolishing the channel — every advisory either files or is dismissed
    in writing — which is a WORKFLOW step-7 change and the cartographer's call.
  - **Grounding errors corrected downstream instead of re-grounded (enters at:
    GROUNDING).** #178's grounding was wrong about `xml:base` (mason caught
    it); #170's was wrong about `Occurs{0,0}` (warden caught it, and a second
    oracle round had to be dispatched *mid-review*); #51's oracle gloss
    contradicted its own quoted spec text and the arbiter overruled it,
    approvingly: *"Mason preferring the grepped spec over the paraphrase is
    the behavior CLAUDE.md requires."* That this keeps working is a credit to
    the downstream agents, but **nothing in the loop mandates verifying an
    oracle claim** — it happened by individual arbiter initiative each time.
  - Two smaller ones, noted only: **subagent GitHub access was unavailable in
    at least three sessions** (#174, #180, #173), and on #180 the warden
    worked from a summary rather than the sketch and got a spec detail
    backwards; and **environment preconditions go unverified at session
    start** — #177 found `testdata/xsdtests` not checked out, making its first
    conformance run **vacuous** (*"Any session that reports a conformance
    result without the submodule present has reported nothing"*). The latter
    is the same family as friction #1 and is partly addressed by the arbiter's
    new clean-status/fetch preamble, but not fully.
- Ratchet: **unchanged** (process docs + one agent prompt; no code, no
  expectations file touched, no `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run).
- Decisions: fixed friction #1 at three entry points rather than one, because
  the two failures entered at *different* steps (judgment base vs. session
  start) and a single note in either place would have missed the other. Did
  NOT file a new issue for friction #2 — #195 already exists, already
  proposes the right fix, and a duplicate would be ledger noise; escalated by
  recommendation instead. Did NOT re-base the ready-queue target band, and did
  NOT implement #195's mechanical guard here: that is tooling code needing the
  full gate and an arbiter verdict, i.e. a develop iteration, not a retro.
  Filed the five leaks as **one** issue (#270) rather than five, since four of
  the five are prose corrections and splitting them would cost more queue
  noise than it buys; #270 says explicitly that the cartographer may split it.
- Surprises: (1) the stale-ref pattern reproduced **on the retro session sent
  to investigate it**, 18 commits behind, which is as clean a confirmation as
  this kind of thing ever gets. (2) The repair-rate regression turned out to
  be the *healthiest*-looking number once read against what the window
  attempted — a 3.4× rise while the schema lane went 0→2997 with zero
  regressions is a good trade, and mistaking it for a discipline problem
  would have produced exactly the wrong fix. (3) **My own first ledger pass
  said "clean" and was wrong.** The leaks were invisible from the LOG and the
  headline issue bodies; they only appeared once every verdict comment on all
  26 landings was read. The duty text already warns that *"the log alone
  under-reports them"* — this run is the evidence for why that sentence is
  there, and the reason the audit must be exhaustive rather than
  representative.
- Friction (this run): the stale ref above cost a fetch and a re-read of the
  repo state before any evidence-gathering could be trusted — now fixed.
- Next: **/retro Part 2 (steward architecture audit)**, delegated separately
  by the orchestrating session — it should weigh the newly-large `xsd`
  finalize surface (Phases A–D across `resolve.go`,
  `particleattribution.go`, `elementconsistent.go`, `complexderivation.go`,
  `effectivetotalrange.go`, `defaultbinding.go`) and the deliberate
  `effectiveRange`/`emptiable` and `mayBe`/`certainly…` splits, all of which
  are *documented* same-shape-different-fact decisions and should be
  confirmed or challenged as such. Next chronicler retro: (a) confirm the
  `origin/main` base rule ended the stale-ref recurrences; (b) check whether
  #195 got promoted and the six-sessions-and-counting manual toil ended;
  (c) watch whether the repair rate falls back as M4 becomes familiar ground;
  (d) check that STYLE P3's broadened marker rule actually shows up in new
  `GAP(` sites, and that no new `PARTIAL`/bare-`GAP:` debt prose appears;
  (e) **watch for a second skipped post-land pass** — one more makes the
  #179-shaped leak a pattern and promotes it to the durable-fix slot;
  (f) discharge or split **#270**.


## 2026-07-26 — /retro Part 2 (steward architecture audit)

- Scope: the M4 window since the 2026-07-19 audit — 26 landings, the new
  `parser` package (#167, #174, #176-179), the `xsd` component shapes
  (#168-172), the finalize/resolve phase (#173, #180, #262), and the
  `schema` conformance lane (#175, #242). Audited against `origin/main`
  `dc933fe` (fetched first; the local branch was the pre-squash retro branch
  — PR #269's new invariant exercised and it caught exactly the ref it was
  written for).
- Full gate green on the audit commit (build/test/vet/lint/conformance);
  docs-only change, ratchet untouched and unread (steward never runs it).

### Per-package verdict

| package | verdict |
| --- | --- |
| `xsderr` | **refactor recorded (F3)** — pure leaf, but see F3 |
| `xsd` | **refactor recorded (F1, F4)** — leaf purity intact (imports only `xsderr`) |
| `parser/xmltree` | **sound** |
| `parser` | **refactor recorded (F1, F2)** |
| `loader` | **sound** |
| `value`, `value/backendtest` | **sound** (but F3 touches `value`) |
| `builtin`, `builtin/strict`, `builtin/native` | **sound** (but F3 touches `builtin/strict`) |
| `regex` | **sound** |
| `conformance` | **refactor recorded (F2)** |
| `xpath`, `validate*`, `codegen`, `codec`, `cmd/goxsd8` | **sound** (doc.go-only stubs) |

Import graph re-derived with `go list`: the DAG holds. `xsderr` and `xsd`
import nothing and only `xsderr` respectively; **nothing imports
`conformance`**; no adapter decoder leaks into the library. The one edge the
DAG did not record was `parser → builtin/strict` (the default backend
`Parse` seeds) — now written into ARCHITECTURE.md.

### Findings (issue-ready; SEE BLOCKER BELOW)

- **F1 — `kind/refactor`, `area/xsd`+`area/parser`, rank 1.** QName
  reference resolution and named-circularity rejection have **two homes,
  chosen by per-component representation accident.** `xsd` stores a complex
  type's `{base type definition}`, `<element ref>`, `<group ref>` and keyref
  as QNames, so `xsd/resolve.go` resolves them at finalize and rejects
  `ct-props-correct` cl. 3 / `mg-props-correct` cl. 2 / `e-props-correct`
  cl. 5 there. But `SimpleType.base` is a **live `*SimpleType` pointer**
  (`xsd/simpletype.go:461`) and `<attributeGroup ref>` is inlined at mapping
  time with no ref component — so `parser/produce.go` must resolve both
  eagerly, and `st-props-correct` cl. 2 circularity is rejected at
  `parser/produce.go:298`, in phase 2. Evidence of upkeep coupling, not mere
  parallel shape: (a) `src-resolve` cl. 1.1 is charged from **both**
  packages (`parser/produce.go:385` and `xsd/resolve.go`'s
  `resolveTypeName`) with two different messages; (b) `produce.go:107`'s own
  comment says its guard is *"mirroring xsd/resolve.go's color-map idiom
  collapsed into one map"* — the traversal idiom is copied; (c) both
  `xsd/doc.go` (*"spec-forbidden circularities are rejected at finalize"*)
  and `parser/doc.go` (*"rejected HERE [phase 3], once"*) state a contract
  the code keeps for only 4 of the 6 reference kinds. Cost of delay is the
  reason this ranks first: `<list>`/`<union>` are next in `parser` and add
  item/member **pointers** — a third eager guard, plus `st-props-correct`
  circular-union rejection landing in the producer too — and
  `<import>`/`<redefine>`/`<override>` after them *re-point* base
  references, which a pointer-valued base makes far more awkward than a
  QName-plus-index one. Proposed direction: give `SimpleType` a QName base
  reference like `ComplexType` has, move both resolutions and both
  circularity rejections to `xsd`'s finalize, delete the producer-side
  guards. Notes: pre-1.0 mobility — `SimpleType`'s constructor signature and
  `builtin.Seed`'s contract both change, and that is exactly the movement
  this window is for.

- **F2 — `kind/refactor`, `area/parser`+`area/conformance`, rank 2.** The
  schema lane re-implements the parser's phase-2 include discovery.
  `conformance/schema_closure.go:188-195` says of its own
  `resolveSchemaLocation`: *"It is a VERBATIM copy of
  parser.resolveSchemaLocation (parser/parse.go) and MUST be kept in sync
  with it."* Verified byte-identical today — so the evidence here is the
  declared coupling, not yet divergence. It is not one function: the file
  also re-implements `assembly.discover`/`.include`/`.fetch` (line 14-17:
  *"parser.Parse cannot be asked which documents those were"*) and
  `parser`'s unexported `attrValue` (as `elementAttr`, line 222-228). Root
  cause is a **seam gap, not laziness**: `parser.Parse` returns components
  but not the document set it assembled, and its most demanding consumer
  needs that set to gate every document in a closure against the
  false-accept allowlist. The copy's own STYLE T5 justification ("no library
  user needs it") is sound as far as it goes but solves the wrong problem —
  exporting `resolveSchemaLocation` would delete one copy; letting `Parse`
  report its closure deletes all three. Cost of delay: every remaining M4
  composition feature (`<import>`, `<redefine>`, `<override>`) adds a
  discovery edge the harness copy must mirror, so the copy grows
  monotonically with the epic; fixing it after those land means porting
  three edges instead of one.

- **F3 — `kind/refactor`, `area/xsderr`, rank 3.** The typed-`Rule`
  currency is unenforced, and the godoc says otherwise. `xsderr/doc.go:48-50`
  states: *"Tests assert that every Rule constructed in this module is
  either in the catalog or the sentinel — an error citing a rule the spec
  doesn't define is a bug by construction."* **No such test exists.**
  `IsValidRule` has zero non-test consumers module-wide; its only three
  tests call it on hardcoded literals. Meanwhile the two conventions have
  split: `xsd`/`parser`/`regex` declare 39 typed `xsderr.Rule` constants
  across 20 files, while `value` (10 sites) and `builtin/strict` (50 sites)
  pass bare string literals inline to `xsderr.New`/`Wrap`, which compile via
  untyped-constant conversion. So a typo'd or non-spec rule ID is accepted
  at 60 call sites today, silently, and lands in the expectations files the
  one rule protects. Cheap to fix and it gets more valuable every milestone;
  ranked below F1/F2 only because it costs no *mobility* to defer. Two
  parts: make the doc.go claim true (a module-wide test that every
  constructed `Rule` passes `IsValidRule`), and converge `value`/
  `builtin/strict` on named constants.

- **F4 — `kind/refactor`, `area/xsd`, rank 4 (godoc drift; steward may
  not edit Go).** `xsd/doc.go`'s *"# Walk API"* section documents a `Walker`
  ("the push driver") and a `Matcher` ("the pull driver") in present tense
  as part of the package contract; **neither type exists** — `go doc ./xsd`
  shows no `Walker` and no `Matcher`. A libuser reading godoc is told the
  package has an API it does not have. Separately,
  `xsd/xpathexpression.go` still defers `TypeAlternative.Test()` and
  identity-constraint `{selector}`/`{fields}` reuse to *"a later issue"*
  though both landed this window (#171, #178). ARCHITECTURE.md may keep
  describing Walker/Matcher as design intent — a design doc is allowed a
  destination — but a package's own doc.go is a contract and must describe
  what it ships.

### Confirmed sound — do not re-flag

- The `effectiveRange`/`isEmptiable` (`effectivetotalrange.go`) vs
  `emptiable` (`particleattribution.go`) pair, flagged for me by the
  chronicler: **same shape, different fact, correctly separated.** The
  files say so and cite why — an empty `<choice>` is NOT emptiable for UPA
  ("accepts nothing at all") but IS emptiable under `cos-group-emptiable`
  cl. 2 (effective-total-range minimum 0); *"Both readings are right for
  their own rule."* Merging them would be the bug.
- `mayBeInSubstitutionGroupOf` (over-approximates, omits cl. 2.3) vs
  `certainlyInSubstitutionGroupOf` (under-approximates) — two deliberate
  approximations serving two rules with opposite safe directions. Sound.
- `xmltree.Name` vs `xsd.QName`, and `xmltree.Namespace` vs
  `xsd.NamespaceBinding`: the raw-XML/component-model boundary, crossed in
  exactly one place (`parser/produce_xpath.go:38-40`). **No third
  name+namespace type appeared this window.** `xsd.EnumerationMember` and
  `xsd.XPathExpression` share the one `NamespaceBinding` record deliberately
  and say so.
- `xsd`'s sealed sums (`TypeDefinition`, `ContentType`, `Term`/`TermOrRef`,
  `Variety`, `AttributeDeclarationOrRef`) and the three Query capability
  views (`ElementResolver`/`AttributeResolver`/`TypeResolver`): minimal,
  consumer-side, one method each, no accretion. STYLE T3 held under a
  window that doubled the package.
- The 2026-07-19 `conformance/datatypes.go` cohort-isolation ruling stands;
  the new `schema` lane does not disturb it.

### Doc corrections landed here (ARCHITECTURE.md)

1. The DAG did not record `parser`'s real edges — added, naming
   `builtin/strict` as the default-backend policy edge.
2. The `xsd` phases bullet claimed all spec-forbidden circularities are
   rejected at phase 3; corrected to state the actual split and mark it as
   drift to repair (F1), with the pre-1.0 ordering argument.
3. "Parsing & loading" had bullets for `parser/xmltree` and `loader` but
   **none for `parser`** — the section predates the package. Added: the
   three phases, include-closure assembly, chameleon coercion, the
   once-per-assembly builtin seeding and why, plus the two seam properties
   consumers depend on (caller-supplied backend; assembled document set not
   reported — F2).

### Findings filed (blocker discharged by the orchestrating session)

GitHub was unreachable from the steward's own subagent context — its tool
set (Read/Grep/Glob/Bash only, per `.claude/agents/steward.md`) has no
GitHub MCP tools, and its shell `gh`/`GITHUB_PAT` attempts both 403'd. That
is a per-agent tool-access limit, not a repo/session-wide outage: the
orchestrating session (full GitHub MCP access) filed the findings and
landed this entry immediately afterward, in the same overall `/retro` run.

- **F1 → #271** (`kind/refactor`, `area/xsd`+`area/parser`) — filed
  verbatim from the write-up above.
- **F2 → #272**, narrowed. Search before filing found **#259** already
  open and already tracking the `resolveSchemaLocation`-copy half of this
  finding (filed 2026-07-25 off a #242 arbiter note) with its own detailed
  Acceptance criteria — filing a second issue over the same ground would
  have duplicated it. #272 covers only the REMAINDER F2 identified beyond
  #259: the `assembly.discover`/`.include`/`.fetch` walk and the
  `elementAttr`/`attrValue` copy, explicitly scoped OUT of #259 and
  cross-referencing it both ways.
- **F3 → #273** (`kind/refactor`, `area/xsderr`) — filed verbatim.
- **F4 — not filed as a new issue.** Search before filing found **#203**
  (open, `ready`) already carries F4's Walker/Matcher
  present-tense-but-unshipped godoc drift as its own Acceptance item 4
  ("Query/Walk sections marked not-implemented... carry the same
  planned/not-implemented marker `parser`'s doc uses") — the same finding,
  independently reached. A comment was added to #203 pointing at this
  audit as a second confirmation and folding in F4's other half (the
  `xsd/xpathexpression.go` "a later issue" comments now stale since #171/
  #178 landed) as an addendum to its scope, rather than opening a
  duplicate. Zero net new issues from F4; the ledger stays accurate
  because the search happened before filing, not after.

**Process note for future audits:** a read-only agent whose findings need
GitHub filing should hand its write-up to the orchestrating session to
file (as happened here), rather than the audit blocking on GitHub access
it structurally does not have — the steward's tool set is deliberately
Read/Grep/Glob/Bash only (it must not implement or touch the tracker
directly per its role boundary), so this handoff is the intended seam, not
a workaround. Worth a line in `docs/WORKFLOW.md`'s Part 2 description if
it recurs.

## 2026-07-27 — #182 — parser: `<xs:import>` cross-namespace references (per-document effective namespace replaces `assembly.tns`)

- Shipped: `parser.Parse` now follows `<xs:import>` as well as `<xs:include>`,
  so a schema assembles from a closure spanning MORE THAN ONE target namespace
  and a cross-namespace QName reference resolves against the imported
  components at finalize. Two commits on `wip/issue-182`: `2a0e1df` (the
  change — 8 files, +875/−196 against `175d302`; arbiter **ACCEPT on round 1**,
  no repair round) and `db463ec` (the arbiter's three comment/doc pre-land
  amendments plus the ratcheted `schema.txt`). New unexported machinery only:
  `discovered`, `docKey`, `importDocument`, `checkNoSelfImport`,
  `checkImportedNamespace`, `importDirective`, `scanKey`,
  `ruleSrcImport`/`ruleSrcImportNoSelfImport`. **Zero new exported
  identifiers** — the only exported-surface movement is doc prose
  (`parser/doc.go`, `Parse`'s comment, `conformance/schema.go`'s status
  block), all reconciled in the same commit per T6.
- The load-bearing design change, and it came out of the warden's pre-flight
  (verdict **revise**, 11 findings, all incorporated): **`assembly.tns` was
  DELETED, not supplemented.** That field's own comment claimed "every
  document of the closure ends up in it, by induction over src-include
  clause 2" — a statement only ever true of an include-only closure, and a lie
  the moment one `<import>` exists. Effective target namespace is now a
  per-document fact recorded where it is established, at discovery:
  `docs []discovered` with `discovered{doc *Document; tns string}`. This is
  one fact one encoding, NOT D3 redundancy — a chameleon include's effective
  namespace comes from its *includer*, i.e. from the discovery path, and is
  genuinely not derivable from the document itself. The comment says so
  explicitly ("Do not collapse this back into a single assembly-wide
  namespace") so nobody "simplifies" it into a derived call. `chameleon()`
  stays a derived predicate. The arbiter called this "the single most
  important thing the diff got right".
- **Import never chameleon-coerces.** §F.1's transformation belongs to
  `src-include` clause 2.3 alone, so an `<import>`ed document is always minted
  in its OWN target namespace — a bare `<import>` of a no-namespace document
  from a document that HAS a namespace leaves those components in no
  namespace. The naive `newProducer(doc, a.tns, …)` would have cross-
  namespaced every imported component AND flipped `producer.chameleon()` on
  for the imported no-tns case, applying coercion exactly where import forbids
  it. `TestParseImportNoChameleonCoercion` pins it; the arbiter mutation-
  verified that reverting to `discover(d2, tns)` fails it.
- **The load-once key widened to `(resolved location, namespace)`.** Location
  alone conflates two distinct component sets once import exists: the same
  document reached as a chameleon `<include>` (coerced into the includer's
  namespace) and as a bare `<import>` (staying in no namespace) must
  contribute both. It stays a LOAD-ONCE index and not an import cycle guard
  (D4/PRINCIPLES 5), and no second namespace-keyed cache was added. In an
  include-only closure the namespace component is constant, so include
  behaviour and the other lanes are bit-identical. `TestParseSameDocumentIncludedAndImported`
  pins it. Related shape decision: `<include>` and `<import>` dispatch in ONE
  document-order pass, so component entry order stays *document* order rather
  than becoming directive-kind-dependent — user-visible in
  `sch-props-correct` duplicate reports (D1/D2), and pinned by
  `TestParseDirectiveDocumentOrder` through a recording `loader.ResolverFunc`.
- `fetch` was generalized, not forked: it takes a `rule xsderr.Rule` and reads
  the directive's element name off `el`, rather than carrying a second
  stringly-typed `kind` beside a rule constant that already discriminates
  (T4/E2). Clause branching is on attribute PRESENCE via `attrValue`'s
  `(string, bool)`, matching the spec's own "**If** the `namespace` attribute
  is present" wording — `namespace=""` is a legal `xs:anyURI` and is *present*.
  A bare `<import>` (no `schemaLocation`) is deliberately NOT routed through
  `resolveSchemaLocation`, which returns the base URI for an empty location and
  would have made a hintless import re-read the importing document.
- Spec: `src-import` (§4.2.6.2) clauses 1, 2 and 3.1/3.2,
  `src-import-noselfimport` clauses 1.1 and 1.2, `sch-props-correct`
  (§3.17.6.1) clause 2 (the no-duplicate-expanded-name rule that repeat
  imports must not trip). `src-resolve` (§3.17.6.2) needed **no code change** —
  `xsd/resolve.go` already resolves by expanded QName against the whole
  finalized `Schema`, so import only had to get S2's components into the
  shared builder under the right namespace. Clause 1 is compared against the
  EFFECTIVE (post-§F.1) namespace, not the raw attribute: §4.2.3's chameleon
  note makes a chameleon-included document importing its includer's namespace
  a self-import too (`TestParseImportSelfNamespaceUnderChameleon`).
  Resolution failure is explicitly not a `src-import` error; only
  post-resolution mismatches are.
- Ratchet (the arbiter's figures, not recomputed here): **schema lane
  2997 → 3026 passing, +29**; 15432 names total unchanged; **0 regressed,
  0 new, 0 vanished, 0 downward flips**; datatypes/instance/xpath/json/ber
  untouched. Every one of the 29 flips has an `<xs:import>` in its root
  document and was previously *declined* by the shape allowlist rather than
  decided, so admitting import is a complete and sufficient explanation for
  the whole delta. Categories: MS-Schema (schE6, schE10, schF6, schG11,
  schG13, schZ015), MS-AttributeGroup (attgA008, attgB009, attgB012, attgB013,
  attgB015, attgC003, attgC014), MS-Additional (addB008, addB035, addB177),
  MS-ComplexType (ctG035, ctG047, ctO006), MS-Group (groupA007, groupA008),
  MS-Particles (particlesIc003, particlesZ003), MS-Element (elemZ008),
  MS-Wildcards (wildZ011), MGroupDef (targetNS00101m2), TargetNS (target007),
  Wild (wild053), anyAttribute (s3_10_6ii02).
- Four judgment calls put to the arbiter; **all four endorsed**, and two are
  worth remembering:
  - **Decline-on-no-D2 in the conformance closure scan.** An `<import>` that
    yields no D2 (no `schemaLocation`, or unresolvable) makes the whole case
    DECLINE, because an empty imported namespace turns every reference into
    that namespace into a *fabricated* `src-resolve` rejection — the one
    direction that can corrupt the ratchet. The arbiter checked the
    granularity (`decidable` early-returns on the first `false`, so a single
    such directive declines the whole case, closing the path rather than
    narrowing it), then reverted the rule and re-ran: the suite still said
    `ok`, because expectations had not yet been raised. That is the exact
    shape of the trap — the relaxed version would have harvested fabricated
    "passes" into the lane file, where they would sit until a correct
    `src-resolve` clause 4 / §5.3 implementation flipped them back and hit the
    never-regress wall. Real wins paid to avoid recording lies.
  - **Not taking §4.2.6.2's optional "ignore all but the first `<import>` for
    a given namespace" licence.** It is explicitly optional ("it is open to
    applications…, but such a strategy risks missing useful information");
    default behaviour is to compose everything. Verified end to end on
    schG11: the suite annotation says *"A import B, B import C, … (A and C
    have conflicting decl)"* with `validity="invalid"`, and we reject with
    `[sch-props-correct] … repeats the expanded name {ns-a}foo` — a genuine
    pass through an implemented rule on genuinely-assembled components, not a
    coincidence. Taking the licence would have lost the win *and* masked real
    duplicate-name violations.
  - Also endorsed: treating `namespace=""`/`targetNamespace=""` as ·absent·
    per the repo-wide `loader.Resolver` sentinel (tracking literal attribute
    presence *alongside* the effective namespace would be a second encoding
    of namespace-absence, D3) — accepted with the note that a strict-literal
    clause 1.2 reading would differ, which `db463ec` now records in the code;
    and leaving the pre-existing unresolvable-`<include>` hazard alone as
    scope discipline, with the prose distinction filed rather than argued.
- Surprises: the warden's pre-flight found that the plan's premises encoded
  *illegal states* rather than merely being incomplete — `assembly.tns`, the
  location-only dedup key, and `newProducer(doc, a.tns, …)` were each a fact
  that would silently become false at the first import, and all three were
  covered by comments asserting the opposite. Pre-flight paid for itself here:
  every one of the three would have been a green-tests-and-wrong outcome.
  Second surprise, from the arbiter: `schZ015`'s
  `<import namespace="ns" schemaLocation=""/>` correctly resolves to the
  importing document itself per RFC 3986, so clause 3.1 fires on `own="" ≠ "ns"`
  — right answer by the right route, on a case whose suite validity is
  `indeterminate` (hence #277).
- Friction: none material. One warden `revise` round pre-flight, one arbiter
  round, no repair round. The arbiter's four "required before landing" items
  were all comment/doc/tracking and needed no re-review, discharged across
  `db463ec` (items 1-2), this entry (item 3), and the cartographer pass
  (item 4).
- Next: three tracking issues filed by the cartographer, discharging the
  arbiter's item 4 — framed there as three faces of one hazard (#276 "do not
  fabricate a rejection", #277 "do not reward an accidental one", #275 the
  under-rejection mirror that can only cost wins):
  - **#275** (`ready`, `kind/bug`, `area/parser`, M4) — `src-import` clause 3
    is skipped whenever `fetch` dedups, root-caused deeper than the verdict
    described: `fetch` returns the SAME `(nil, nil)` for two different facts
    — `ErrNotFound` (no D2, clause 3 correctly n/a) and a `docKey` dedup hit
    (D2 does exist) — and `importDocument`'s `if d2 == nil { return nil }`
    short-circuits ahead of `checkImportedNamespace`. Reachable, with a
    verified chameleon-include repro. Under-rejection only. Acceptance also
    requires the structurally identical `src-include` clause 2 skip to be
    fixed by the same mechanism or explicitly dismissed in a comment.
  - **#276** (`ready`, `kind/gap`, `area/conformance`) — closure-scan
    asymmetry: an unresolvable `<xs:include>` is ADMITTED
    (`schema_closure.go:171-173`) while #182 made `<xs:import>` DECLINE the
    analogous case (`:238-241`). Acceptance forces either symmetric declining
    (which may surrender recorded passes — a downward flip to be declared to
    the arbiter up front and re-derived by a ratchet run, never hand-edited)
    or prose that answers the arbiter's counterexample. Cross-referenced #257
    and #272.
  - **#277** (`ready`, `kind/gap`, `area/conformance`) — `resolveExpected`
    maps W3C suite `validity="indeterminate"` to *expects-invalid* via the
    bare `Validity == "valid"` test at `runner.go:305,312,315`; the word
    `indeterminate` appears nowhere in `conformance/` or `docs/`, so the
    policy exists only in that comparison. 16 declarations in the pinned
    submodule, all `msMeta`; **2 are live passes today** (`elemZ031`,
    `schZ015`), leaving 14 winnable by any rejection for any reason.

## 2026-07-27 — #263 — xsd: `cos-content-act-restrict` decided (product/subset walk) and wired in as `derivation-ok-restriction` clause 2.4.2

- Shipped: the `return true` stub at `xsd/complexderivation.go`'s
  `contentTypeRestricts` is gone; Content type restricts (Complex Content)
  (Structures §3.4.6.4) is now decided statically, and `derivation-ok-restriction`
  clause 2.4.2 is a real conjunct. Three commits on `wip/issue-263`:
  `1091309` (the change, against `aea7be2`), `03b2894` (the repair round —
  comments and tests only, code-only diff byte-IDENTICAL, verified by the
  arbiter), and `2489959` (the ratcheted `schema.txt`). New files:
  `xsd/contentrestricts.go` (524 lines — the product/subset-construction walk
  deciding both clause 1's language containment and clause 2's
  `ctr-child-type-subsumption` in ONE pass) and `xsd/namespaceconstraint_subset.go`
  (122 lines — `cos-ns-subset`, §3.10.6.2, all four variety cases plus the
  conjunctive `{disallowed names}` tail, unexported pending #52).
  `xsd/defaultbinding.go` gained `loc-testSubP` clause 4 (all six Element
  Declaration sub-clauses, in spec order) and `keywordSubsumes`, the ONE
  encoding of the `skip ⊒ lax ⊒ strict` bucket lattice, which both the attribute
  half (`checkKeywordSubsumes`, clause 3/c-ran) and the element half
  (`bindingSubsumes`, clause 2.4.2) now reach instead of each deriving it
  (STYLE T4). **Zero new exported identifiers**, verified by a byte-diff of
  `go doc -all ./xsd` in BOTH arbiter rounds. **Zero `tools/rulecat` changes**,
  as the issue's post-land reconciliation comment required — the clause anchors
  (`ctr-child-type-subsumption`, `key-dft-binding`, `loc-testSubP`) carry no
  `**Schema Component Constraint:**` label, so they are cited in message text
  only and never widen `xsderr.IsValidRule`'s closed set. This was the warden's
  ruling on #262's trial, applied here without re-litigation.
- The architecturally load-bearing decision, and the one the repair round exists
  to write down correctly: **B's position automaton must be walked as a SET of
  positions (subset construction), not as a single position.** `cos-nonambig`
  (Phase C, already run) rejects competing DISTINCT particles, but ·compete·
  relates *particles*, so the unfolded COPIES of one particle are exempt by
  particle identity and several are routinely live at once — and, since 1.1
  stopped forbidding the pairing (Appendix G.1.3), an ·element particle· may be
  live beside a ·wildcard particle· that admits the same name. A matched set is
  therefore HETEROGENEOUS in general: different `{term}`s, and genuinely
  different, possibly disagreeing ·default bindings·. Clause 1 continues into
  the UNION of every member's ·follow· set; clause 2 is decided EXISTENTIALLY
  (`someBindingSubsumes` — passes when SOME member subsumes), which is a
  documented fail-open, not a claim the members agree. Collapsing the set to
  `matched[:1]` drops all 35 gained cases, so this is not a stylistic point.
  R is walked position-by-position, which is sound because any copy R can take
  is a sequence R admits. The automata are `particleattribution.go`'s, unchanged
  and unforked, on the same `unfoldCopies` bound `cos-nonambig` was decided over
  — a second, differently-bounded unfolding would explore a state space nothing
  had validated (STYLE T4).
- Spec: `cos-content-act-restrict` clauses 1 and 2 (§3.4.6.4),
  `ctr-child-type-subsumption`, `key-dft-binding` cases 1 and 4/5/6 (the element
  half; cases 2/3 stay attribute-side), `loc-testSubP` clauses 1-3 (the keyword
  lattice) and 4.1-4.6 (`c-vs-ct`/`key-val-sub-type-restricts` at 4.5,
  `c-tt-equiv`/`key-equiv-tt` at 4.6), `cos-ns-subset` (§3.10.6.2),
  `derivation-ok-restriction` clause 2.4.2 (§3.4.6.3), `cos-all-limited`
  (§3.8.6.2, which licenses `usesAllCompositor`'s tree walk to agree with the
  spec's `T.{content type}.{particle}.{term}.{compositor} = all`).
  **`cos-ns-subset` is NOT cited by `cos-content-act-restrict`** — clause 1 names
  no algorithm at all — it is this reduction's own per-transition
  wildcard-vs-wildcard compatibility test, and the file says so explicitly so a
  later reader does not mistake it for a spec cross-reference.
- Ratchet (the arbiter's figures, not recomputed here): **schema lane
  3026 → 3061 passing, +35**; 12406 → 12371 failing; **zero regressions on any
  lane**; datatypes unchanged 1043/31; instance unchanged 0/26426; ber/json/xpath
  empty. All 35 flips are `fail` → `pass`, line count unchanged (no new, no
  vanished), and the arbiter measured them against a scratch copy in round 1
  before the ratchet was written. Categories: **MS-Particles2006-07-15 ×29**
  (particlesHa021/052/081/082/083/101/103/121/123/140/143/144/162/163/164/166/
  168/169/183/184, particlesIc002/Ic004, particlesId003/Id006/Id012,
  particlesIe006/Ie009/Ie012/Ie014), **CType ×2** (final00101m4, final00101m5),
  **MS-ModelGroups2006-07-15 ×2** (mgE006, mgE010), **All ×1** (all215),
  **MS-Wildcards2006-07-15 ×1** (wildZ009) — i.e. exactly the
  particle/model-group/complex-type restriction shapes this constraint decides.
- Two spec allowances are in play and they are DIFFERENT; the file keeps them
  apart and so should any later reader. (i) The narrow `{compositor} = all`
  leniency — "if the type definition being checked has
  `T.{content type}.{particle}.{term}.{compositor} = all` and an implementation
  is unable to determine … the implementation may provisionally accept" — is
  what licenses skipping an ·all· group on the RESTRICTION side, and its
  precondition genuinely holds here because `addAll` models `all(P1…Pn)` as
  `(P1|…|Pn)*`, a strict SUPERSET of the interleave; being spec-licensed rather
  than a deliberate incompleteness, that arm carries **no** `GAP` marker. B needs
  no such leniency — the same over-approximation makes B look larger, which can
  only accept. (ii) The broader implementation-defined (a)/(b)/(c) detection
  clause is what the *pre-#263 stub* relied on and is now invoked only for the
  product-state ceiling. Conflating the two was the trap the issue's
  reconciliation comment flagged in advance.
- **Five `GAP(xsd)` markers in `contentrestricts.go`, every one fail-open by
  construction** (the whole file's "DIRECTION OF EVERY APPROXIMATION" note: each
  undecidable fact resolves so R looks smaller or B looks larger, because 2.4.2
  is one conjunct of clause 2's DISJUNCTION, so a missed rejection costs a
  suite case while a spurious one false-rejects a valid schema):
  (a) Open Content on either side is not unfolded into the automata, so a content
  type carrying one is provisionally accepted (names #265; no producer emits
  `{open content}` yet, so the arm guards programmatic construction only);
  (b) the `maxProductStates = 4096` ceiling — a WORK ceiling that abandons the
  whole walk and provisionally accepts, tested *before* enqueueing so it never
  truncates a completing walk into a false answer (**names no issue**);
  (c) an R-wildcard covered only by the UNION of ≥2 live B-wildcards is assumed
  covered rather than decided — a single live wildcard is still left to
  `cos-ns-subset`, where the relation is exact, so the common
  narrow-the-wildcard rejection is unaffected; closing it needs `cos-aw-union`
  (§3.10.6.3), whose own definition is partial (points at #52/#265);
  (d) two GLOBAL element declarations with different expanded names are admitted
  unconditionally, because `parser/produce.go` still passes a nil
  `{substitution group affiliations}` to every `NewElementDeclaration`, so
  charging the absence would false-reject W3C-valid cases (elemZ027_a/_b/_e/_f,
  particlesZ008/Z028); a LOCAL declaration on either side still needs the names
  to agree, per `e-props-correct` clause 3 (**names no issue**; the producer gap
  is out of scope here);
  (e) `key-dft-binding` cases 4/5 carry the qualifier "and it does not have a
  ·governing element declaration·", an assessment-episode fact, so a wildcard
  position always reports its keyword (**names no issue**; it is the element-side
  twin of `attributeDefaultBinding`'s case-3 marker).
  `defaultbinding.go` also gained one: clause 4.2 compares VALUES while
  `ValueConstraint` carries only `{lexical form}`, so two fixed constraints with
  different lexical forms are accepted — the same fail-open
  `checkAttributeValueConstraintSubsumes` already takes for clause 5.2.2.
  **Three of the five sites above name no open issue** ((b), (d), (e)); flagged
  here for the post-land cartographer pass to harvest — deliberately NOT filed
  from this entry.
- **One arbiter REJECT, then ACCEPT.** Round 1 (`1091309`) charged two findings,
  both comment-and-test only, no algorithm change requested: (1) [P2] the file's
  central design invariant was FALSE in two places — the header note and
  `matchPositions`'s doc both claimed every member of a matched set carries the
  same `{term}` and so the same binding, and the worst sentence went on to say a
  binding could be read "off any of them". It cannot; the arbiter demonstrated it
  empirically (B = `sequence(<element name="e" minOccurs="0"/>, <any
  processContents="skip" minOccurs="0"/>)` against R = `sequence(<element
  name="e" nillable="true"/>)`, where the element binding FAILS `loc-testSubP`
  4.1 and only the skip binding subsumes) and noted `coveringWildcardUnion`
  returns distinct wildcard positions by construction. The code was right; the
  rationale was wrong, and in this repo the rationale is the cross-session
  record. (2) three load-bearing paths had no test that could fail under
  mutation — the subset construction, the existential clause-2 check, and the
  global/global substitution-group arm all survived their mutations with
  `go test ./xsd` green, defended only by 15k opaque suite cases.
- The repair (`03b2894`) rewrote both anchors to state what actually holds and
  added three tests, each manually verified by mutate-and-revert:
  `TestContentRestrictsSubsetConstruction` (dies under `b: matched[:1]`),
  `TestContentRestrictsMatchedSetMixedTerms` (dies under
  `range matched[:1]`), `TestContentRestrictsGlobalSubstitutionGap` (dies when
  the global/global arm returns false). The arbiter re-ran all three mutations
  ITSELF rather than reading the tests, then additionally mutated
  `elementParticleAdmits` to admit unconditionally and confirmed the controls die
  in the widening direction too — the tests are two-sided, not decorative. It
  also swept for residue and confirmed the one surviving "same `{term}`" phrase
  (in `someBindingSubsumes`) is correctly scoped to copies of one particle.
- Surprises: the round-1 finding is the second time in three sessions that the
  arbiter's charge was against a COMMENT rather than code (cf. #182, where the
  warden found `assembly.tns`'s comment asserting the opposite of what would be
  true after the first import). The pattern is the same both times — a design
  note written before the general case was understood, left standing after the
  code outgrew it. Also notable: the existential clause-2 acceptance was
  explicitly endorsed rather than merely tolerated. It accepts a derivation full
  ·attribution· would arguably reject (an Element Declaration should win
  attribution over a co-live wildcard), and the arbiter's ruling was that
  charging the declaration's binding instead would reject on a run B may never
  take — a false reject, the one direction this file may not take — so
  provisional-accept with the divergence named and PINNED IN A TEST is better
  than leaving it unpinned.
- Friction: one repair round, entirely comment-and-test; the +35 movement was
  unchanged by it. Two arbiter notes not charged as findings, recorded here so
  they are not lost: (1) `unfoldCopies`'s 2/2 copy cap is justified in
  `particleattribution.go` as verdict-preserving **for `cos-nonambig`**, an
  argument about competition sets as identifier sets that does NOT transfer to
  language containment, where truncation changes both L(R) and L(B) — both sides
  truncate under the same caps and the direction worked out fail-open for every
  shape the arbiter could construct, with no regressions, but it is an unmarked
  approximation the file's own approximation-direction section does not mention;
  (2) `contentTypeRestricts` discards `addParticle`'s error into `return true`
  rather than wrapping it (STYLE S3/E1), accepted as a documented judgment call
  because the site returns `bool` and the unreachability argument is written out.
- Next:
  - **P3, for the post-land cartographer pass:** `someBindingSubsumes`'s
    existential accept is a deliberate incompleteness with **no `GAP(` token** on
    it, unlike the other five fail-opens in the same file. The prose is thorough
    and honest, but P3 is explicit that prose does not count —
    `grep -rn "GAP("` is the debt sweep and this site is invisible to it. The
    arbiter named this its OWN round-1 miss (present at `1091309`, not raised
    until round 2) and explicitly declined to convert it into a second rejection;
    it wants a follow-up issue, not a park. One-line fix.
  - Three unowned `GAP(xsd)` sites listed above ((b) the product-state ceiling,
    (d) the global-substitution-group arm, (e) the `key-dft-binding` cases 4/5
    qualifier) plus the `unfoldCopies`-cap note need tracking issues. Not filed
    from this entry by design.
  - This closes the last unblocked leaf of the retired #181 sequence-and-choice
    content-model restriction validity. The epic thread is #262 (landed,
    `f02e934`) → #263 (this) → **#264** (`cos-ct-extends`), the remaining
    sibling, still blocked on #228.
  - Landing note carried out of the verdict: the squash-merge body must NOT
    reuse `1091309`'s commit text, which still contains the false sentence
    "Every member of a matched set carries the same {term}, so clause 2 is
    unaffected by the widening" — copying it into the PR body would re-enshrine
    the invariant at the one place future readers look.
